# Poké TCG

Monorepo for a single-player Pokémon TCG game: battle through the gym leaders of each Pokémon generation against CPU opponents. React SPA + Hono/tRPC API on Vercel, Supabase (Postgres, Auth), Prisma ORM.

**Current stage:** the game loop. The home screen is the current run (see [Runs](#runs)): encounters and battles chosen on a map, leading up to a gym leader, then the shop and the next run, played with a deck built on the Collection tab from your unlocked cards. The CPU's moves play out one at a time (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)). Battles outside runs are on the Single battle tab (see [Single battles](#single-battles)). Multiple saves per account and later generations come later.

```

Both need Pillow
                        nds_render.py: renders a Nintendo DS sequence (a sound effect) to a WAV file from its MIDI file, its bank and wave archives, as the Nitro SDK's sequencer plays it. platinum_extras.py: makes Poffin cooking's pictures, sounds and data from a checkout of the pret/pokeplatinum decompilation (see Extras): `python tools/extras/platinum_extras.py <pokeplatinum checkout>` writes public/extras/poffin/, Platinum's berries' icons, trees and blender pictures in public/extras/, and the campaign's berry-data-gen4.ts (run it after emerald_extras.py, whose berry list it reads)
                        emerald_roulette.py and emerald_slots.py: make the Game Corner's roulette's and slot machines' pictures, data and sounds from a checkout of the pret/pokeemerald decompilation (see Extras): `python tools/extras/emerald_roulette.py <pokeemerald checkout>` writes public/extras/roulette/, `emerald_slots.py` public/extras/slots/ (with the tiles' red 7, seven.png). game_corner_map.py: makes the Game Corner's room, Mauville's (game-corner.png) and the campaign's game-corner-map.ts (its walkable tiles, machines, tables, people and their words, the counter's texts); its people's sprites are emerald_sprites.py's (girl_2 and cook added for it). m4a_render.py also plays key-split instruments, drum kits and the Game Boy's wave channel (the fanfares' voices)
                        sdat.py: reads a Nintendo DS sound archive (SDAT: HeartGold/SoulSilver's, which pokeheartgold keeps whole) for nds_render.py: a sequence (SSEQ) played into its events, its bank (SBNK) and the bank's waves (SWAR). hgss_voltorb_flip.py: makes Voltorb Flip's pictures, sprites, sounds and text from the pret/pokeheartgold decompilation, downloaded into a cache (see Extras): `python tools/extras/hgss_voltorb_flip.py <cache>` writes public/extras/voltorb-flip/ and voltorbFlipText.ts
CLAUDE.md               Project notes and decisions for Claude Code (read automatically each session)
```

## Web app structure

Pages load data, set their own width and side padding, and hand data to components. Components render. Hooks hold interaction state and behaviour. Utils are plain functions with no React, so the play rules for the UI can be read (and tested) on their own. Every move is still validated by the server.

### `src/pages/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `Play.tsx` | The Play tab (`/`): a menu of its four modes (the user's), the Badge Challenge (`/badge`, the runs), the Battle Tower (`/tower`), the Idle Adventure (`/idle`, only in local debug for now) and Training (`/battle`), a tile each filled with a scene from the games (the user's: `public/play/`, Cerulean Gym's entrance with its Gym Guide, HGSS's Battle Tower lobby, Route 1 with Red setting off, the Saffron City Dojo), its text over a dark fade, with how it stands (a saved challenge, the highest floor beaten, an idle run's area and badges); a mode under way keeps the tab on it (`usePlayRedirect`: a Battle Tower challenge under way and not saved from anywhere in Play, a run under way from the menu; never an idle run, which plays on its own) |
| `Idle.tsx` | The Idle Adventure (`/idle`, see [Idle Adventure](#idle-adventure)): with no run under way, Professor Oak's lab and the starters (`StarterScene`; from the second run, the Pokédex's caught species too); under way, the run playing itself (`useIdlePlayback`): the area walked (`AreaScene`), a battle (`BattleScreen`), or a town's choice (`TownMenu`, its `Store` under it); under the scene the Party, Bag, Pokédex, Settings and Log tabs and the badges; Give up (its ₱ lost: only beating the Champion banks them); the tick meter over the scene, only in a battle (`TickMeter`: narrow, the battle's turn and a bar filling over its 10 s; its row kept unseen otherwise so the scene doesn't jump; the user's); Oak's question, the town's choice, the notices and the catch-up's pages (what happened while away, `catchUpPages`; Escape closes them) in the scene's text box (`DialogueBox`, the Battle Tower's and the Game Corner's); auto play (see Idle Adventure's Music) FireRed's song for where the player is, the battle's theme, Oak's lab's with no run under way, the Hall of Fame once won (`idleMusic`); debug builds' warp to any area |
| `Tower.tsx` | The Battle Tower (`/tower`, see [Battle Tower](#battle-tower)): its rooms as scenes (`TowerScene`, the scripts in `components/tower/towerScripts.ts`), started once the overview is fetched: a battle under way opens its game, a result not yet shown its scene (remembered, `poke-tcg.tower-seen`), a challenge between battles its battle room, otherwise the lobby; the deck picker under the scene while the receptionist waits (`DeckMenu`), a saved challenge's deck shown at the desk, the challenge under way's floor, wins and BP under the map with the VS banner (`VsBanner`, the VS splash screens' look drawn in the page: double rules, a blue and red-orange band with a zigzag seam, streaks, the orange VS; on a phone one compact row, smaller cards with their text under them and a smaller VS, so it fits under the map without scrolling), name | cards | VS | cards | name: each side's headline cards by the VS (`DeckCards`), the trainer's name (the account's, the opponent's) and deck line outside them, the opponent's once they're in, their deck named from a themed pool by its main type (`TOWER_DECK_NAMES`, `mainType`, `towerOpponentDeck` in tower.ts: "Tidal Wave Deck", never the trainer's name; the user's) (`tower.overview`'s `opponentDeck` and `opponentDeckName`, the battle's own deck made again from its seed; "Next challenger" till then; the user's), and no Return to Home button (removed, the user's: the receptionist's Leave Battle Tower leaves); auto play the generation's lobby or rooms music (`towerMusic`) |
| `Home.tsx` | The Badge Challenge (`/badge`, from the Play tab's menu), the run hub, laid out like the game: within the same side margins, filling the window; on a big screen the run's numbers and its log are in the site menu (`NavRail`). The run's name and step, then the map and the encounter (or a prompt to choose one on the map) with the cards unlocked this run below it; after a run, `RunOver`. On a phone the side panel's tabs (Run, Run log, Options): during a run with a route map, under the deck choice (or under the encounter under way; `PanelTabs`), 24rem tall, collapsible to its tab row (PanelTabs' `collapsible`: the chevron at the row's end, or picking a tab, opens it again; remembered with the panel's preferences, `poke-tcg.game-log`); and, during a run, the run's numbers and its log in the site's ☰ panel under the pages, as the big screen's menu has them (`PhoneNavRun`; agreed 2026-10-23: the round button that floated in the top right corner is gone) |
| `SingleBattle.tsx` | Training (`/battle`, from the Play tab's menu; Return to Home under its opponent settings), single battles: the deck for the next battle shown large, with Edit this deck and New deck links to the Collection tab, your decks to pick from (with their headline Pokémon and card split) and the test deck; a random trainer or a boss to face; the CPU's difficulty; and the Battle button, which plays the battle transition (`useBattleTransition`) and opens the game as it ends. The choices are remembered in the browser |
| `Inventory.tsx` | Inventory (`/inventory`): the equipment (`EquipmentPanel`), and the other items by category with their pictures, every section's tiles one size (as many columns of at least 15rem as fit, one height, descriptions cut to 2 lines with the whole on hover; the badge cases too) (see [Inventory](#inventory)) |
| `Settings.tsx` | Settings (`/settings`, under Extras in the site menu): the account name (`AccountNameField`), the battle options also in the board's side panel (skip coin flip animations, `OptionsPanel`), End turn's and attacks' reminders (each on or off: `EndTurnReminders`), the music player's auto play and its soundtrack, the font (`FontPicker`: Pokémon R/B/Y, the pixel font, Pokémon R/S/E (each pixel font's author and licence in its tooltip), Pokémon S/M (Noto Sans JP, a free SIL Open Font License stand-in for Sun & Moon's Rodin, which is Fontworks' commercial font, self-hosted and trimmed to the English characters, at Standard's sizes and weights; `noto`, the default), or Standard, the system font, each named in itself; `useFontStyle`), and last the colour theme (`ThemePicker`, see [Colour themes](#colour-themes)) |
| `Cosmetics.tsx` | Cosmetics (`/cosmetics`): the card backs and playmats (`CosmeticsPanel`; see [Cosmetics](#cosmetics)) |
| `Extras.tsx` | Extras (`/extras`, after Community Decks in the site menu): a tile per minigame (`EXTRAS`), and their pages, `/extras/berry-farm` (`BerryFarm`), `/extras/berry-blender` (`BerryBlender`) and `/extras/poffin-cooking` (`PoffinCooking`), `/extras/game-corner` (`GameCorner`) and `/extras/voltorb-flip` (`VoltorbFlip`), each with a link back (see [Extras](#extras)) |
| `Packs.tsx` | Booster packs (`/packs`): the bank, and a pack for each set with packs; clicking one opens it in `PackOpening`. On a phone, a grid of smaller packs 4 wide |
| `Game.tsx` | Loads the game view, sends moves to the API (one at a time) and renders `<Board>`. For a run's battle, the side panel gets the Run tab. There's no line of the opponent's name, battle rules or items at the top (removed, the user's); once the battle is over, `BattleResult` (the Board's overlay, centred on the play area) shows the result (an action that ends the battle skips to its last move, but that move's coin flips still play first, as any others: the flip that decided it, then the result; the user's) and a Back to the run button (a single battle: Next battle, back to the Training tab) |
| `Decks.tsx` | Deck builder (`/decks`): the deck list first (`DeckList`); on a big screen the decks pane's width is dragged by the handle between it and the card search (`PaneHandle`, from 13rem, just the tiles' three headline Pokémon (their text hidden by `DeckTile`'s container query under 15rem, the toolbar's Decks label under 14rem, the name a tooltip), to half the window or 720px, a double-click back to 24rem; remembered, `poke-tcg.deck-pane`); an open deck on the left as a button back to the list (`CurrentDeck`, the same tile as the deck list's, `DeckTile`, its name and "← All decks" across the top (`titleAbove`), the cards and ratio under them, and where the list shows the Pokémon's names its card back and coin (the coin face on, `SpinningCoin`'s `flat`), its own or the account's defaults, `CosmeticsPreview`; renamed with its pencil button), save and delete, and its cards (`DeckPanel`); the card search (`DeckFilters`, `CardGrid`) on the right, where clicking a card adds a copy. On a phone, the decks and the search are side panels (see Phone layout). Cards you haven't unlocked are darker (`useUnlocked`); they can be added, with a warning that the deck can't be played until they're unlocked. Share and Import (see [Deck sharing](#deck-sharing)) |
| `Community.tsx` | Community Decks (`/community`, between Collection and Extras): the decks players have published, the latest first, with a search; each a tile of the same size (its description cut short with an ellipsis) with its author, date, description, `DeckSummary` and how many of its cards you haven't unlocked. Clicking a tile focuses it (agreed): it grows a little, shows its whole description and its buttons along the bottom (hidden otherwise): its card list (Cards), Import (saved as your deck and opened in the deck builder), Copy code, and Unpublish on your own; your account name (`AccountNameField`) above the decks (see [Deck sharing](#deck-sharing)) |

### `src/components/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `Board.tsx` | The game table. Gets card play from `useCardPlay`, holds the hover preview, and lays out the components below (on a phone, `phone/PhoneBoard.tsx` lays them out instead). A move being set up (Trainer, attack or power questions, a held card, retreating, a Pokémon's menu) is cancelled by clicking anywhere except a Pokémon slot, a hand card or the move's own panel (elements marked `data-keep-action`). Clicking your Active Pokémon (a tap on a phone) opens a panel centred on the card (`cardMenu`, `cardPanel`) with its attacks, power, Retreat and other moves (turning a face-down card over, discarding Shapeshift or Clefairy Doll), and clicking a benched Pokémon with a power opens one on it with that power; an attack's or power's questions are asked in it (hidden while one of your own Pokémon is to be clicked, which is then a prompt). It closes with a click elsewhere, Esc, or once the game moves on. The card Peek showed hangs off the Active's right |
| `StatusBar.tsx` | A large End turn button, centred in the middle of the table between the two sides; no line of whose turn it is above it (the user's: the turn banner says so), and while there's no End turn a message in its place (a promotion, the result; no "CPU's turn" either). The button row keeps its height (it stays when there's no button), so the button never moves. End turn goes the moment it's pressed and comes back on your next turn once its banner has gone (Board's `endedOn`; the user's). During setup Start battle takes its place, with Reset cards hanging off its left (on a phone Start battle is centred on the screen with Reset cards on its right, and the setup line is on a banner above the bar: `SetupBanner`, agreed). End turn, and an attack (which ends the turn too; agreed), first list what you could still do (`utils/reminders.ts`, `useTurnReminders`): attach an Energy card from your hand, use the Stadium (Lucky Stadium), use a Pokémon Power that works now. Keep playing, or End turn (Attack); each reminder is turned on or off on the Settings page only (agreed), remembered in the browser (`poke-tcg.skip-end-turn-energy-check`, `-stadium-check`, `-powers-check`) |
| `StadiumCard.tsx` | The Stadium card in play (shared by both players), face up to the left of your Active Pokémon at the bench's size (`.stadium-column` in `index.css`), with the hover preview and right-click view; an empty dashed spot when there's none. One you can use once a turn (Lucky Stadium) is used like a benched Pokémon's power: on your turn it pulses with a blue outline when it can be used (`.playable-pulse`, as playable hand cards do), and clicking it uses it at once (or a warning says why not) |
| `OptionsPanel.tsx` | The side panel's Options tab: whether the CPU's Pokémon and Energy cards are shown large when it plays them (`PlayedCard`; Trainers always are), saved by `useOptions` |
| `DebugPanel.tsx` | The Debug tab (see [Debug tools](#debug-tools)): beside the logs in the site menu on a big screen (`NavRail`), in the side panel's tabs on a phone: draw a card, put any card or an Energy into your hand, set any Pokémon's HP. Loaded lazily, and left out of production builds |
| `NavRail.tsx` | The site menu on a big screen (see [Site menu](#site-menu)): a column of the pages' icons down the left edge under the top bar, Settings under Extras; the top bar's ☰ widens it, each page's name beside its icon, taking more room on the left (remembered, `poke-tcg.nav-open`). Under the pages (a margin and a line above them): open, `NavSections`, the run's numbers (`RunStats`, during a run) and the logs (Run log, Battle log and, with the debug tools, Debug tabs) filling the rest of the menu down to its bottom; the run's section's height is set by dragging the bar under it (`Resizable`; remembered with the log tab, `poke-tcg.nav-sections`); minimised, the run's HP and ₱, the bank and Stardust (`RunSummary`) |
| `PageHeader.tsx` | A page's heading, the same on every tab but the Collection and the game board (Play, the run screen's run name and step, Inventory, Cosmetics, Packs, Community Decks, Training, Settings): the title in one size (the pixel font, Pixellocale, at 27px), an optional grey line beside it and actions on the right (the Packs tab's bank and Stardust), with the same gap below |
| `NavLog.tsx` | Passes the game page's log (and its game id and view, for the Debug tab) up to the site menu's Battle log tab: `NavLogProvider` around the layout, `useGameLogInNav` (the game page, as its moves play back) and `useNavGameLog` (NavRail) |
| `SidePanel.tsx` | A panel sliding in from the left or right edge, over the page, closed by a click beside it, Escape, or a swipe back towards its edge: the site's ☰ menu on a phone, and the Collection tab's decks and search on a phone |
| `ThemePicker.tsx` | The Settings page's colour themes (see [Colour themes](#colour-themes)): a collapsible list per group, System themes (Slate and Start Menu, the user's) then each generation's, an arrow beside each heading (the one holding the theme in use open at first; a closed one names the theme in use in it), 5 compact tiles a row (3 on a phone), each a small picture of the app in its colours (the page, a panel, text and the accent) and its name, where its colours come from on hover; a click puts it on at once |
| `MusicPlayer.tsx` | The music player (see [Music](#music)): `MusicProvider` (the audio and its panel, mounted with the layout; `useMusicHold` pauses its song for a minigame's fanfare and plays it on) and `MusicControls` (its small bar: at the right end of the top bar on a big screen, or in the ☰ menus) |
| `ToolPops.tsx` | A Pokémon Tool working by itself (a Berry between turns: the log entry's `tool`), shown over its Pokémon (the user's): a big green "+20" (or "Cured") with the Tool's name, popping in, rising and fading (`.tool-pop`, `TOOL_POP_MS`); `Game.tsx` sets them as their move is shown (`toolPopsAfter`, `ToolPopsContext`) and waits for them before the next move; every `Slot` shows its Pokémon's |
| `ReturnToHome.tsx` | Return to Home: back to the Play tab's menu (on a finished run, a saved or ended Battle Tower challenge, Training) |
| `Toasts.tsx` | Messages in the bottom right of the page (amber warnings such as why a card can't be played, red errors for a move the server rejected) that fade away after a few seconds, newer ones stacking underneath, nearest the corner. The instruction for a held card ("Click a highlighted slot") is a prompt instead: it stays up while the card is held and disappears the moment it's played or put down |
| `OpponentArea.tsx` | The CPU's bench, active, deck and prizes, and its hand face down and half off the top edge as on a phone (`PhoneOppHand`; face up while your Omanyte's Clairvoyance works, a click spreading it out; no line of its name or hand size, the user's). When an effect asks for one of the CPU's Pokémon, the choices are highlighted and clicked (several for Gigashock, marked "Chosen"). The Trainer being played floats over this side, its bottom level with the CPU's active; the card the CPU played (`PlayedCard`) shows over its Bench and Active. While a Trainer for one of the CPU's Pokémon is dragged, those Pokémon are its drop targets (`drop`) |
| `FloatingDialog.tsx` | Holds those floating panels, with an eye button that fades them to 20% opacity with a dashed outline and lets every click through to what's underneath (only the eye button stays clickable); the deck search only fades |
| `AttackList.tsx` | Attack buttons in your active Pokémon's card panel (opened by clicking it). On your turn each shows what it would do to the Defending Pokémon now, from the view's `attackDamage` (`attackDamageRanges`: the attack played out on a copy of the game, so its own effect, such as Karate Chop's 50 less 10 per damage counter, Weakness and Resistance, PlusPower, Defender and effects in play such as Harden all count, and an attack that would be stopped shows 0): one number, or a range when coin flips or choices decide it. Only that number is shown ("Confuse Ray (80)"); the tooltip lists only what changes it from the printed damage and by how much ("Weakness (×2): +30", "PlusPower: +10", "Defender: −20"; a range where coin flips decide it), from the view's `steps`: `traceDamage` in `attacks.ts` notes each change the damage to the Defending Pokémon goes through in `hit`, the attack's own effect counted as "<attack>'s effect". Otherwise plain damage after Weakness and Resistance (listed in the tooltip), and variable damage as printed. Attacks it can't use are disabled with the reason as a tooltip |
| `AttackChoicePanel.tsx` | Asks the current question of an attack being used: energy to discard (a set number, or any number for Wildfire and Burning Fire), a defender's energy or attack, an Energy card on the opponent's Pokémon (Energy Control), a type or a Weakness (Texture Magic), cards from your discard pile (Energy Conversion, Scavenge), whose deck (Prophecy) or Pokémon (Devolution Beam), or confirming the benched Pokémon picked on the board (Gigashock). Pokémon in play, the Active included (Telekinesis, Lightning Rod), are picked on the board; a "you may switch" Bench pick (Pull In, Spiral Aura) highlights the Active too, clicked to not switch |
| `PrizeCards.tsx` | Prize cards face down (face up after Here Comes Team Rocket!) in one overlapping column of 6, the last shown whole and level with the bench (upside down on the CPU's side), left of your play area (under your Concede button) and right of the CPU's |
| `DeckAndDiscard.tsx` | A player's deck (face down) and discard pile (top card face up) with counts, in a column beside each play area (discard above deck on your right; mirrored both ways on the CPU's left), with the deck level with the bench. Clicking the discard pile (yours or the CPU's) opens it to view. |
| `DiscardPane.tsx` | A discard pile spread out in a window in the middle of the screen, styled like the card choices (`PendingChoicePanel`: large high-res cards in a scrolling grid), newest card first, with the large preview on hover; closed with ×, Esc or a click beside it |
| `CardBack.tsx` | A face-down card; `mine`, one of the player's own (their deck, Prize cards), in their card back (`useCosmetics`) |
| `CosmeticsPanel.tsx` | The Cosmetics tab's panel (see [Cosmetics](#cosmetics)): its Card backs, Playmats and Coins sections (on a phone each folds away with a − beside its title and back with a +, remembered: `poke-tcg.cosmetics-collapsed`; the card backs 3 to a row there; the card backs' grid is 3 rows tall, the rest scrolled to, `useRowsTall`); a tile per card back, playmat and coin, with Buy with banked ₱ or the run that unlocks it, and once had Assign (`DeckAssign`, `AssignDialog`: its decks and whether it's the default); a playmat's preview answers clicks, and a coin flips when clicked (`CoinPreview`) |
| `coinDesigns.tsx` | The coin cosmetics' looks (`COIN_DESIGNS`, drawn as SVG and CSS): each coin's heads and tails faces and its rim's colour |
| `Playmat.tsx` | A playmat (see [Cosmetics](#cosmetics)): its geometric pattern on a canvas filling its parent (on a big screen each side of the table; on a phone the whole battle screen's background, behind everything, `-z-10`, and at 0.6 the size, `scale`), in the colour theme's colours (`playmatColors`), answering a click on the parent's empty space (not on cards, slots or buttons) subtly: a soft wave (`wave`, `wavesAt`) that lights lines and points and nudges them a few pixels, or light running along a pattern's lines; nothing turns or flips (agreed). Dot matrix, Grid, Crosses, Pinstripes, Contours, Hexes, Constellation, Triangles; Plain draws nothing. Mats of one `group` (both sides of the table) share clicks: a click on either side carries on into the other, its wave coming in from beyond the edge |
| `TurnOrderFlip.tsx` | The coin flip for turn order when a battle starts: the game's own coin flip (`CoinFlip`: just the coin, the same size, speed and place, beside the Active Pokémon of whoever calls it). Shown once per game in the browser session (`sessionStorage`), while the game is being set up; a click closes it |
| `CoinFlip.tsx` | Each coin flip in a move's result, one after another: just the coin on the play area (`Board`'s `overlay`, inside the `PlayArea`; no panel; once it has landed, Heads! or Tails! just above it, in the room its flight used; what it's for read out to screen readers only), beside the Active Pokémon of the side it comes from (`placeBeside`, measuring the rows marked `data-coin-anchor` and the slots marked `data-active-slot`): your flips just to the right of your Active (a 36px gap; past the Peek card, `.attack-column`, when it's shown), the CPU's mirrored to the left of its Active Pokémon, each centred on its row and kept inside the play area (on the phone layout, in the space left of that side's Active Pokémon, its bottom level with the Active's: its row's `data-coin-spot`, agreed; the coin 30% smaller there, `SpinningCoin`'s `sm`). The game waits for them; a click anywhere on the table skips them, and the Settings page's "Skip coin flip animations" turns them off. `SpinningCoin` is a 3D coin in the player's coin cosmetic (`useCosmetics`, `COIN_DESIGNS`): faces on a rim of thin stacked discs, it dips as the thumb loads it, is flicked (a burst of streaks and a flash) and flips end over end through an arc, lands (a ring of dust and specks) on its face, bounces and settles, in `SPIN_MS` (1s, the turn-order flip's too), its shadow shrinking as it rises (Web Animations API; the effects' keyframes in `index.css`); with reduced motion it just shows the face |
| `GameLog.tsx` | The game log (attacks, coin flips, Special Conditions, knockouts), with room for buttons in its header (in a game, the "Skip coin flip animations" setting; in the site menu, the log tabs). It keeps the newest entry in view by scrolling only its own list |
| `PageNav.tsx` | The site's pages with their icons (`PAGES`, in `NavRail` and the menus; each icon a pixel-art sprite from `public/nav/`, `navIcon`, drawn at its own 32px), `SETTINGS_PAGE` (last in the menus, under Training) and `PageMenu`, the pages as a ☰ menu's buttons: the phone's site menu (SidePanel), Settings last and, as a row of icon-only buttons (the names their labels), the top of the phone battle screen's ☰ menu (`LogSheet`'s `pages`), which covers the header |
| `AccountSettingsSync.tsx` | Signed in (mounted in main.tsx): loads the account's settings (`settings.get`) and connects them (`utils/accountSettings.ts`), saving changes with `settings.save` (see [Account settings](#account-settings)); renders nothing |
| `LogDrawer.tsx` | The side panel's tabs: Run during a run (on a phone; a big screen has the run in the site menu), the log (Battle log, or Run log on the run screen), Options (`OptionsPanel`), and Debug with the debug tools enabled, the tab picked remembered in the browser (`poke-tcg.game-log`). `LogSheet` is the phone layout's: the tabs full screen, with Concede. `PanelTabs` is the same tabs inline, filling the box they're put in: on a phone, the run screen's panel under the deck choice. On a big screen a battle has no side panel: the logs and Debug are in the site menu (`NavRail`), the options on the Settings page |
| `PowerPanel.tsx` | The active Pokémon's Power button under its attacks (in its card panel), or the current question while a power is being used. Clicking a benched Pokémon with a power opens a panel on it (`Board`'s `cardPanel`) with the power's name and a Use button (not its text, agreed 2026-10-23), or why it can't be used now; the power's questions are asked there too. `PeekCard` shows the card you looked at with Peek until your next move. Damage Swap, once both Pokémon are picked and more than 1 counter can go, asks "Yes, all N" (every counter, when the receiver can take them all), "Yes, N" (as many as it can take without being Knocked Out) or "No, just 1" (`PowerChoices.counters`; the gym leader CPU still moves 1 at a time) |
| `TurnBanner.tsx` | The turn and setup banners (`SplitBanner`, the user's): across the play area, each half on its own band (`halfBand`, reaching 90% of the way from the Active Pokémon slot to its end of the play area: `BAND_REACH`, the user's) fading in from its outer end and out to the background at the Active Pokémon slot's edge (`data-active-slot`), so the two never meet in the middle; centred on the bar between the two sides (StatusBar's `data-banner-row`, its whole height) and behind its buttons (they sit above it, solid even when disabled, so it never shows through them), its text split either side of them: on the left "Your Turn" or "Opponent's Turn" (`TurnBanner`, when a turn begins: `useNewTurn`) or "You're going first." / "You're going second." (`SetupBanner`), on the right the turn count or "Place your Basic Pokémon." (on a phone the setup line's halves on bands of their own just above and below the button row, the play area's width, each sliding in from its side: `stacked`, the user's: beside the buttons its text was squeezed small; and its turn banner one band across the button row, "Your Turn · Turn 3" on one line left of the buttons (across the band on the CPU's turn), sliding in from the right, then fading: `single`); both halves the same size (the user's): 57% of the band's height (70%, then 10% smaller twice: the user's; the setup line's 20% smaller again, `SETUP_TEXT`), or the largest that fits both beside the buttons on one line (`placeHalves`; on a phone, when that would be under 16px, both wrap onto two lines instead), each centred on its own band (the user's), moved outwards only as far as it must to clear the buttons (`data-banner-avoid`, measured every frame while it shows; 16px from them on the left and 24px on the right, 20px on a phone). Both layouts alike (72px tall, 48px on a phone). It fades in as each half's text slides in from its own side (the left from the left edge, the right from the right), then fades away (the user's; it used to slide across): the turn's after 1.2s (the CPU's reply waits for it, `TURN_BANNER_MS`), the setup line's once Start battle is pressed |
| `TrainerPanel.tsx` | The Trainer card being played (floating over the CPU's side): its name, text and questions one at a time; answering the last one plays it (a single card or Pokémon is chosen by clicking it). Cards from your hand (Computer Search, Item Finder, Energy Retrieval, Maintenance, Pokémon Breeder, Pokémon Trader) are picked by clicking them in the hand itself (`handPick` in `useTrainer`), with a Choose button when it takes more than one. A Trainer with no questions gets a Play button. A question answered by a single click in the hand or on the board (Pokémon Breeder, Gust of Wind, Potion…) shows no panel, only a prompt toast that stays until the card is played or cancelled; a Trainer that can't be played shows a warning toast instead of the panel |
| `PendingChoicePanel.tsx` | Finishing a search from a Trainer, attack or power: take a card from your deck (Computer Search, Pokémon Trader, Energy Search, Poké Ball, Call for Family) or your discard pile (Recycle), put a card from your hand on top of your deck (Special Delivery), discard an Energy card from a Pokémon (Pure Body), pick how many cards to draw (Computer Error), evolve an Eevee from your deck or skip it (Chain Reaction), or reorder the top cards of a deck (Pokédex, Prophecy) by dragging them or with the arrow buttons. Only you see these cards. Some come up during the CPU's turn (Computer Error, Chain Reaction). Shown big in the middle of the table (not counting the game log), as wide as its cards need, with large card images |
| `RetreatPanel.tsx` | Retreat button under the attacks (the Active's card panel); while retreating, picks which energy to discard (when it matters) and prompts for a benched Pokémon |
| `Hand.tsx` | Your hand along the very bottom of the screen, centred, each card partly below the edge. At rest it keeps to the play area's width; only a hovered card's neighbours spill into the page margins (up to the window edge and the game log) when they need the room; they overlap when there are too many to fit. Hovering a card grows it, lifts it fully into view and slides its neighbours aside, squeezing them closer rather than off the screen while every card keeps at least a 2rem strip showing to hover; the selected card is lifted too. On a phone (`compact`) the cards are half hidden, and a tapped one comes up whole and enlarged (`touched`; or the selected card, as a Trainer asks for cards), its neighbours making way as for a hovered card so it never covers one (agreed 2026-10-23). Dragging a card back over the hand puts it back (`HAND_ZONE`), even where the dragged image overlaps the bench |
| `HandCard.tsx` | One draggable, clickable card in your hand; while a Trainer asks for hand cards it's highlighted if it can be picked, ringed and raised once picked, and faded if not. On your turn, with nothing in progress, a card that can be played pulses with a soft blue outline (`.playable-pulse` in `index.css`, as in TCG Pocket) |
| `PlayedCard.tsx` | The card the CPU just played or used, large on a radial shadow (over the CPU's Bench and Active on a big screen, the middle of the play area on a phone) while the move is on screen, then bursting away (see [Seeing the CPU's moves](#seeing-the-cpus-moves)) |
| `PlayArea.tsx` | Both sides of the table, filling the window's height above the hand; the cards scale to the space (`.play-area-cards`). While a Trainer card is dragged it glows green with the pointer over it, where dropping plays the card, and red with the pointer anywhere else, where dropping does nothing. Energy and Pokémon cards dragged to a slot do the same: red unless they're over a slot they can go to (then the slot glows instead) |
| `Slot.tsx` | An active or bench slot: drop target, highlight ring, tint band and "Play"/"Attach"/"Evolve"/"Promote"/"Choose" label (both across the middle of the card, clear of the Energy along the bottom). Any attack, power or Trainer that asks for a Pokémon in play is answered by clicking a highlighted slot (`pokemonPick` in `Board.tsx`), not a button. A Transformed Ditto shows the card it's a copy of, with a "Ditto" chip. On your turn, with nothing in progress, a Pokémon whose Pokémon Power can be used, and your Active Pokémon when it has an attack it can use, pulse with the playable cards' blue outline (`usable`, `.playable-pulse`) |
| `Card.tsx` | Card art with damage, attached-energy (a row of `EnergyIcon`s along the bottom), attached cards (Pokémon Tools, Trainers that stay attached, Shapeshift: a row of round icons of their art in the top left, `AttachedIcon`, each showing its card in the hover preview), Special Condition and protection overlays; opens the hover preview, and the `CardInspector` on right-click. With `shine` (the Active Pokémon's slot), a holo or galaxy card (from the catalog) is drawn as a still `HoloCard` |
| `CardImage.tsx` | A card image that shows the card back until it has loaded (its background, removed once the image is in so it never shows through the rounded corners). Used for every card image: the board, hand, previews, viewers, the deck builder, run cards; `HoloCard` does the same with its own back |
| `EnergyIcon.tsx` | One energy as a coloured disc with its type's MUI symbol (leaf, flame, drop, bolt, eye, fist, moon, gear, star; Rainbow Energy is a rainbow disc with sparkles), so types differ by shape as well as colour. Used on cards in play and in the attack, retreat and debug panels |
| `CardPreview.tsx` | The large card preview that follows the cursor and resizes with the mouse wheel (except over the deck-search lists, where the wheel scrolls the page). It goes once the pointer is no longer over the card it came from (`Preview.from`) or leaves the window, even if the card was removed or covered under the pointer and its mouseleave never came |
| `PreviewContext.ts` | Context that lets any card show or hide the hover preview |
| `PreviewProvider.tsx` | Provides the hover preview outside the board (the run hub), with the wheel resizing it or, for pages that scroll, scrolling the page |
| `EquipmentPanel.tsx` | The Inventory's Equipped section: the trainer with a slot per piece of equipment (2×3) on the right of its box, and beside that, in their own grid, the pieces not equipped, to drag onto them; without a hover, the piece tapped is described at the bottom of the screen (`ItemDetails`) (see [Inventory](#inventory)) |
| `DialogueBox.tsx` | The games' text box over a scene's bottom edge (`DialogueBox`, the Battle Tower's, the Game Corner's and the Idle Adventure's, the user's): a line typed out a letter at a time (25ms; `instant`: shown whole, for a line up only a moment), its speaker's name over it, a question's choices beside it (above it on a phone) once it's typed, each a word or a `DialogueChoice` (a picture before it, or greyed out and not to be picked), or with `inline` side by side inside the box under the line, each its picture over its words (the Idle Adventure's starters); `choices: []` a line with nothing to click; `maxWidth` bounds it, centred (the Idle Adventure's); `dialogueKey` a dialogue's key; a click on it, anywhere on its scene (`pressRef`) or Enter shows the line whole, then goes on (Enter and Space left to the page's own fields, and to its buttons reached with the keyboard); game art, so fixed colours |
| `TrimmedSprite.tsx` | A pixel-art sprite without the transparent margin round it (`TrimmedSprite`: its visible box measured in the browser once an image, from a canvas's alpha, the rest cut off; the image files left whole), pixel perfect: drawn on a canvas, unsmoothed, at a whole number of device pixels a sprite pixel (`scale` rounded to one, following the display scaling and zoom), the Idle Adventure's starters in Oak's text box |
| `BattleTransition.tsx` | The battle transitions, as the mainline games' trainer battles open (the user's: the Battle Tower's first, then every Play mode's battle): one of five at random (the double white flash, then blinds, an iris, shutters, bars or a diagonal wipe; `SceneTransition` plays one inside its parent, the Idle Adventure's scene before each battle, the user's; keyframes `tower-*` in index.css), `BattleTransitionProvider` (in the layout) and `useBattleTransition()(open)` play one over the play area (the user's: the layout's `<main>`, `data-battle-area`, below the top bar, `data-top-bar`, never over the site menu or the top bar; measured as it starts), open the battle's page as it closes and fade the black away once it's there (a run's battles, after the trainer's "!", Training's and the Battle Tower's, after the opponent's line; the user's) |
| `BadgeCases.tsx` | The key items' badge cases, one per generation of runs, each opening to its badges (see [Inventory](#inventory)) |
| `TrainerSpritePicker.tsx` | The Inventory's Change trainer window: every Showdown trainer sprite, searchable (see [Inventory](#inventory)) |
| `ItemIcon.tsx` | An item's picture from `public/items/` (pixel art drawn crisp; badges smooth) |
| `HoloCard.tsx` | A card with the holo effect (it imports the holo styles, `styles/holo/index.css`, so they load with the first holo card shown, not every page): tilts toward the pointer while shine and glare layers follow it, settling flat when the mouse leaves or a finger lifts off (not while it presses, agreed). Used by `CardViewer` (the right-click view) and by the pack opener. `still` fixes the shine and stops it tilting or reacting to the pointer: the Active Pokémon on the board, when it's a holo. `motionRef` (`HoloMotion.play`) moves it anyway along a scripted pointer path: the Active's slot animations (`Slot.tsx`: dropping in, the damage shake, the attack lunge, the heal and Energy glows) tilt and shine it with them, then it settles back to its still light |
| `DeckFilters.tsx` | Deck builder search (on a big screen every line named in a first column, Search, Kind, Type, Pokémon, Cards, so the controls line up, all one height; the number of cards shown and Clear filters at the end of the last line; on a phone Clear filters at the top, the number at the bottom): name, release sets (any number of them, ticked in `SetPicker`'s list, grouped by generation (`setGroups`: Gen 1 · Kanto, Gen 2 · Johto, then Other for any set in neither), each generation's heading ticking or unticking all its sets; its All sets ticks every set, so one or two can be unticked, and with every set ticked unticks them all), sort (set number by default; Dex Number; type), Unlocked and Locked toggles and Works and Not work toggles (whether the engine implements the card; every filter starts off, showing every card; as with the kind toggles, both of a pair off, or both on, shows every card). On a big screen: the search, sets and sort on the first line, Favorites, the kinds and Holo on the second, the types on the third (always one line: smaller, then just their icons, as the space narrows), the Pokémon filters on the fourth, and Unlocked/Locked and Works/Not work on the fifth. "Holo" shows only holo, galaxy-foil and holo Energy cards (`isHoloCard`), Favorites only the account's favorites; the kind toggles (Pokémon, Trainer, Energy), the type toggles (one per type, for Pokémon and Energy alike) and the Pokémon filters (stage, HP range, has ability). The search matches card names and attack names, and "Stadium" finds every Stadium Trainer (`cards.list`'s `stadium`: TCGdex has no Trainer type for these sets, so it's the Stadium rule in the card's text) |
| `DeckList.tsx` | The deck builder's deck list: a button per deck in its saved order (drag to reorder, dnd-kit; on a touch screen hold a deck for a quarter of a second first, so swipes scroll) laid out as agreed with the user: its 3 headline Pokémon large on the left (`FeaturedCards`) with the Pokémon/Trainer/Energy bar and its counts under them (`RatioBar`, `RatioCounts`; just coloured dots and numbers, the labels as a tooltip); on their right its name (larger, with a pencil button to rename it) with the headline Pokémon's names under it (comma separated, wrapping when they don't fit), and the star to favorite it (`decks.favoriteDeck`, `Deck.favorite`; not the automatic run deck) and its card count (green when it can be played, no "ready") at the bottom right. Phones get the same tiles (`compact` only tightens the panel's padding). A toolbar across the top has icon buttons for New deck, Copy, Import (a deck code), Publish (Copy and Publish: a prompt toast asks for a deck, its tiles outlined; clicking one copies it, or opens its Share dialog, with its code and Publish to Community Decks; Escape, the icon again or a click elsewhere cancels) and Filters, which pops out Favorites (only the favorite decks) over the generations (`GenerationFilter` with `toggles`, `icon` and `before`, as on Community Decks, all on to start, the icon marked while it filters: only the decks with cards from a generation ticked are listed), and `CurrentDeck`, the open deck's button back to the list. A deck holding locked cards is greyed out with a Locked tag (`LOCKED_TAG`, also on the run's, the route's and Training's deck choices). The tiles (`DeckTile`, `DeckSummary`, `CompactDeckTile`) read the catalog from `CatalogContext` and the unlocks from `useUnlocked` themselves |
| `DeckShare.tsx` | Deck sharing's dialogs: `ShareDeckDialog` (a saved deck's share code with Copy, and Publish to Community Decks with a description, under the account name) and `ImportDeckDialog` (paste a code or a text deck list to save its deck as a new one); `CopyButton` |
| `AccountNameField.tsx` | `AccountNameField`: the account name (or "Choose a name") with Change, on the Settings page (the top bar's account drop-down was removed, agreed 2026-10-23) (also in the Share dialog and on Community Decks; see [Deck sharing](#deck-sharing)) |
| `TopBarStats.tsx` | The big screen's top bar's bank, EXP and Stardust (`TopBarAccount`, each with its sprite from `public/nav/`; the bank and Stardust over 999 in short, `shortNumber`, the exact number alone on hover), and the phone's top bar's figures (agreed): during a run its HP (`HpBar`, small) and ₱, then the bank and Stardust, each an icon and its number; the bank and Stardust over 999 in short (`shortNumber`: 1.2k, 12k, 1.2M), a tap on one showing its exact number briefly in a small bubble below it that fades away (`ShortStat`, `exact-fade` in `index.css`) |
| `DeckPanel.tsx` | The deck being built: its cards by section (holos tagged Holo, since they share their non-holo version's name) with − / + buttons (+ disabled with the reason when a rule forbids another copy), the count out of 60, what keeps it from being played, and "(partial)" on cards that aren't fully implemented. Cards not unlocked are faded, marked (locked) |
| `CardGrid.tsx` | Scrollable grid of low-res card images; the number of columns follows the width. Rows overlap so only each card's top strip (its name, set by the `gap-y-6` row gap) shows; hovering a strip brings that card to the front, clicking selects it. In the deck builder a card in the deck has its count in the top right and a − in the top left that takes one copy out per press (`onRemove`). With `isLocked`, cards not unlocked yet are darker with a lock. When the rows would leave space below, they spread out to fill the height, up to whole cards with no overlap. With `holdToInspect` (the deck builder, where a click adds a copy), pressing and holding a card shows it large in the middle of the screen, with its details, until the press ends, and that press adds nothing. In the deck builder, right-clicking a card opens it in `CardInspector`. Only the rows in view are rendered (`useGridWindow`, `gridWindow`: those whose cards reach the viewport, the fanned rows above included, and 2 below), in a box as tall as every row; the grid and its cells (`GridCell`) are memoised, so its handlers must stay the same between renders (see Performance) |
| `CardInspector.tsx` | The right-click view (closed by its ×, Escape, or a click anywhere but the card and its controls), everywhere in the app: a card large in the middle of the screen, as holding it shows, but staying open until Escape or a click outside. With a deck open, − and + change its copies in the deck (+ disabled with the reason when it can't be added). Its ★ button adds the card to the account's favorites or takes it out (see [Favorites](#favorites)); above it, a holo shows how many copies are unlocked (a special holo's or holo Energy's count, 4 of a holo-only card) |
| `InspectContext.ts` | `InspectContext`, `CatalogContext` (the card catalog by id) and `useInspectOnRightClick`: right-click props for a card (a catalog card, or an id looked up in the catalog) that open the `CardInspector`; with `clearsPreview` they hide the hover preview first (`Card`, `RunCard`) |
| `CardInspectProvider.tsx` | Around every page once signed in: loads the card catalog once per session (`CatalogContext`) and opens a right-clicked card in the `CardInspector` (loaded the first time a card is opened), with all its effects and no deck buttons. The deck builder provides its own `InspectContext`, with − and + for the open deck |
| `CardViewer.tsx` | A card's high-res image for the deck builder's hold and right-click views (low-res shown while it loads), sized to the window, with set, rarity, stage, type, HP and max attack. A locked card is darkened and shown still (no `HoloCard`), marked Locked |

### `src/components/phone/`

The battle screen's phone layout (see [Phone layout](#phone-layout)); `Board` keeps all the logic and lays these out instead of its usual table.

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `PhoneBoard.tsx` | The phone layout of the board: `Board` keeps all the logic and passes its pieces (the slots, status bar, hand, dialogs, drag overlay) here, and this lays them out with `PhoneTable`, filling the screen over the site's header; it owns the round ☰ button's full-screen menu (`LogSheet`, with Concede) and the CPU's hand spread out on a tap while it's face up (Clairvoyance) |
| `PhoneTable.tsx` | The board in one upright column, TCG Pocket style: the CPU's hand in a strip at the top (kept for it, so it never covers the Bench: `--opp-hand-strip`), the CPU's Bench, its Active row (both Actives the same size, agreed), the bar between the sides (☰ menu, Stadium, status, End turn), your Active row and your Bench; each Active row has its piles on the right (`PhonePiles`). Sized by `.phone-cards` (index.css) |
| `PhonePiles.tsx` | `PhonePrizes` (one small stack with its count; face-up prizes open spread out), `PhonePiles` (the Prize cards above the deck, and the discard pile, half as big again, to their left, centred on them; each with its count; a tap on the discard spreads it out in `DiscardPane`) and `PhoneOppHand` (the CPU's hand in the strip at the top of the screen above its Bench, and on a big screen along the top of the CPU's side (OpponentArea), face down and upside down, half off the edge; a tap on it shows its card count briefly in a bubble under it; face up with Clairvoyance, a tap also spreading it out) |

### `src/components/run/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `RunHeader.tsx` | The run's name and how far along it is |
| `StatusBox.tsx` | The run's HP and EXP as Black 2's player box (the user's), at the top of the run's numbers: the box from the sheet (`b2-box.png`, its bars emptied), the HP bar's fill (`hpBarFill`; no HP numbers, the user's), and the EXP bar (its fill the game's stripes, and the box's "EXP" letters through two masks, `b2-exp-dark.png` and `b2-exp-light.png`, in the colour theme's accent, `--color-yellow-300`, or with the Settings page's Themed EXP bar off the game's blue: `useExpTheme`, the user's): 50 EXP fills it (`EXP_PER_BAR`, the user's: a battle's 10 a fifth, an Extra EXP battle's 20 two fifths); a gain past the end fills it, starts it again empty and goes on with the rest, once for each time it fills (`expBarFill`, tested in `StatusBox.test.ts`); drawn at 2×, never smoothed |
| `HpBar.tsx` | The run's HP as a Pokémon health bar (the user's, in place of hearts; Black 2's, the user's: its "HP" tag and strips from `public/bars/`, 2 screen pixels to the sprite's 1, the fill's tile repeated along a bar of any width, `BAR_BORDER`, `barTile`), in the minimised site menu and the phone's top bar (the run's numbers show `StatusBox`): full and green, after one lost battle 45% and yellow, after two 15% and red (`hpBarFill`, tested in `HpBar.test.ts`); a Pokémon Center's heal (1 HP) takes it back up one level, the bar sliding to its new length and colour |
| `RunStats.tsx` | The run's numbers, in the site menu on a big screen (`NavRail`) and the side panel's Run tab on a phone: HP as a Pokémon health bar (`HpBar`), ₱ and the bank, the account's Stardust, HP and the run's EXP in Black 2's player box (`StatusBox`), the account's EXP, the battle rules and consumables in effect, the equipment worn and what it does, and the Bag. The equipment and the Bag fold away (`RunSection`) |
| `DeltaFloat.tsx` | A run's HP or ₱ changing: the change (`+₱150`, `−1 HP`) floats up from beside the number and fades (`delta-float` in `index.css`, 1.8s), green for a gain and red for a loss, in `RunStats` and the minimised site menu's HP and ₱. Each change floats, several at once if they come quickly; nothing on first showing, or with reduced motion |
| `RunSection.tsx` | A section of `RunStats` that folds away (the Equipped list and the Bag): clicking its heading (the section's name and how many things are in it) opens or closes it; open at first, remembered per section in the browser (`poke-tcg.run-sections`) |
| `RunMap.tsx` | The path of steps as a vertical column on the left, climbing from the first step at the bottom to the run's last boss at the top: the encounter taken at past steps, what later steps offer (faded; Gym Trainers and bosses labelled, bosses bigger), and the current step's options as large quick-select buttons: clicking one goes there at once, a battle with the current deck (`RouteChoice` beside the map describes them all and can switch decks). It scrolls on its own, kept centred on the current step. On a phone it's only as tall as the current step and 2 steps either side (`AROUND`), with smaller badges, and scrolls for the rest |
| `RouteView.tsx` | For a run type with a route (Pewter City: Route 2): the route's pixel map in a window alone in the top row, as wide as the page allows up to the widest of the run's maps at 2× (at least 48rem; no scaling beyond 2×) (28rem tall at first; drag the handle under it to change its height, double-click to reset; remembered in the browser under `poke-tcg.route-map`), at 2×, which pans by dragging or with the mouse wheel (up and down; sideways with a trackpad or Shift), opening centred on the player (at the run's first step he starts at the route's `entrance`, a tile in from the edge the run starts at, e.g. Route 2's 9,78, and walks to the first spot; the target button centres it again; the speed button below it makes every walk, fade and glide 1×, 2× or 3× as fast, clicked in turn, showing one, two or three arrows, remembered in the browser under `poke-tcg.route-speed`; on a phone, a lock button below that stops dragging from panning the map, so a swipe over it scrolls the page, the view still following Red and the target button still working; on by default, remembered under `poke-tcg.route-lock`). On a phone, once an encounter is over (its reward taken), the run screen scrolls back up to the map (`HomePage`). When the run moves on, Red walks to the next spot tile by tile (of the shortest walks, when the spot's people stand in a line, the one getting onto the spot's column (a row of people, on one y) or row (a column of them, on one x) soonest and coming straight along it, so he steps into their line of sight only as he arrives, as in the games; `routePath`'s `lineOfSight`; agreed) with FireRed's walk cycle, the view following him, where a walkable path joins the spots (`routePath` in `routes.ts`: around the people standing on the route where it can, up to 40 tiles; a route's `links`, a gatehouse's doors, fade him out at one door and back in at the other); with no path he fades out, the view glides to the new spot, and he fades back in. On Route 2 every spot can be walked to from the one before, the east gatehouse (spots 4 to 5) the only fade (the cut-tree, item ball and gatehouse door tiles walkable, from the user; a test checks). Every spot shows its figures from the start: upcoming spots their step's options, the current spot Red (FireRed's player sprite) with its options or the encounter under way, passed spots the encounter taken (faded). Figures stand on walkable tiles near their spot, facing it, each on its own tile: beside Red (left and right) wherever they can, then in line ahead of him (the way the map's encounters go: its `flow`, 'up', 'down', 'left' or 'right', e.g. Route 2 up, Route 5 and Route 6 down, gyms up, Routes 4 and 22 right, Routes 8 and 15 left; on a left or right map, beside means above and below; a spot can go its own way, `spotFlows`: Pokémon Tower 1F's first, walked up to from the door, is 'up'), Red facing that way by default, behind, and only then off the lines; each way 2 tiles from Red, or the tile next to him where that one can't be walked on, and 3 tiles away only once no way has either (agreed). A battle's trainer stays, at full opacity, while its reward is chosen, and fades with the other passed spots once Red walks on. Figures are a trainer's class's FireRed overworld sprite, bosses their own; for the other encounters the shop's clerk, Nurse Joy, the collector for a gift, the trader, and an item ball for a Mystery until it's revealed, then who its event is about (`encounterSprite` in `utils/overworld.ts`, `public/overworld/`), each with a speech bubble above it (below its feet when it stands below Red's spot; black-outlined, tail too) showing its encounter type's icon (`ENCOUNTER_STYLE`, as in the encounter panels). With a gym interior (`gym` on the run type: Pewter Gym, FireRed's map), the Gym Trainer and leader steps are inside it: after the route's last encounter Red fades out and back in at the gym's door, the view switching to the gym, and walks to the Gym Trainer, who stands halfway in where FireRed has Camper Liam (3,8) (a Kanto gym's Gym Trainer is picked each run from the trainers in that gym in FireRed and LeafGreen, a gym's `trainers` in bosses.ts, e.g. Vermilion's Sailor Dwayne, Engineer Baily or Gentleman Tucker, each with the sprite FireRed gives them there: `GYM_SPRITES` and the Tamer, Channeler and Engineer classes in `utils/overworld.ts`, the Engineer's `public/overworld/balding_man.png` converted like the others, its first 9 frames with colour 0 transparent; Johto's come from a general list), then on to the leader, who waits on their platform (Brock, 6,5) all along; the Gym Guy stands by the door. Without one, the gym steps' figures stand at the route's last spot. A run type with more than one route (`routes` on the run type; Vermilion City: Route 5, then Route 6) walks them in order, each holding its spots' steps: after a route's last encounter Red walks to its `exit` (if it has one: Route 5's gatehouse to Saffron, 24,32; the Saffron run's Route 5 leaves by it for the gym too), fades out, and comes back in at the next route's `entrance` (Route 6: Saffron's south gate, 13,5), the view switching to it. Choosing a battle (or accepting an Ace Trainer's) puts FireRed's "!" balloon over the trainer (`public/overworld/exclamation.png`, popping open over its three frames) and turns Red to them, as when a trainer spots you; the battle's request goes at once, and its game opens when it's in and the "!" has shown for a second (`spotted` and `SPOTTED_MS` in `useRun`), which hides the loading time. Choosing any other encounter makes its NPC walk up beside Red at once (walk cycle, along the route to the free tile next to him it reaches soonest), and turn to him, while its request runs, and stays there, faded, once the encounter is over (on a reload, on the free tile beside the spot nearest where it stood); a Mystery's character only once it's revealed, and an item ball stays put. Replaces `RunMap` for those runs. The view's panning, size and height are `usePan`, the walk `useWalk`, the figures' placement `utils/routeFigures.ts`, and what's drawn on the map `RouteSprites.tsx` |
| `RouteSprites.tsx` | What `RouteView` draws on the map, in map pixels: `OverworldSprite` (a FireRed overworld sprite on a tile, facing a way, with its walking frame, step slide, fade and the tall grass cutting off its legs), `Figure` (a figure by its spot: a boss's own sprite or an overworld one, with `EncounterBubble`, the speech bubble of its encounter's icon), `Exclamation` (FireRed's "!" over a trainer who spotted you) and the debug `TileOverlay` (every tile's x,y and walkable area, ledges amber with an arrow the way they're jumped (`ledgeAt`; the user's), spots, entrance and exit); each sprite's strip is redrawn once with 2 see-through pixels round every frame (`useGutteredStrip`), so a fractional zoom or a walk between whole pixels never samples a sliver of the next frame (a dark line beside Red walking north, the user's) |
| `RouteChoice.tsx` | The middle of the run screen while choosing the next encounter: every option of the current step at once, kept compact (agreed: short lines, assuming the player knows this kind of game; no "Lose" line for ordinary battles; the other stops show just their title). The battles come together, each with its opponent (special battles with their kind beside it), just the 2 Pokémon types most common among the Pokémon their class favours (`trainerFavorites`, typed from the catalog, with Energy icons; hover says what they are), a short note for Ace Trainers, Gym Trainers and bosses (the deck, walking away, rematch), the reward in one line (₱ · EXP · card rarity, and a boss's badge or prize), the whole row being the button that takes it (no Battle or Go buttons: clicking anywhere on an option, or Enter on it, chooses it; an arrow on its right says so; agreed); then one deck choice for whichever battle is taken, a drop-down menu on every screen (`DeckMenu`: on a desktop its scrolling list floats over the page below it, or above without room below) with the decks starred on the Collection tab below it under Favorite decks (agreed): Current run (the run's deck) first, or switch to one of your other playable decks (with their headline Pokémon and card split, and a link to the Collection tab). The other encounters come together, each with what it offers (a Mystery's event once the Itemfinder has revealed it) and Go. On a big screen the panel is always two columns (agreed 2026-10-22): the encounters on the left (the battles, then the other stops under them, headed Encounters, or Encounter for one), the deck choice on the right (headed Battle with, or Your deck on a step with no battle). The deck picked is switched in with whichever option is taken, battle or not (`run.choose`'s `deckId`). On a phone the other stops come above the battles and the deck choice (under the stops when the step has no battle). On a phone the other stops are headed Event and the decks are a drop-down menu (`DeckMenu`) whose entries are deck tiles as on the Collection tab (name, headline Pokémon, the Pokémon/Trainer/Energy bar), the run's deck picked. On a phone, `side` (the side panel's tabs, `PanelTabs`) is at the bottom of the panel |
| `EncounterPanel.tsx` | The encounter being played: a battle (go to its game; an Ace Trainer can be declined first), a battle's reward (pick 1 of 3 cards; on a phone they shrink to share one row, RunCard's `fluid`, as a free card's do; a card shop's share two rows), a free card, a card shop, a trade, a Pokémon Center or a Mystery event. Cards on offer that you haven't unlocked yet (rewards, free cards, shop stock, a trade's card, the shady dealer's) are tagged NEW |
| `RunDeckPanel.tsx` | The cards unlocked in this run, under the encounter: one per kind with how many came, grouped into Pokémon, Trainers and Energy, in the left half of its pane (wrapping within it), and the Bag (`BagGrid`) in the right half (the user's; the link to the Collection tab removed). On a phone the halves are one above the other and the cards start minimised, opened by the button in the top right corner |
| `BagPanel.tsx` | Consumables and misc items you own with a Use button, disabled past the run's limits or for a consumable during a battle (in `RunStats`; folds away, `RunSection`), and `BagGrid`: the same items as Black 2 and White 2's Bag (the user's): a list of names and counts scrolled through (arrow keys too), the one clicked outlined in the accent, and along the bottom its picture, what it does and Use, in the right half of the run screen's unlocked cards pane (`RunDeckPanel`); both through `useBag` |
| `RunOver.tsx` | After a run: beaten (`ItemShop` first, then the results (EXP, the cards gained, badge), then `NewRunPicker` for the next run; the results and the shop each have a close button that shrinks them to a slim bar, which opens them again. They stay closed for that run only, remembered in the browser under `poke-tcg.run-over-closed`, so the next run's end shows them full size), or lost (start over, or Retry with 3 HP for testing), or conceded (start over, saying what was banked) |
| `SaveRunDeck.tsx` | After a run (beaten, lost or conceded), before the next run: save the run's deck as it ended under a name filled in with the run's number and type ("Run #3 – Celadon City", `run.saveDeck`). Unsaved, the next run takes the run deck over |
| `ConcedeRun.tsx` | Concede the run, below the cards unlocked this run while choosing the next step: asks first, saying what's kept (the cards gained, and the ₱ if a battle was finished) |
| `ItemShop.tsx` | The post-run shop: items for account EXP, from the stock rolled after the latest run (`shopStock`: 3–4 consumables, 1–2 misc items and 1 piece of gear, never a one-of-a-kind item the account already has, such as the Itemfinder, another taking its place; `run.shop`, and `run.buyItem` refuses anything else). The stock is saved on the run (`Run.shop`, migration `20261027090000_run_shop`) the first time the shop opens once the run is over, so buying something doesn't change what's offered. Consumables and misc items that are used up show how many are in your bag; other items you have show an Owned badge. Each item has its picture (`ItemIcon`) on the left of its name and description. Equipment and misc items that aren't used up can't be bought again while you have one (`onlyOne` in `packages/campaign/src/items.ts`; `run.buyItem` refuses too), so the button says Already owned. `run.shop` returns the counts (`owned`) |
| `DeckMenu.tsx` | The deck choice of `RouteChoice` and `NewRunPicker`: a drop-down menu whose entries are the Collection's deck tiles (`DeckMenu`, with `DeckTile` from DeckList.tsx, a deck that can't be played showing why under its Pokémon's names), and `DeckOption`, a deck tile to pick (the favorite decks under the menu) |
| `NewRunPicker.tsx` | Picks the next run's type and one of your decks (from the Collection tab, in a drop-down menu of deck tiles, `DeckMenu`, with the favorite decks under it; `run.overview` lists each deck's cards and whether it's a favorite), then starts it. The deck picked to start with is the one played most recently (`User.lastDeckId`, migration `20261031090000_last_deck`: set by starting a run with it, switching a run's battle to it, or a Training battle with it), or, when that deck is gone or can't be played, the top playable one in the deck list. A Generation dropdown (Gen 1 · Kanto, Gen 2 · Johto) picks whose runs are listed, starting on the latest run's generation (agreed 2026-10-23: the Next run and Start a run pickers open on its first open run; Start over on the run itself); run types are buttons: the final boss's overworld sprite (`public/trainers/`), the run's name, and for a gym run its gym leader and the types their deck is built around (`bossTypes`), or for a longer run a row of all its bosses' sprites; beaten runs are ticked ("Badge", or "Badge ×N"), locked ones say what unlocks them and can't be picked. Decks that break the rules or hold locked cards are listed with why and can't be picked |
| `RunCard.tsx` | A card offered or owned in a run, with the large hover preview (as on the board) and the `CardInspector` on right-click, its rarity, an optional price and an optional corner badge |
| `encounterIcons.tsx` | A Material UI symbol and colour per kind of encounter, Gym Trainers, gym leaders, the Elite Four and the Champion included (`EncounterBadge`) |

### `src/components/extras/`

The Extras page's minigames (see [Extras](#extras)).

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `BerryFarm.tsx` | The Berry Farm: the farm's map (as a run's `Route`, panned by `usePan`) with a tree of every berry at its stage (each stage's first frame, then its second, as the game plays them), the player (Brendan or May) walking to a tree clicked (`routePath`) to pick it (`extras.harvest`, a "+N" rising from it), an unripe one's time left in a toast; Gather all ripe (`extras.harvestAll`) above it; the Berry Pouch under it; debug builds' Ripen every tree (`extras.ripenAll`) |
| `BerryBlender.tsx` | The Berry Blender's page: the account's berries to choose one from (each with its flavours), its max speed record, the Pokéblock Case (its newest 50, each in its colour's picture), and the game (`BlenderScreen`) |
| `BlenderScreen.tsx` | The Berry Blender's screen, drawn on a canvas 4× the GBA's 240×160 as Emerald draws it: the berries thrown in, the centre dropping in and the 3-2-1 countdown and START, the game at 60 frames a second (`blenderFrame`) with the score symbols, sparkles, progress bar and RPM, then the wind-down, the ranking, the results, the Pokéblock made (`extras.blend`) and "Would you like to blend another BERRY?"; auto play plays Cycling while it spins (`blenderMusic`) |
| `minigameSound.ts` | The minigames' sounds (`MinigameSound`): loaded and played with the Web Audio API, each on its player with its priority (a new one replacing the one playing unless that one's is higher), one looped with its pitch (`startLoop`, `setPitch`), all stopped (`stopAll`); their volume, the same for every minigame (`loadVolume`, `saveVolume`: `poke-tcg.minigame-volume`, the blender's old key read if it's unset) |
| `MinigameVolume.tsx` | The volume slider under a minigame's screen (the speaker beside it silencing the sounds and back) |
| `blenderSound.ts` | The Berry Blender's sounds (`BlenderSound`): Emerald's, each on its music player (SE1, SE2 or the fanfare) with the game's priorities, the whirring looped (`startWhirring`) |
| `PoffinCooking.tsx` | Poffin cooking's page: the account's berries to choose one from (each with its Platinum flavours and smoothness), the Poffin Case (its newest 100, each with its sprite, level and smoothness), and the cooking (`PoffinScreen`) |
| `PoffinScreen.tsx` | Poffin cooking's screen, the DS's two screens drawn on a canvas 3× their 256×192 as Platinum draws them: the berry dropping in, the stylus hint and the countdown, the cooking (`cookFrame`, 30 frames a second, the pointer held on the bottom screen the stylus) with the batter turning and growing with its speed and fading into the next phase's, the flames flickering, the arrows, warnings, puffs and scorch marks, then DONE!, the results (`extras.cook`) and "Would you like to keep cooking Poffins?"; auto play plays Poffins (`poffinMusic`) |
| `poffinSound.ts` | Poffin cooking's sounds (`PoffinSound`): Platinum's, each on its player (SE 1, 2 or 3), and each phase's lap, spill and burn sounds |
| `GameCorner.tsx` | The Mauville Game Corner's page: the Coin Case, and the game's room (`GAME_CORNER_MAP`) the page's whole width (on a phone at least 2×, scrolled sideways; the user's) with its people, the counter's clerks drawn 2px lower and cut off at its top edge so their legs and feet are hidden behind it (`COUNTER_EDGE`, `SINK`; the user's, as the Battle Tower's receptionist); a slot machine or roulette table clicked walks the player there (`routePath`) and sits them at it, the coin clerk sells coins for banked ₱ with her words, her menu as her question's choices, the money and coins boxes beside them (`extras.buyCoins`), the others say their lines, in the Battle Tower's text box (`DialogueBox`, the user's: a click anywhere on the room, Enter or Space shows the line whole, then goes on; Escape too, or EXIT at her question); the door's mat walks the player out, back to the Extras menu; each round played settled in turn (`extras.coinRound`); auto play plays the Game Corner's theme (`gameCornerMusic`); debug builds' Fill the Coin Case (`extras.fillCoins`) |
| `RouletteScreen.tsx` | The Game Corner's roulette, drawn on a canvas 4× the GBA's 240×160 as Emerald draws it: the table's question ("The minimum wager at this table is…", or "You don't have enough COINS."), then the board at 60 frames a second (`rouletteFrame`): the grid's selection and its flashing box, the wheel spinning, the ball, Shroomish and Taillow, the messages and Keep playing?; arrows, Z/Enter/Space (A), X/Backspace/Escape (B), a click on a square bets on it, and on-screen D-pad, A and B buttons for touch. Each ball's bet and payout go to `onRound`, leaving to `onExit`; the music pauses for the win fanfares (`useMusicHold`) |
| `rouletteDraw.ts` | The roulette's screen, drawn pixel by pixel from the game's tiles, tilemaps and palettes (`renderRoulette`): the table's felt, BG 2 (the wheel, an affine BG turned with it), BG 1 (the grid, sliding off and on), BG 0 (the selection's box) and the sprites among them by priority and subpriority, Shroomish's shadow blended over what's under it |
| `rouletteSound.ts` | The roulette's sounds (`RouletteSound`): Emerald's sound effects and its 2 fanfares, each on its music player (SE1 or SE2) with the game's priority (`ROULETTE_SOUNDS`), the cries on their own player, and Taillow's wing flaps looped until it flies off |
| `SlotMachineScreen.tsx` | A Mauville Game Corner slot machine, drawn as the GBA's PPU draws it: the BGs from Emerald's tiles and tilemaps (the machine, the reels' shading blended over the reels, Reel Time's sliding window, the info screen, the message and YES/NO windows) and the sprites in their palettes and priorities (reels, CREDIT/PAYOUT counters, the digital display's scenes, Pika Power bolts, Reel Time's Pikachu machine), with the game's palette effects (lines lighting and flashing, the lights, the Poké Ball's shine, Pikachu's aura, fades), window 0's wipes and the sprite mosaic, 4× on a canvas at 60 frames a second (`slotFrame`); keys, a click or tap as A, and buttons under the screen; each spin's bet and payout to `onRound` |
| `slotSound.ts` | The slot machines' sounds (`SlotSound`): Emerald's sound effects and fanfares, each on its music player with the game's priority, and Reel Time's song (MUS_ROULETTE) looped while its lottery runs (`startRoulette`) |
| `VoltorbFlip.tsx` | Voltorb Flip's page: the game's screen, its boards and rounds from the API (`extras.voltorbRound`, `extras.voltorbSettle`), Quit at its menu back to Extras |
| `VoltorbFlipScreen.tsx` | Voltorb Flip, drawn on a canvas 3× the DS's two 256×192 screens as HeartGold draws them and ported frame by frame from pokeheartgold's `src/voltorb_flip/`: its task engine and workflows (the menu, Game Info's screens, flipping, the memo, quitting, winning, the payout, the reveal), its input (touch and D-pad), sprites and sounds |
| `voltorbFlipSound.ts` | Voltorb Flip's sounds (`VoltorbFlipSound`): HeartGold's sound effects and fanfares |
| `voltorbFlipText.ts` | Voltorb Flip's text, by message number (msg_0039; generated by `hgss_voltorb_flip.py`) |
| `BerryIcon.tsx` | A berry's bag icon (`BerryIcon`) and its name ("Cheri Berry", `berryName`) |

### `src/components/tower/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `TowerScene.tsx` | A Battle Tower scene (see [Battle Tower](#battle-tower)): `useScene`, its director, runs scripts of awaited steps (someone walking along the room's paths, `routePath`, at the run maps' pace and walk cycle; someone following another a step behind; a line said, waiting for a click or Enter; a question asked, waiting for a choice; a line left up while a deck is picked; fading between rooms; the elevator riding), a new script or leaving the page stopping the one running; `TowerScene` draws the room at the largest zoom that fits (up to 3×, 60% of the window's height) (on a phone, the user's: as tall as the screen below it allows, less room for the deck picker, closed (`PHONE_PANEL_ROOM`, 250px), so it shows without scrolling; a battle room never wider than the window, both sides of its court in view, wider than the window where it must be, the view following the player across it; the dialogue box the scene's width with smaller text, a question's choices above it on the right), its people as on the run maps (`OverworldSprite`), and the games' text box over its bottom edge (`DialogueBox.tsx`, shared with the Game Corner) (game art: fixed colours, not the themes'), with a question's choices on its right, each line typed out a letter at a time as in the games (25ms a letter, held by Pause; the user's), a click on it, anywhere on the scene or Enter showing it whole, then going on (the user's), a question's choices coming up once its line is done (the box as narrow from a conversation's first line as when they come up: `reserve` keeps their room beside it, the receptionist's from the desk, the attendant's from his turn, so its text never reflows; the user's); before each battle the battle transition (`BattleTransition.tsx`, over the play area as a run's, not just the scene; the user's), the battle's page preloaded meanwhile; debug builds with the debug tools on add buttons in its top right corner, as the run map's: Show tiles (the run maps' `TileOverlay`, and the room's named places outlined and named) and Pause (every wait, the walk cycle and the elevator's shake held until it's lifted; a walk stops at the end of its step) |
| `BpShop.tsx` | The lobby's BP Shop (the user's), opened by its vendor (Exchange BP at the desk), closed with Return to reception: the unlocked sets' booster packs for Battle Points (`BP_PACK_PRICE`, from `tower.overview`), each opened in the Packs tab's window (`PackOpening` with `currency` bp) |
| `VsBanner.tsx` | The VS banner under the tower's map (the user's): the player's deck against the opponent's, as the VS splash screens draw it (game art: fixed colours) |
| `towerScripts.ts` | The Battle Tower's scenes as scripts: the lobby (`lobbyScript`: to the desk, the receptionist's saved-challenge question, then her welcome and its choices, the user's script: Start challenge (the floor where checkpoints give a choice, then the deck; "Please head up the stairs to your Battle Room." in Gen 1's lobby, "I will now show you to the Battle Room." where she leads the way), Exchange BP (over to the BP Shop's vendor, a Poké Mart clerk at 14,9 in Gen 1's lobby, the player at 14,11: his welcome, then the shop under the scene until Return to reception, then back to the desk), Explain the challenge, Leave Battle Tower (her goodbye, "Thanks for visiting the Battle Tower!" and "We hope to see you again soon!", then out of the door to the Play tab's menu); then led to the elevator, or in Gen 1 up the stairs), the elevator's ride and the corridor (the clerk leading the way), the battle room (the opponent coming in, their line, the battle), back from a battle (`afterBattleScript`: the opponent's line, leaving; a loss: the attendant's "Aww, you lost! That's the end of your Battle Tower challenge." and "You won X battles in a row. Please make your way back to the lobby.", the player out by the door, fading in on Gen 1's lobby stairs and down to the desk, the receptionist's "Thanks for taking the Battle Tower Challenge!" and "Would you like to start another Battle Tower challenge?" with her choices (`lobbyScript`'s `returning`; the user's), and the same walk back after Save and quit (she checks before a new challenge ends the one just saved), Concede and beating floor 100, the attendant's line ending "Please make your way back to the lobby."; the attendant's prize (BP), checkpoint and question: Continue, Save and quit, Concede, and in debug Skip battle), and between battles after a reload (`roomScript`); each set's staff (`STAFF`) |

### `src/components/idle/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
