| `AreaScene.tsx` | The Idle Adventure's map scene: `MapScene` (a game screen centred on the page, 20 tiles by 17 at the map's 3×, narrower with the page and shorter with the window (most of its height below the header, at least 22rem; on a phone 22rem at 2×), the map cut off at its edges, centred across a way it fits (Oak's lab), otherwise panned to keep the player in the middle, its people, the "!" over a trainer who has seen the player, a fade between maps; what's passed in drawn over it, and its `dialogue` in the text box, `DialogueBox`, no wider than 768px or the map drawn (`maxWidth`), a click anywhere on the scene a click on the box; the map hidden until the scene is measured and its picture loaded and decoded, the pan's slide on only from the frame after it was first placed, so coming back from a battle never pans and a new map never draws in bit by bit: the user's), `usePreloadMaps` (fetches every map of the area the run is in and the next two, their towns' grind loops' too) and `AreaScene` (the area's map for the playback's step: Red or Leaf walking the track, surfing on water, its trainers where the game has them, Swimmers swimming; a notice in the text box, shown whole, nothing to click) |
| `BattleScreen.tsx` | A battle as it plays: FireRed's 240x160 layout scaled to its box (container query units): the terrain or room background, the foe's front sprite (its trainer's pic before they send one out), the player's Pokémon's back (each where FireRed draws it: its species' picture offset and the foe's elevation, `pic` in the species data, from the decompilation's `*_pic_coordinates.h` and `enemy_mon_elevation.h`), healthboxes drawn in FireRed's colours, under the screen the player's Pokémon's moves with their cooldown bars (`MoveBars`, the mode's one addition: each slot move's bar a smooth timer filling over its cooldown (its ticks × `TICK_MS` from the moment it went up, keyframes `idle-cooldown`; the user's), lit when ready; not the basic move, which never cools down: the user's), the player's box just above the text box with FireRed's EXP. bar along its bottom (the dark green strip, EXP in yellow, the striped track filling light blue toward the next level: the battle party's EXP., then each gain's), a thrown ball shaking, the text box with the latest line, or a run of rewards all at once (each Pokémon's icon, EXP. gained, level reached and moves learned; the user's) (the catch-up's pages over it in the scenes' `DialogueBox` if a battle starts before they're read); the hit blink and attack lunge (keyframes `idle-*` in index.css); game art, so fixed colours |
| `TownMenu.tsx` | A town: its map with the player before the Pokémon Center and its choice in the text box (Gym or the Elite Four, Grind to Lv. N before an unbeaten gym, greyed out once the party is there, Store, Continue once the gym is beaten; Gym or Continue picked after 5 s with no choice, `TOWN_WAIT_MS`, store open or not, its countdown in the corner), and `Store` (its stock, prices, the bag's counts, buying) |
| `PartyScreen.tsx` | The party in two columns (the user's; one on a phone), in party order left to right: its order (earlier and later), each Pokémon's types, nature, ability, stats, IVs and EVs, its basic move and 3 slots with their cooldowns (or automatic), the stones in the bag it can use; `MonIcon` (a party icon, its two frames in turn), `TypeBadge` |
| `Pokedex.tsx` | The account's Pokédex: Kanto's 151 (unseen dark, seen grey, caught in colour), a species' entry (FireRed's text, height, weight) and its best IVs and EVs; with `onPick`, a new run's starter picked from it |
| `IdleSettings.tsx` | The mode's settings (auto-heal and its Revive policies, auto-catch, the auto-buy balls count, the full-party toggles, auto-evolve, auto-grind), `Bag` (the bag's items) and `catchUpPages` (what happened while away, as pages for the scene's text box) |
| `StarterScene.tsx` | Professor Oak's lab with the three starters' balls on his table, and his question in the text box with a choice for each, side by side under his line (`inline`, its front sprite, its margin cut off by `TrimmedSprite`, over its name) |

### `src/components/packs/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `BoosterPack.tsx` | A booster pack: its real wrapper art (`art`, the scan from `PACK_ART`, its sides trimmed a little and corners rounded), or for a set without one (the Black Star Promos, never sold in packs) the set's colours and logo on foil with crimped ends; a sheen sweeps across it. Sizes `lg` (opening), `sm` (the Packs tab) and `xs` (the phone's grid: as wide as its box, without the small print). It floats while waiting, shakes while being bought, and when opened its top seal is torn off from left to right: the torn strip folds back over the tear like a page being turned, showing its silver inside and getting longer as the tear goes on, then comes away, leaving nothing of the seal behind (`Seal`, `pack-tear` with the `--peel` custom property), with a gold flash behind the middle of the pack that fades out softly. The pack stays where it was through the shake and the tear (its float is paused, and the window keeps the controls' space while it tears) |
| `PackOpening.tsx` | The window for opening a pack (it scrolls when it's taller than the screen, and the page behind it doesn't, `useScrollLock`): click it to buy it with the bank's ₱, the top tears off, the 11 cards burst out face down into a fan and gather into a stack (Web Animations API) while their large images load, then click through them or Skip (every card of every pack, pack after pack; only the rares, on the same screen, with the commons separated or when several packs are opened on auto open), ending on the list of every card opened, the rares and holos framed and tagged with a count; Done closes it. Each card flips up as a `HoloCard`, with the card viewer's tilt and holo effect; rares and holos glow behind, and new unlocks pop a NEW badge. Everything is sized to the screen: the pack (`BoosterPack` `xl`) and the revealed card (`PACK_REVEAL`, layout.ts) as big as the window's height allows, the commons and the list in wide layouts with larger cards (`PACK_COMMON`, `PACK_SUMMARY`, through `RunCard`'s `width`) |
| `Fireworks.tsx` | Fireworks over the whole screen for about 4 seconds (`canvas-confetti`, on its own canvas that lets clicks through; none with reduced motion), set off by an all-rares pack |
| `GenerationFilter.tsx` | The Packs tab's generation filter: a dropdown with a checkbox per generation (Gen 1 · Kanto, Gen 2 · Johto); every set shows until some are picked (each visit starts with all); with `toggles` (Community Decks and the Collection's deck list) each box turns a generation on or off, all on to start; with `icon` (the deck list's toolbar) the button is a Filters icon |

### `src/hooks/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `useRun.ts` | The home screen's data (`run.overview`, always refetched on arrival) and the run's moves (`run.act`, `choose`, `useItem`, `retry`, `start`); a move that starts a battle goes to its game, and one that gains a card refreshes the unlocks. Choosing a battle marks its trainer `spotted` (the route map's "!"), and once the request is in and the "!" has shown for `SPOTTED_MS` (1s), the battle transition plays (`useBattleTransition`) and its game opens as it ends (preloaded meanwhile) |
| `useRunMusic.ts` | Auto play's cue for a run (the run page and its battles: `runMusic`, and the Pokémon Center's jingle as Nurse Joy heals), for a Training battle (`usePracticeMusic`) and for a Battle Tower battle (`useTowerBattleMusic`: a trainer's battle theme of its floor's generation) |
| `useTower.ts` | The Battle Tower's data (`tower.overview`, refetched on arrival) and moves (`tower.start`, `act`, the debug-only `debugSkipBattle`), `towerUnderway`, and `usePlayRedirect` (keeps the Play tab on the mode under way) |
| `useIdle.ts` | The Idle Adventure's data (`idle.overview`) and moves (`idle.start`, `act`, `settings`, the debug-only `debugWarp`), and `useIdlePlayback`: catches up the time away (`idle.catchUp`, again while there's more; a hidden tab counts as away, the user's: its playback stops while hidden, since the browser slows its timers and the time would be lost, and starts over with a catch-up when the tab is shown again, `useTabVisible`), then asks for each next segment (`idle.next`) and plays it: the walk a step at a time, a trainer's "!", the battle's events a turn per 10 s (`TICK_MS`; a line's silent events after it, an attack's hit with its HP drop and blink, half a second after it, `HIT_BEAT_MS`; a run of rewards, EXP., levels and moves learned, as one line, shown at once), the tick for the page's tick meter (`turn`, `tickAt`), a line for money, catches, evolutions and badges; stops when the run waits in a town or the page goes |
| `useIdleMoney.ts` | The idle run's ₱ for the top bar while in the mode (apart from useIdle.ts, so the layout doesn't load the mode's data) |
| `useEscape.ts` | `useEscape(onEscape, enabled)`: calls `onEscape` when Escape is pressed anywhere, while `enabled` (every dialog, panel and menu that Escape closes, and the move hooks' cancel) |
| `useHoldToInspect.ts` | Press and hold to inspect: after 350ms an item is `inspecting` until the press ends anywhere, and the click ending the hold is swallowed (moving 6px first cancels it). Used by `CardGrid` on the Collection tab, where a finger's hold opens the card, as soon as it registers (not on letting go), in the `CardInspector` (a phone has no right-click) |
| `useUnlocked.ts` | Which cards the account has unlocked (`decks.unlocked`): `isLocked(card)`, never for basic Energy |
| `useSwipe.ts` | A quick sideways swipe on a touch screen (60px across, at least twice as far across as up or down, within 600ms): the phone Collection's swipe right to open the decks panel, and a `SidePanel` swiped back towards its edge to close it |
| `useScrollLock.ts` | Stops the page scrolling (and a finger panning anything) while a card is viewed large: the `CardInspector`, and a card held in `CardGrid`. The inspector still scrolls itself when it's taller than the screen. Through `lockPage`, so overlapping locks can end in any order |
| `useFavorites.ts` | The account's favorite cards (`decks.favorites`): `ids` (the latest first), `isFavorite`, and `toggle` (`decks.setFavorite`, shown at once and undone if it fails) |
| `useOptions.ts` | The player's options (the Options tab): saved in localStorage (`poke-tcg.options`) and shared by the tab and the game page. Both default to on |
| `useCosmetics.ts` | The card back image, playmat and coin in use: in a battle its deck's (`DeckCosmeticsContext`), otherwise and for any kind it has none of, the account's defaults (`cosmetics.list`) |
| `useThemedFigures.ts` | The Settings page's Theme toggles (the user's): Themed EXP bar (`useExpTheme`, on by default: the run's EXP bar and its "EXP" in the colour theme's accent, or the game's blue; `StatusBox`) and Themed Pokédollars (`usePokedollarTheme`, off by default: the run's ₱ in the accent, or the Pokédollar icon's gold, `POKEDOLLAR_GOLD`; `usePokedollarColor` for the run's numbers, the minimised menu and the phone's top bar); each kept in localStorage and with the account (`expTheme`, `pokedollarTheme`) |
| `useToggleSetting.ts` | `toggleSetting`: an on/off Settings page choice remembered in the browser and with the account (`registerSetting`/`saveSetting`), as a shared hook |
| `useSkipFlips.ts` | Whether coin flips skip their animation: saved in localStorage (`poke-tcg.skip-coin-flips`) and set on the Settings page and in the phone's side panel |
| `useColorTheme.ts` | The colour theme: saved in localStorage (`poke-tcg.theme`), put on `<html>` as soon as the module loads (main.tsx imports it, so there's no flash of the default) and shared by everything that shows or changes it |
| `useWalk.ts` | `RouteView`'s walk: when the run moves on, the player walks to the new spot tile by tile along `routePath` (around the figures standing there; into their line of sight only as he arrives), through gatehouses fading out at one door and in at the other, onto the next map by its exit and the next one's entrance, or fades out and back in with no path (`MAX_WALK` 40 tiles); the view following him. Also the `Walker` state, the pace (a bike 2×, tall grass `GRASS_PACE`) and the fade timings |
| `usePan.ts` | `RouteView`'s view: its height (the handle under it, `poke-tcg.route-map`), its measured size, and where the map sits in it: the offset, kept inside the map (`centreOn`, `centreOnMap`), the glide it moves with on its own, and panning by dragging (the map is moved directly while the pointer moves, the offset catching up when the drag ends) or the mouse wheel (off while a phone's map is locked) |
| `useFontStyle.ts` | The UI's font (Settings): Pokémon R/B/Y (Pixellocale, the pixel font), Pokémon R/S/E (`emerald`: drawn 125%, its headings 24px), Pokémon S/M (`noto`, the default: index.html's `data-font` too, for the first paint) or Standard (the system font); saved in localStorage (`poke-tcg.font`) and put on `<html>` as `data-font` as soon as the module loads (main.tsx imports it). `index.css` reads it: Standard swaps Tailwind's sans for the system font and puts back the sizes and weights tuned for the pixel font (`text-chip`, `text-nav`, `text-title`, `font-nav`, `font-heading`) to the ones the site had with it |
| `useWindowFill.ts` | Makes an element fill the window from where it starts to the bottom (the game table, the run hub and the Collection tab on a phone) |
| `useIsPhone.ts` | Whether the screen is a phone held upright (`(max-width: 767px) and (orientation: portrait)`), kept up to date: the battle screen and the Collection tab switch to their phone layouts |
| `useLockedScreen.ts` | Holds the page still for the phone battle screen: no scrolling, overscroll or pull to refresh, and no pinch zoom (a locked viewport tag, plus Safari's gesture events and two-finger touches blocked); undone on leaving |
| `useTouchHold.ts` | A long press (450ms, a finger kept within 8px) on a touch screen; `Card` uses it to open the `CardInspector`, as right-click does |
| `useCardPlay.ts` | Playing cards from the hand by click (select, then click a highlighted slot; Esc cancels) or drag and drop (Trainers onto anywhere in the play area, or onto the Pokémon they're for: `onDropOnSlot`, a slot under the pointer counting before the play area). Tracks the selected and dragged card, reports why a clicked card can't be played (`notify`), and returns the highlighted slots, their label, click handlers and the `DndContext` props |
| `useAttack.ts` | Using an attack: attacks straight away or asks its questions one at a time (via the engine's `nextAttackChoice`), and says why an attack can't be used (Esc cancels) |
| `usePower.ts` | Using Pokémon Powers: lists your Pokémon's powers with why each can't be used, tracks the benched Pokémon clicked to show its Activate button, and asks a power's questions one at a time (Esc cancels) |
| `useToasts.ts` | The messages `Toasts` shows: `push(kind, text)` adds one, which fades out after 4 seconds; one already showing isn't repeated |
| `useTrainer.ts` | Playing a Trainer from the hand: says why it can't be played, asks its questions, and plays it as soon as the last one is answered (Esc cancels). Dropped on the play area, a Trainer with no questions is played at once. One whose first question is a Pokémon (a Pokémon Tool, a Potion…) can be dropped onto one of those Pokémon, highlighted while it's dragged (`dropTargets`), which answers it (`dropOn`) |
| `useRetreat.ts` | Retreating the active: tracks the chosen energy, says why retreat is blocked, and returns the bench slots to switch in and their click handlers (Esc cancels) |

### `src/utils/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `targets.ts` | Where a hand card can be played, why it can't, which action playing it sends, and its target label |
| `slots.ts` | Slot ids (`active`, `bench-N`), converting them to engine targets, empty/occupied slots, and which slot an in-flight move is about |
| `debug.ts` | `DEBUG_BUILD` (whether this build has the debug tools) and `useDebugEnabled` (and whether the API accepts them) |
| `turn.ts` | Whose turn it is, whether you must promote, and the status headline |
| `reminders.ts` | `endTurnReminders`: what End turn reminds you of (an Energy card to attach, a usable Stadium, usable Pokémon Powers) |
| `preview.ts` | Preview size limits, wheel zoom, saving the size in localStorage, and positioning next to the cursor |
| `dragOverlay.ts` | `centerOnPointer`: keeps a dragged card's image centred on the pointer, wherever the card was grabbed |
| `layout.ts` | Shared card widths: small (bench, prizes, piles) and large (active), which scale with the board through CSS variables, the one-card-wide side columns (prizes, deck and discard) so both active slots line up, the page side margins (`PAGE_WIDTH`, undone by `PAGE_BLEED` for the hand), and the fixed sizes of card thumbnails off the board (`CARD_THUMB_XL`…`XS`: deck tiles, deck lists; `RUN_CARD`) |
| `styles.ts` | Class strings of the shared chrome: the agreed dialog style (`DIALOG` and its `DIALOG_BACKDROP`) and its buttons (`BUTTON`, `CHOICE_BUTTON`, `OPTION_BUTTON`, `PAGE_BUTTON`, `ICON_BUTTON`), and the run screen's panel headings (`PANEL_HEADING`, `PANEL_COUNT`: Battles, Events, Battle with, Unlocked this run, Bag in one style, the user's) |
| `themes.ts` | The colour themes (`COLOR_THEMES`, see [Colour themes](#colour-themes)): each a neutral hue and tint and an accent hue, made into Tailwind's `--color-slate-*` and `--color-yellow-50`…`400` variables (`themeVars`) on slate's and yellow's own lightness steps, the chroma kept inside sRGB |
| `accountSettings.ts` | The Settings page's choices kept with the account (see [Account settings](#account-settings)): each setting's store registers how to read its stored value and apply another (`registerSetting`) and reports its changes (`saveSetting`); `connectAccountSettings` (from `AccountSettingsSync`) brings them in line once signed in and sends changes together a moment later |
| `packArt.ts` | `PACK_ART`: each set's real booster wrappers (scans of the English packs from the [Bulbagarden Archives](https://archives.bulbagarden.net), trimmed and resized, in `public/packs/<set>/`): Base Set's Charizard, Blastoise and Venusaur, Jungle's Flareon, Scyther and Wigglytuff, Fossil's Aerodactyl, Lapras and Zapdos, Base Set 2's Gyarados, Mewtwo, Pidgeot and Raichu, Team Rocket's Giovanni, Gyarados, Jessie & James and Team Rocket, Gym Heroes' Brock, Erika and Misty, Gym Challenge's Blaine, Koga and Sabrina (the archive's Lt. Surge and Giovanni packs are a watermarked scan and a low-quality photo, so they're left out; Koga's hanging tab is cropped off). Gen 2's are product photos from TCGplayer and PriceCharting (TCGplayer's 1000px images and PriceCharting's photos; Neo Genesis's Lugia is a Heritage Auctions scan, its backdrop and caption cropped off), as Bulbagarden blocks downloads: Neo Genesis's Lugia and Typhlosion, Neo Discovery's Umbreon, Neo Revelation's Suicune, Neo Destiny's Celebi and Tyranitar, Expedition's Blastoise, Aquapolis's Arcanine and Skyridge's Ho-Oh (some are smaller than 720px tall; Southern Islands had no booster packs, so its pack is drawn). Gen 3's EX sets have all four artworks each, scans from the Bulbagarden Archives (its media URLs download with a plain client): EX Ruby & Sapphire's Lairon, Mightyena, Manectric and Sceptile, EX Sandstorm's Shiftry, Seviper, Zangoose and Dusclops, EX Dragon's Absol, Rayquaza, Latias and Latios, EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua's Sharpedo, Aggron, Houndoom and Walrein, EX Hidden Legends' Metagross, Registeel, Regice and Regirock, EX FireRed & LeafGreen's Charizard, Blastoise, Pikachu and Venusaur, EX Team Rocket Returns' Tyranitar, Mewtwo, Gyarados and Scyther, EX Deoxys's Deoxys (Normal, Defense and Attack Forms) and Rayquaza, EX Emerald's Groudon, Deoxys (Speed), Rayquaza and Kyogre, EX Unseen Forces' Typhlosion, Steelix, Raikou and Umbreon, EX Delta Species' Flareon, Metagross, Salamence and Dragonite, EX Legend Maker's Banette, Arcanine, Mew and Aerodactyl, EX Holon Phantoms' Zangoose, Mewtwo, Kabutops and Gyarados, EX Crystal Guardians' Delcatty, Blaziken, Jirachi and Charizard, EX Dragon Frontiers' Tyranitar, Latias, Salamence and Nidoking and EX Power Keepers' Aggron, Raichu, Walrein and Absol (EX Dragon's Latios, EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua's Aggron, EX Emerald's Kyogre and EX Unseen Forces' Umbreon are shop and listing photos, sharper than the archive's; EX Dragon's other three and EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua's Houndoom and Walrein are small, about 540px and 350px tall). `pickPackArt` picks one per set each time the Packs tab is opened |
| `cardBack.ts` | The card back image (the classic one, in `public/cardbacks/`) and `cardBackUrl`, a card back cosmetic's image |
| `cardColors.ts` | Background colour for each card type |
| `holo.ts` | Maps catalog and engine cards to the attributes the holo styles select on (rarity, supertype, subtypes, era), and a galaxy-art holo's mask (see Holo cards) |
| `holoPreload.ts` | `preloadHolos`: loads the texture images of the holo styles (`TEXTURES`, by rarity) and the holo masks of the account's unlocked holos when the Collection tab opens (`DecksPage`), once a session, so a card's foil is ready when it's first viewed large |
| `spring.ts` | Spring physics for the holo effect's tilt and glare |
| `math.ts` | `round`, `clamp` and `adjust` (re-map a value between ranges) |
| `pageLock.ts` | `lockPage`: stops the page scrolling until released (html's overflow hidden, not body's, which would make body its own scroll box and push the sticky top bar and site menu off the top of a scrolled page; both stop bouncing). Locks are counted, so the page's own settings come back only when the last lock goes (`useScrollLock`, `useLockedScreen`): overlays closed out of order can't leave the page stuck |
| `overworld.ts` | FireRed overworld sprites for the route map (and, for the Johto maps, HeartGold/SoulSilver's: `hgss/<name>`, 12-frame strips of 32x32 with their own right-facing frames, read through `standFrame`, `walkFrame`, `mirrored`, `stripFrames` and `spriteWidth`; for the Hoenn maps Emerald's: `rse/<name>`, 9-frame strips as FireRed's, with Emerald's own sprites for its trainer classes and gym trainers, `RSE_CLASS_SPRITES` and `RSE_GYM_SPRITES`; `playerSprite` is Red, Ethan or Brendan, walking, biking or surfing; `trainerSprite` and `encounterSprite` take the map's sprite set): `trainerSprite` (a trainer's class to its sprite, with FireRed's own stand-ins for classes it has no sprite for: Super Nerds the scientist, Jugglers the man, Gamblers the old man, Psychics the channeler, Burglars the Poké Maniac, Bird Keepers the boy; Swimmers and Rocket Grunts come as men and women, and `waterSprite` is a Swimmer's swimming sprite, in water), `spriteWidth` (32px: the biker, and Red's bike and surfing sprites, `red_bike` and `red_surf`, the latter's frames rearranged into the same 9) and the strips' frames: 9 each, as FireRed's standard animations use them (standing down, up and left, then two walking frames for each; right mirrored), with the walk cycle (`WALK`: step, stand, step, stand, 133ms a frame; a tile every 267ms) |
| `music.ts` | The music player's `Track` type, `playlist` (a generation's songs, or all, game by game with battle music after the rest; auto play's `hidden` ones left out) and `groupByGame` |
| `routeFigures.ts` | Who stands where on the route map: a `RouteFigure` (a step option's trainer or NPC, or the encounter under way's: `encounterFigure`), `placeFigures` (each spot's figures on their own walkable tiles near it, `nearbyTiles`: beside it across the flow first, then ahead, behind, off the lines; Swimmers in the water, everyone else on land; a passed NPC where it walked to) and `facing` |
| `deckBuilder.ts` | Deck builder data: the search filter and sort (`filterForDeck`, `sortForDeck`), adding and removing copies within the rules (`cantAdd`, `changeCount`), the deck's problems, its Pokémon/Trainer/Energy counts (`deckRatio`) and headline Pokémon (`featuredPokemon`) |
| `gridWindow.ts` | The card grid's row windowing (`CardGrid`): the rows to render for a scroll position (`gridWindow`, from the first row whose card still hangs into the viewport, with `OVERSCAN_ROWS` below) and the grid's height (`gridHeight`), from its measured `GridLayout` |
| `cardList.ts` | Catalog card data: the `CatalogCard` type, types, stage labels and set names, `foldText` (searches lower-cased with accents dropped, so "e" finds "é": the card search, Community Decks, the cosmetics' deck picker and the debug card search; agreed 2026-10-23), the name and attack search (`matchesSearch`, each card's folded text kept once it's been searched) and the unlocked/locked filter (`Ownership`, `matchesOwnership`), used by the deck builder |

### `src/styles/holo/`

Holo card effect: layered gradients, blend modes and textures moved by CSS variables that `HoloCard` sets from the pointer position. Each file styles one card type, selected by the card's `data-rarity`, `data-subtypes` and `data-supertype` attributes.

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `index.css` | Imports everything in cascade order and sets the variables' resting values. Imported by `HoloCard.tsx`, so it loads with the first holo card |
| `base.css` | Card structure (3D tilt, front/back, shine and glare layers) and the default glare every card gets |
| `cards.css` | Shared variables: textures, colours and art-window clip shapes measured on modern cards |
| `cards/*.css` | One style per rarity: `regular-holo.css` is the WOTC-era holo and `cosmos-holo.css` the galaxy foil; the others (reverse holo, V, VMAX, rainbow, secret, gallery…) are for later eras |
| `wotc.css` | WOTC tuning: card aspect ratio and art-window clip shapes re-measured on TCGdex scans for the classic (Base–Neo), Trainer, e-Card and EX layouts (EX: the art window, and for Pokémon-ex the border around the frame too, `data-pokemon-ex`), separate stage-badge shapes for Neo, and the galaxy-foil shape around the art (Base Set 2's galaxy holos use the art-window shapes instead, like the holos they reprint); holo Energy is foil across the whole card, every style (Reverse Holo, Galaxy, Radiant…) with none of its shapes masking any of it out (`data-holo-energy`, so the Full Art styles too); glare at half strength |

Holo cards are those with `variants.holo` in their TCGdex data (the rarity alone doesn't say: Base Set holos are listed as "Rare"), plus the promos Venusaur (Wizards Promo 13) and Cool Porygon (15), holofoils TCGdex lists as normal only (`TCGDEX_MISSING_HOLO` in the campaign's `card-fixes.ts`). The API passes it on as `holo` in `cards.list` and in the engine's card definitions. Galaxy-foil cards (the Entei and Pichu promos and the Southern Islands set) have no TCGdex flag, so `isGalaxyFoil` in the campaign's `card-fixes.ts` lists them. Base Set 2's 20 holo rares are shown with the galaxy foil too, to set them apart from the Base Set and Jungle holos they reprint, in the art window where those have their holo; `cards.list` returns it as `galaxy`. Every holo Pokémon of Gen 1's and Gen 2's sets (`GALAXY_ART_SETS` in `card-fixes.ts`: Base Set to Skyridge; not holo Trainers or Energy) is shown the same way (asked by the user), its Pokémon masked out of the foil so it stands in front of it. The web tells it apart from the galaxy foil around the art (on reverse-only prints) by `holo` (`galaxyArt` in `utils/holo.ts`, `data-galaxy-art`, which picks the art-window clip in `wotc.css`). Each has a mask image (`public/holo-masks/<card id>.png`, 600×825, opaque where the foil shows, transparent over the Pokémon) that `holoAttributes` gives `HoloCard` as `attrs.mask` (the library's `.masked` rule masks the shine, and `wotc.css` the glare). The masks are made by `tools/holo-masks/make_masks.py --sets …`, which cuts out the art window (classic or e-Card) and runs a background-removal model on it: rembg's `birefnet-massive`, or for the cards in its `OVERRIDES` another model that cut them better. With `--compare` it also runs a second model and reports the cards where the two disagree or the Pokémon's share of the art is implausible; those were checked by eye in its `--preview` images (the foil area tinted), the rest weren't. Of the 254 (2.6 MB), 203 are BiRefNet massive's, 38 `birefnet-general`'s and 2 `u2net`'s; 11 close-ups and full-scene arts that no model could cut out (most of them Expedition's and Aquapolis') are `none`: their mask is opaque, so the foil covers the whole art window, as a classic holo's does.

### `src/lib/`

| File | Description |
|---|---|
| `trpc.ts` | Query client and typed tRPC client for the API: `cards.list` alone through its own link (`splitLink`, a GET with no Authorization header, so it's cached), everything else batched |
| `supabase.ts` | Supabase client and Google sign-in/sign-out |
| `auth.ts` | `useSession` hook for the current Supabase session |

## Game engine (`packages/game-engine/src/`)

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `engine.ts` | State types and the `applyAction` reducer: setup, playing cards, retreat, turn flow (Pokémon Checkup between turns, knockouts, prizes, promotions). `cloneState` copies a state for a change but its card library, which every state of a game shares (nothing changes it after `createGame`; see Performance) |
| `attacks.ts` | Attack resolution: legality (`attackError`), the questions an attack asks (`nextAttackChoice`), weakness/resistance and prevention, and the context card effects run with |
| `powers.ts` | Pokémon Powers: legality (`powerError`), their questions (`nextPowerChoice`) and `usePower`; whether a power works (`powerActive`: Special Conditions and Toxic Gas), Prehistoric Power (`evolutionBlocked`), Clairvoyance (`seesOpponentHand`) and Ditto's Transform (`refreshTransforms`, run on every action) |
| `trainers.ts` | Trainer cards: legality (`trainerError`, including Headache's lock and Pokémon Tower), their questions (`nextTrainerChoice`), `playTrainer`, Stadiums (`stadiumError`, `pokemonTower`), and pending choices only the choosing player sees (`PendingChoice`, resolved with `RESOLVE_CHOICE`; attacks and powers start them too). A choice can belong to the player whose turn it isn't (Computer Error, Chain Reaction): the CPU answers its own first, and the game waits for the player's |
| `effects/` | Card-specific effects by card id: attacks (`base1.ts` for the Base Set, `jungle.ts` for Jungle, `fossil.ts` for Fossil, from shared pieces in `common.ts`), Pokémon Powers (`base1-powers.ts`, `jungle-powers.ts`, `fossil-powers.ts`) and Trainers (`base1-trainers.ts`, `jungle-trainers.ts`, `fossil-trainers.ts`, `neo1-trainers.ts`), and promo cards (`promos.ts` attacks, `promos-powers.ts`, `promos-trainers.ts`). Team Rocket's attacks are in `rocket.ts`, its powers in `rocket-powers.ts`, and its Trainers and special Energy (`ROCKET_ENERGY`, looked up with `energyEffect`) in `rocket-trainers.ts`. Base Set 2's attacks, powers and Trainers all come from its reprint table (`base4.ts`), and Legendary Collection's from its own (`lc.ts`, tested in `lc.test.ts`). `test-kit.ts` sets up duels for their tests, and checks a choice always terminates: `settles` follows every legal answer to a pending choice (`choiceAnswers`), then the last answer accepted (nothing, where allowed) of each question that follows, whoever it's for, and fails if a path is still asking after a dozen answers or no answer is accepted; `playAuto` plays a Trainer with the first answer to each of its own questions (`jungle.test.ts`, `jungle-powers.test.ts` and `jungle-trainers.test.ts` cover Jungle; `fossil.test.ts`, `fossil-powers.test.ts` and `fossil-trainers.test.ts` cover Fossil; `promos.test.ts`, `promos-powers.test.ts` and `promos-trainers.test.ts` the promos and Super Energy Retrieval; `rocket.test.ts`, `rocket-powers.test.ts` and `rocket-trainers.test.ts` Team Rocket). Gym Heroes is in `gym1.ts`, `gym1-powers.ts` and `gym1-trainers.ts` (tests: `gym1.test.ts`, `gym1-trainers.test.ts`, `gym1-extras.test.ts`), Gym Challenge in `gym2.ts`, `gym2-powers.ts` and `gym2-trainers.ts` (tests: `gym2.test.ts`), Neo Genesis in `neo1.ts`, `neo1-powers.ts` and `neo1-trainers.ts` (with its special Energy, `NEO1_ENERGY`; tests: `neo1.test.ts`, `neo1-powers.test.ts`, `neo1-trainers.test.ts`), Neo Discovery in `neo2.ts`, `neo2-powers.ts` and `neo2-trainers.ts`, with `neo2-prints.ts` for its non-holo prints of the holo rares (tests: `neo2.test.ts`, `neo2-powers.test.ts`, `neo2-trainers.test.ts`), Southern Islands in `si1.ts` (tests: `si1.test.ts`), Neo Revelation in `neo3.ts`, `neo3-powers.ts` and `neo3-trainers.ts` (tests: `neo3.test.ts`, `neo3-powers.test.ts`, `neo3-trainers.test.ts`), Neo Destiny in `neo4.ts`, `neo4-powers.ts` and `neo4-trainers.ts` (with Miracle Energy, `NEO4_ENERGY`; tests: `neo4.test.ts`, `neo4-powers.test.ts`, `neo4-trainers.test.ts`), Expedition in `ecard1.ts`, `ecard1-powers.ts` and `ecard1-trainers.ts` (`ecard1-prints.ts`: its non-holo prints of the holo rares; tests: `ecard1.test.ts`, `ecard1-powers.test.ts`, `ecard1-trainers.test.ts`), Aquapolis in `ecard2.ts`, `ecard2-powers.ts` and `ecard2-trainers.ts` (with its special Energy, `ECARD2_ENERGY`; tests: `ecard2.test.ts`), Skyridge in `ecard3.ts`, `ecard3-powers.ts` and `ecard3-trainers.ts` (with its special Energy, `ECARD3_ENERGY`; tests: `ecard3.test.ts`). The later EX sets' attacks that share a wording are played by `ex-templates.ts` (each wording and its building block), from `ex-template-data.ts`, which `packages/game-engine/scripts/generate-ex-templates.ts` writes from the snapshot (tests: `ex-templates.test.ts`; see Shared EX wordings). EX Ruby & Sapphire is in `ex1.ts`, `ex1-powers.ts` and `ex1-trainers.ts` (tests: `ex1.test.ts`), EX Sandstorm in `ex2.ts`, `ex2-powers.ts` and `ex2-trainers.ts` (tests: `ex2.test.ts`), EX Dragon in `ex3.ts`, `ex3-powers.ts` and `ex3-trainers.ts` (tests: `ex3.test.ts`), EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua in `ex4.ts`, `ex4-powers.ts` and `ex4-trainers.ts` (with Aqua, Magma and Double Rainbow Energy, `EX4_ENERGY`; tests: `ex4.test.ts`), EX Hidden Legends in `ex5.ts`, `ex5-powers.ts` and `ex5-trainers.ts` (tests: `ex5.test.ts`), EX FireRed & LeafGreen in `ex6.ts`, `ex6-powers.ts` and `ex6-trainers.ts` (with Multi Energy, `EX6_ENERGY`; tests: `ex6.test.ts`), EX Team Rocket Returns in `ex7.ts`, `ex7-powers.ts` and `ex7-trainers.ts` (with Dark Metal and R Energy, `EX7_ENERGY`; tests: `ex7.test.ts`), EX Deoxys in `ex8.ts`, `ex8-powers.ts` and `ex8-trainers.ts` (`EX8_ENERGY`; tests: `ex8.test.ts`), EX Emerald in `ex9.ts`, `ex9-powers.ts` and `ex9-trainers.ts` (`EX9_ENERGY`; tests: `ex9.test.ts`), EX Unseen Forces in `ex10.ts`, `ex10-powers.ts` and `ex10-trainers.ts` (`EX10_ENERGY`; tests: `ex10.test.ts`), EX Delta Species in `ex11.ts`, `ex11-powers.ts` and `ex11-trainers.ts` (with the Holon Energy cards, `EX11_ENERGY`; tests: `ex11.test.ts`), EX Legend Maker in `ex12.ts`, `ex12-powers.ts` and `ex12-trainers.ts` (with React Energy, `EX12_ENERGY`; tests: `ex12.test.ts`), EX Holon Phantoms in `ex13.ts`, `ex13-powers.ts` and `ex13-trainers.ts` (with δ Rainbow Energy, `EX13_ENERGY`; tests: `ex13.test.ts`), EX Crystal Guardians in `ex14.ts`, `ex14-powers.ts` and `ex14-trainers.ts` (`EX14_ENERGY`; tests: `ex14.test.ts`), EX Dragon Frontiers in `ex15.ts`, `ex15-powers.ts` and `ex15-trainers.ts` (`EX15_ENERGY`; tests: `ex15.test.ts`), EX Power Keepers in `ex16.ts`, `ex16-powers.ts` and `ex16-trainers.ts` (`EX16_ENERGY`; tests: `ex16.test.ts`) |
| `highlight.ts` | `moveHighlights`: the card each of a player's moves played or used and the Pokémon it changed, for the board to show the CPU's moves (tests in `highlight.test.ts`) |
| `status.ts` | Battle log, coin flips, clearing conditions when a Pokémon leaves the active spot, and the card an in-play Pokémon counts as (`inPlayDef`, `effectId`: a Transformed Ditto's copy) |
| `view.ts` | `getPlayerView`: what one player may see, and on their turn `attackDamage`: what each of their Active Pokémon's attacks would do to the Defending Pokémon now (`attackDamageRanges` in `cpu/shared.ts`, played on copies of the game like the CPU's attack choice; worked out once per state, however many times the state is viewed) |
| `cpu/index.ts` | CPU difficulties (`CPU_DIFFICULTIES`, `cpuFor`) and `runCpu`, which plays at the game's `cpuDifficulty` |
| `cpu/passive.ts` | The **Passive** CPU (`passiveCpu`): benches, evolves and attaches matching Energy; never attacks, retreats or plays Trainers |
| `cpu/trainer.ts` | The **Trainer** CPU (`trainerCpu`): a simpler gym leader for the other fights, with its short list of Trainers and special Energy (`TRAINER_CPU_PLAYS`, `TRAINER_CPU_ENERGY`) |
| `cpu/gym-leader.ts` | The **Gym leader** CPU (`gymLeaderCpu`): its rules, step by step |
| `cpu/evaluate.ts` | The generic judgement for cards with no rule of their own: moves tried on imagined copies of the state and the position scored (`positionScore`, `bestMove`, `attackBoost`, `survive`, `specialAttachment`); tests in `evaluate.test.ts` |
| `cpu/shared.ts` | What the CPUs share: the turn context and common moves (benching, evolving, aiming Energy, Switch or retreat, finishing a search), choosing the first active, each attack simulated over every coin-flip outcome (`attackOptions`, remembered per state for the length of one decision: `withAttackMemo`, which each CPU's decision runs inside), the opponent's threat next turn, Energy arithmetic and which cards are useful |
| `cpu/*.test.ts` | Tests per difficulty (`gym-leader.test.ts` has one block per rule, with Base Set cards); `choices.test.ts`: every difficulty answers a question with nothing to click with nothing, and picks one where there is something |
| `debug.ts` | `applyDebug`: testing cheats (a card into the hand, draw, set a Pokémon's HP). Not `GameAction`s, so normal moves can never reach them; each is logged |
| `checkup.test.ts` | `captureCheckup`: the state after a move and before its turn's Pokémon Checkup, and as the checkup ended (a Berry's heal logged with its Pokémon and amount, before the next turn) |
| `rules.test.ts` | Battle rules (`GameState.rules`: type damage, and items' damage bonus and guard, condition guard, opening hand, lucky flips) and `cardImplemented` |
| `view.test.ts` | `getPlayerView`: the RNG, decks and the opponent's hand never shown; Secret Plan's face-down card hidden from the opponent; a pending choice shown only to its player; Clairvoyance's hand; Here Comes Team Rocket!'s Prize cards; `attackDamage` for the current player only |
| `affliction.test.ts` | `afflictionBlocked`, one test per blocker (Clefairy Doll, Thick Skinned, Flash Touch, Pollen Shield, an attack's conditionProof, a conditionGuard rule), and Sleep! going through it |
| `hooks.test.ts` | The hooks `applyAction` runs after an accepted action: Energy Evolution (an Energy card attached or moved by any effect of Eevee's owner) and Triggered Poison |
| `effects/choices.test.ts` | Every kind of pending choice terminates: `settles` (test-kit.ts) follows every answer to the choices Trainers, attacks and powers start, the ones that go on to another question or may be declined above all |
| `effects/dual-types.test.ts` | Pokémon with two types: Weakness and Resistance by either type, Herbal Scent, Wild Growth and Cinnabar City Gym |

Rules follow the 1999 Base Set rulebook. Turn order: `createGame` flips a coin for the first player (the human): heads they go first, tails the opponent does (`TURN_ORDER_FLIP`, logged with "Goes first."). Setup: each player puts a Basic Pokémon in the active spot and any Basic Pokémon cards on the Bench, hidden from the opponent (the log doesn't name them), can take them all back (`RESET_SETUP`), and presses Start battle (`START_BATTLE`, `PlayerState.ready`) when done; Clefairy Doll can't be placed then. Once both have started, the Pokémon are revealed in the log and whoever won the flip takes turn 1 (and can't attack on it); when that's the CPU, it plays its turn as soon as the player has started. The CPU places only its active and starts at once (it benches on its first turn). Special Conditions: Asleep (flip between turns to wake), Confused (flip to attack, tails = 30 damage to itself; it retreats with no flip: the modern rule, agreed for every set), Paralyzed (until after its owner's next turn), Poisoned (10 between turns, 20 from Toxic), Burned (20 between turns, then a flip: heads cures it; no Base Set, Jungle or Fossil card Burns, but the promo Rapidash's Super Singe does). Poisoned and Burned are separate from each other and from Asleep, Confused and Paralyzed, which replace one another; only the active can have any of them, and benching, evolving and Full Heal remove them all. The checkup between turns handles them in order for both actives: Poisoned, Burned, Asleep, Paralyzed (tests in `burned.test.ts`). Trainers: PlusPower (+10) and Defender (−20) attach to a Pokémon and change attack damage after weakness and resistance, PlusPower first; they're discarded at the end of the turn / the opponent's next turn. Clefairy Doll is played onto the Bench as a 10 HP Pokémon (it can't start as your Active or count as a Basic for the opening hand), can't retreat or get Special Conditions, gives no prize when Knocked Out, and can be discarded during your turn. Scoop Up can't take your only Pokémon in play; Devolution Spray can Knock Out. Double Colorless Energy provides 2 Colorless energy. Pokémon Powers stop working while their Pokémon has any Special Condition, Poisoned and Burned included (agreed, also for the older cards printed "Asleep, Confused, or Paralyzed"; `hasSpecialCondition`), unless they say they work anyway; the attack-copying powers and bodies (Psylink, Mimic, Psymimic, Fellowship, Versatile) are checked with `powerActive` in `usableAttacks` like the rest. Whether a power, body or Trainer card may give a Pokémon a Special Condition is one check, `afflictionBlocked` (engine.ts: Clefairy Doll, Thick Skinned, Flash Touch, Pollen Shield, a run's `conditionGuard` rule…), the same one attacks' `inflict` makes. Dropping a Water Energy on a Water Pokémon uses Rain Dance when a Blastoise can, so it doesn't use up the turn's attachment. Where TCGdex's attack text differs from the printed card, the effect carries the printed wording and the API shows that instead.

Team Rocket (`effects/rocket.ts`, tests in `rocket.test.ts`): every card is done: attacks, Pokémon Powers, Trainers and special Energy, and the set is in the run pool and has a pack. CPU decks (`generateDeck`) can use its Pokémon; the gym leader CPU plays them through its generic judgement (see The CPU opponent). New mechanics: Stare switches off a Pokémon Power until the end of the opponent's next turn (`InPlayPokemon.powerOffUntil`, wherever it goes); Mirror Shell does the damage Dark Wartortle takes back to the attacker, with nothing applied to it; the opponent flips for Knock Down and Bench Manipulation (`ctx.oppFlip`); Fling and Vanish shuffle a Pokémon into its owner's deck (with nothing left in play after Vanish, its player loses); Energy Bomb puts each Energy card on a benched Pokémon the player clicks; Continuous Fireball's discards are the player's choice; Third Eye draws up to 3 (the player picks how many); Rapid Evolution evolves Magikarp from the deck with no evolution limits; Teleport Blast and Flame Pillar have "you may" choices (Don't switch; discard up to 1). TCGdex drops Energy symbols (Afternoon Nap, Fireball, Playing with Fire, Ember, Flame Pillar, Water Gun) and has Dark Magneton's attacks wrong on both prints; the effects carry the printed `text`, `damage` and `cost`. TCGdex also says the Dark Stage 2 Pokémon evolve from the plain Stage 1, so `apps/api/src/cards.ts` makes them evolve from the Dark one (Dark Alakazam from Dark Kadabra).

Team Rocket Pokémon Powers (`effects/rocket-powers.ts`): Summon Minions, Sneak Attack and Reel In work when their card is played from the hand, by evolving or with Pokémon Breeder (`onPlayFromHand`, called by `playedFromHand`): Summon Minions benches up to 2 Basic Pokémon from the deck (`pickFromDeck` with `count` and `optional`); Sneak Attack lets the player click one of the opponent's Pokémon on the board for 10 damage, with Weakness and Resistance, or skip it (the `oppPokemon` pending choice); Reel In takes up to 3 Pokémon cards from the discard pile (not with Pokémon Tower in play). Hay Fever stops both players playing Trainers (`trainersBlocked`). Sinkhole makes the opponent flip whenever their Active Pokémon retreats, 20 damage on tails. Sticky Goo adds 2 to the opposing Active Pokémon's retreat cost while Dark Muk is Active. Final Beam flips when an attack Knocks Out Dark Gyarados; heads does 20 per Water Energy on it to the attacker, with Weakness and Resistance. Frenzy works while Dark Primeape is Confused (`ignoresConditions`) and adds 30 to any damage it does, before Weakness and Resistance, so Confusion's self-damage is 50. Evolutionary Light, Pollen Stench, Matter Exchange, Gather Fire, Long Distance Hypnosis and Trickery are used once a turn; Trickery switches the Prize the player picks (face down, by position) with the top card of the deck. A power that takes a hand card but doesn't put it onto a Pokémon (Matter Exchange) is never used by dropping the card on a Pokémon.

Team Rocket Trainers and special Energy (`effects/rocket-trainers.ts`): Here Comes Team Rocket! turns both players' Prize cards face up for the rest of the game (`GameState.prizesFaceUp`; the view sends `prizes` for both sides). Rocket's Sneak Attack shows the opponent's whole hand in a dialog (the `oppHand` pending view) and the player clicks one of its Trainer cards for them to shuffle into their deck, or Done when there are none. The Boss's Way finds an Evolution card with Dark in its name. Challenge! asks the opponent (the `challenge` pending choice, answered with count 1 or 0); the CPU accepts whenever its Bench has room and its deck has a Basic Pokémon (agreed). Accepted, the player who played it benches any number of Basic Pokémon from the deck, then the opponent does (`challengeSearch`, `pickFromDeck` with `then`); declined, or with both Benches full, the player draws 2. Digger passes the flip back and forth until someone gets tails and takes 10 on their Active Pokémon. Imposter Oak's Revenge discards a card from the hand; the opponent shuffles their hand away and draws 4. Nightly Garbage Run shuffles up to 3 Pokémon or basic Energy cards from the discard pile into the deck. Goop Gas Attack stops every Pokémon Power on both sides until the end of the opponent's next turn (`PlayerState.powersOffUntil`, checked in `powerActive` and `powerError`). Sleep! puts the Defending Pokémon to sleep on heads. Rainbow Energy (`anyType` on the card, `'any'` in an `energyPool`) counts as every type while attached, one at a time: it pays any cost (`payCost`, which uses it for a typed symbol only when nothing else can), counts for effects about Energy of a type (Final Beam, Hydro Pump), and is a Fire (or any) Energy card for discards and moves (`isEnergyCardOf`: Ember, Fireball, Energy Trans, Gather Fire). Attached from the hand it does 10 damage to its Pokémon, which can Knock it Out. Full Heal Energy cures Asleep, Confused, Paralyzed and Poisoned, and Potion Energy removes a damage counter, when attached from the hand; both provide Colorless. Attaching Energy, and resolving a choice during the turn (Sneak Attack), now check for Knock Outs. The gym leader CPU plays most of them (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)).

Gym Heroes (`effects/gym1.ts` attacks, `gym1-powers.ts`, `gym1-trainers.ts`; tests in `gym1.test.ts`, `gym1-trainers.test.ts` and `gym1-extras.test.ts`): every card is done, and the set is in the run pool and has a pack. CPU decks (`generateDeck`) can use its Pokémon and the Trainers the gym leader plays (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)). An owner's Pokémon (Brock's Rhyhorn) evolves only into one with the same owner, and a plain Pokémon never evolves from an owner's: TCGdex has the plain names, so `apps/api/src/cards.ts` gives each owner's Stage 1 and Stage 2 its owner (`OWNER`; Lt. Surge's Magneton's missing "Magnemite" too), and the engine's name check does the rest. TCGdex leaves out the damage of Lariat, Horn Hazard, Child's Punch, Egg Bomb, Drill Tackle, Razor Wind and Clamp; the effects carry the printed `damage`. New mechanics: `hit` takes `resistance: false` (Hook Shot, and Pewter City Gym for Pokémon with Brock in their names) and `swift` (Swift: nothing on the Defending Pokémon changes the damage); Suggestion and Tunneling stop attacks (`effects.noAttack`), Crystal Beam stops Energy being attached (`noEnergy`), Shadow Images makes the attacker flip until Rocket's Scyther takes damage (`shadowImages`), Fire Wall does 10 back with Weakness and Resistance (`fireWall`), Focus Energy doubles Gnaw (`focusEnergy`), Deflector is Light Screen. Magic Pollen asks for a condition (the `condition` attack choice). Fairy Power returns any number of the player's Pokémon, clicked on the board, with a Done button (`returnOwn`); Take Away, Call of the Night and Phoenix Flame shuffle Pokémon into their decks; Charge takes Lightning Energy from the discard pile; Sleight of Hand puts up to 3 hand cards on the deck (`sleightOfHand`), then takes as many basic Energy cards; Jellyfish Pod and Call for Friend search by name (`pickFromDeck` filters `named`, `ownerBasic`, `ownerPokemon`). Pain Amplifier puts damage counters, which aren't damage. Eggsplosion counts Energy provided, as Big Eggsplosion does.

Gym Heroes cards that ask during play (agreed rulings): Bench Guard asks, once an attack is over, for each other benched Pokémon it damaged, whether to move 10 of that damage to the benched Brock's Rhydon (the CPU does unless that Knocks Rhydon Out). These questions wait in `GameState.queuedChoices` behind the attack's own choice, and the turn ends after the last. Flee is asked of the Defending player before the attack (the `flee` pending choice; the attack waits in it): they click the benched Pokémon to switch with, or Done; if the attack then damages Misty's Tentacruel, it takes the damage, nothing else the attack does affects it, and it's switched once the attack is over (the CPU flees with 40 HP or less left). Crosscounter's double damage back is an attack by Rocket's Hitmonchan, so Weakness and Resistance apply. Celadon City Gym is used by clicking the Stadium, any number of times a turn (`stadiumAnyTimes`), while your Active Pokémon with Erika in its name has an Energy card and a condition to cure (`stadiumBlocked`; `stadiumError` takes the player). Vermilion City Gym adds a question to every attack by a Pokémon with Lt. Surge in its name (the `vermilion` attack choice: Flip or Don't flip); the CPU always flips. Charity stays on the Active Pokémon until the end of the turn, then goes back to the hand (`attached.toHand`), and adds a question to its attacks: the most damage to do to the Defending Pokémon (the `charity` choice, `damageCap`; the CPU never holds back). Recall lets the Active Pokémon use the attacks of the cards under it this turn (`PlayerState.recallTurn`; `usableAttacks` lists them after its own, by attack index, and the attack list shows their card's name). Sabrina's ESP: after an attack by its Pokémon flips coins, the player may re-flip them all once (the `reflip` choice keeps the state from before the attack and plays it again with new flips; the log keeps both). Narrow Gym limits Benches to 4 (`openBenchSlot` and `benchRoom` in status.ts, used wherever a Pokémon is put onto a Bench, the CPUs included); played, each player with 5 benched returns 1 to their hand, the opponent first (`narrowGym`). Blaine's Quiz #1: the player picks a Pokémon card from the hand; the opponent picks its length from 4 (`quiz`, the `options` view; the CPU guesses at random). Lengths aren't in TCGdex, so the API sets `length` on Pokémon from their Pokédex number (`apps/api/src/lengths.ts`: heights from PokeAPI, rounded to the inch as the cards print them).

Gym Heroes Pokémon Powers (`effects/gym1-powers.ts`): Pollen Defense flips when an attack damages the Active Erika's Vileplume and confuses the attacker on heads; Restless Sleep does 20 to an attacker while Rocket's Snorlax is Asleep (both work whatever its condition). Shell Armor takes 10 off attack damage and Strange Barrier makes a Basic Pokémon's attack of 20 or more do 10 (`barriers` in attacks.ts, for benched damage too). Photosynthesis makes Erika's Oddish's Energy all Grass in `energyPool` (Stare stops it; Toxic Gas and Goop Gas Attack don't, as `energyPool` sees no other Pokémon). Rebirth returns a Knocked Out Rocket's Moltres to the hand unless its power wasn't working (`rebirth`, in `resolveKnockouts`). Energy Charge moves Lightning Energy cards to the Active Lt. Surge's Magneton as often as the player likes; Fragrance Trap (once a turn) switches in the opponent's benched Pokémon chosen, on heads; Natural Healing removes a damage counter once a turn. Shell Armor and Rebirth ("you may") are always used.

Gym Heroes Trainers (`effects/gym1-trainers.ts`): Brock, Erika (up to 3 for each player, as Computer Error), Lt. Surge (a Basic Pokémon from the hand becomes the Active Pokémon), Misty (discard 2; 20 more damage this turn from Pokémon with Misty in their names, `PlayerState.misty`), The Rocket's Trap, Blaine's Last Resort, Brock's Training Method, Erika's Maids (the 2 cards traded go into the deck once the search is over, `tradeAway`), Erika's Perfume (the opponent's hand in a dialog; any number of its Basic Pokémon onto their Bench, `oppHandBasics`), Good Manners, Lt. Surge's Treaty (the opponent picks from labelled buttons, the `options` pending view; the CPU takes the Prizes unless it has more left), Minion of Team Rocket and Tickling Machine (both can end the turn: `TrainerContext.endTurn`; Tickling Machine sets the hand aside in `PlayerState.setAside` until the end of the opponent's next turn), Misty's Wrath (`pickFromTop`), Secret Mission (the opponent's hand, then discard any number and draw as many: `secretMission`, `discardDraw`), Blaine's Gamble, Energy Flow, Misty's Duel (the coin flip the card allows decides the winner), Sabrina's Gaze and Trash Exchange. Stadiums: The Rocket's Training Gym and Cerulean City Gym change retreat costs (`retreatModifier`, so `retreatCost` and `retreatError` take the Stadium), Pewter City Gym turns off Resistance for Brock's Pokémon, and No Removal Gym makes Energy Removal and Super Energy Removal ask for 2 other cards from the hand to discard (`removalTax` in trainers.ts). The gym leader CPU plays most of them (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)).
Gym Challenge (`effects/gym2.ts` attacks, `gym2-powers.ts`, `gym2-trainers.ts`; tests in `gym2.test.ts`): every card is done, and the set is in the run pool and has a pack. CPU decks (`generateDeck`) can use its Pokémon and the Trainers the gym leader plays (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)). Its owners' Pokémon evolve as Gym Heroes' do; TCGdex's "Nidoran♀"/"Nidoran♂" become "Giovanni's Nidoran ♀"/"♂", as the cards are named. TCGdex leaves out 13 attacks' damage; the effects carry the printed `damage`. Agreed rulings: Rocket's Minefield Gym puts 2 damage counters on tails (the printed card leaves out the number); Chaos Gym's tails lets the opponent play the card; Blaine's Quiz #3 offers 4 names (the CPU guesses at random); Brock's Ninetales and Lt. Surge's Secret Plan have their full rules.

Gym Challenge mechanics: Shapeshift attaches an Evolution card from the hand to Brock's Ninetales once a turn (an `attached` entry with `shapeshift`, read by `shapeshiftCard`); `inPlayDef` treats it as that card with its own power, `usableAttacks` uses that card's attacks, it can't evolve, and clicking it discards the card (`DISCARD_SHAPESHIFT`), as do Asleep, Confused and Paralyzed (`dropShapeshifts`). Lt. Surge's Secret Plan puts a hand card face down onto the Bench (`InPlayPokemon.faceDown`): it's `FACE_DOWN` (a Basic Pokémon with no attacks) for both players, the opponent's view hides its card, and it's turned over (`revealFaceDown`, discarded if it isn't a Basic Pokémon) when it's attacked or damaged, evolved, retreated, or clicked by its owner (`FLIP_FACE_DOWN`). Chaos Gym flips for every other Trainer: on tails the card goes to the opponent's hand for the moment (`GameState.borrowed`, the `borrowedTrainer` choice, whose view says it's `playable`) and they may play it as theirs (the CPU does if it's the first thing it would do with that card; `borrowedTrainerMove`), then it goes to its owner's discard pile. Sabrina's Psychic Control borrows a Trainer from the opponent's discard pile the same way until the end of the turn. Koga's Ninja Trick asks the Defending player before the attack, like Flee (`preAttackQuestion`), and is discarded once its Pokémon is benched (`activeOnly`). Psylink lists the benched Psychic Pokémon's attacks after Sabrina's Alakazam's own (`usableAttacks` takes the Bench). Fortitude, Relaxing Scent, Rebellion, Scram, Shock Blast and Energy Drain react to attacks; Gaseous Form and Giant Growth change HP in `inPlayDef`. Lie Low and Barrier Attack reduce damage (`effects.reduceDamage`), Earthdrill checks `effects.lastAttack`, Hyper Needle and Ancestral Memory are used once while in play (`effects.usedOnce`, kept when benched), Ink Spurt needs heads until it's benched. Attack questions added: `eitherDiscard` (Invigorate), `oppDiscard` (Pranks), `amount` (Helping Hand, Group Attack). Trainers: Blaine allows a second Fire Energy card onto the same Blaine's Pokémon (`blaineTurn`, `blaineAttach`), Giovanni lifts the evolving limits for one Pokémon (`giovanniTurn`), Koga poisons on damage (`kogaTurn`), Sabrina moves all Energy between two Sabrina's Pokémon (`TrainerChoices.target2`), Brock's Protection stops the opponent's attacks and Trainers removing Energy (`energyProtected`; attacks that discard it check `energyCanGo`, attacks that move it to another Pokémon, the hand or the deck check `energyCanMove` in `effects/common.ts`), Transparent Walls prevents damage to the Bench (`benchDamageProtectedUntil`), Warp Point queues both players' switches. Stadiums: Resistance Gym (20 less Resistance) and Cinnabar City Gym (no Weakness for Water on Blaine's) in `applyWeaknessResistance`; Rocket's Minefield Gym in PLAY_BENCH; Viridian City Gym on every evolution (`evolvedHook`); Fuchsia City Gym (once a turn) and Saffron City Gym (any number of times) are used by clicking the Stadium. Misty's Wish, Blaine's Quiz #2 (`guess`), Master Ball (`pickFromTop` with `master`), Max Revive, Misty's Tears, Rocket's Secret Experiment, Erika's Kindness, Giovanni's Last Resort and Fervor have their own steps. The gym leader CPU plays most of them (see [The CPU opponent](#the-cpu-opponent)).
Neo Genesis (`effects/neo1.ts` attacks; tests in `neo1.test.ts`): every Pokémon attack, Pokémon Power, Trainer and special Energy is done (`neo1-powers.ts`, `neo1-trainers.ts`; tests in `neo1-powers.test.ts`, `neo1-trainers.test.ts`), and its packs and cards belong to Gen 2 only: Johto runs and Neo Genesis packs, never Kanto runs' pool (agreed; a player's own deck holding Neo Genesis cards can still start any run). The Baby Pokémon (Pichu, Cleffa, Elekid, Magby) are flagged `baby` by the API, as TCGdex lists them as Basic (`BABIES` in `apps/api/src/cards.ts`). Agreed rulings: Pilfer can be used even if Aipom is the only Pokémon in play (its player then loses); Static Electricity counts every Mareep in play, both players', and the Energy all go to the attacking Mareep; Jaw Clamp and Sticky Nectar stop any effect, whoever's, from changing the held player's Active Pokémon (`activeLock`/`activeLocked` in engine.ts, checked by Switch, Gust of Wind, Scoop Up, Step In, Cowardice, `switchOwn`, `switchDefender`, `shuffleIntoDeck`, Star Boomerang and the `switchIn`, `returnOwn` and `fuchsia` choices) and it can't retreat. The printed cards were checked where TCGdex drops an Energy symbol (Elemental Blast: a Fire, a Water and a Lightning; Static Electricity: Lightning; Slowpoke's Water Gun: Water).

Neo Genesis mechanics (`PokemonEffects`): Screech (`screech`: attacks that damage it do 20 more, after Weakness and Resistance, through the attacker's next turn); Endure (`endure`: an attack that would Knock it Out leaves it with 10 HP, as Fortitude does); Pollen Shield (`pollenShield`: no Asleep, Confused, Paralyzed or Poisoned from attacks); Freeze (`frozen`) and Spider Web (`stuck`): no attacking, or no retreating, until it's benched or evolves; Mean Look, Jaw Clamp and Sticky Nectar (`heldBy`: no retreating while the Pokémon that held it is the opponent's Active, `heldActive`). A Char counter (`InPlayPokemon.charCounter`) stays while the Pokémon is in play, Active or Benched: after every turn its owner flips, and tails puts 2 damage counters on it (`betweenTurns`). Sputter switches every Pokémon Power off through the attacker's next turn (`powersOffUntil`, as Goop Gas Attack). Super Metronome copies an attack of any of the opponent's Pokémon: a `copySource` question (answered in `copyFrom`), then `copyAttack` with `of`. Sunkern's Growth attaches any Energy cards (`attachFromHand` without a type).

Neo Genesis Pokémon Powers: Berserk, Herbal Scent and Fire Boost work when the card is played from the hand (`onPlayFromHand`); Herbal Scent and Fire Boost ask whether to flip first (the `mayFlip` pending choice, labelled buttons), Fire Boost then searching with `pickFromDeck`'s `attachTo`. Mind Games (agreed): whenever a player plays a Trainer card (Stadiums and Pokémon Tools too) and the opponent has a Slowking with it working, the card waits (`mindGames`, a pending choice for the opponent, `mindGamesFlips`): they flip, one flip per Slowking until one is heads, which puts the card on top of its owner's deck, or let it be played (the engine plays it then, with `GameState.mindGamesPassed`); the CPU always flips. Wild Growth (`wildGrowth`, refreshed after every action: `refreshWildGrowth`) makes `energyPool` count each basic Grass Energy card on its owner's Grass Pokémon twice; two don't stack (agreed). Final Blow and Hydroelectric Power are asked before Megahorn and Floodlight (agreed), a `boost` attack question shown only when the power is working (`AttackSituation.powersOff` for Goop Gas Attack and Sputter); a "yes" sent when it wasn't asked does nothing. Playful Punch ends the turn (`endTurnNow`, which `USE_POWER` now handles as Trainers do, `afterTrainer`); Glaring Gaze uses Sneak Attack's `oppHandTrainer`; Fire Recharge and Downpour need the discard pile and hand (`PowerSituation.me.discard`). A power's `text` overrides TCGdex's wording, as attacks' does (Downpour).

Neo Genesis Trainers (`neo1-trainers.ts`): Pokémon Tools (Focus Band, Berry, Gold Berry, Miracle Berry) are `attached` entries with `tool`, one per Pokémon (`toolOf`, `discardTool`), kept until it leaves play; the Berries work by themselves between turns once they can (agreed: `useBerry` in `betweenTurns`), and Focus Band in the attack's damage (`fortitude`: heads, 10 HP left). Stadiums: Ecogym sends a non-Colorless Energy card another player's effect discards back to its owner's hand (`discardTheirEnergy`, used by every effect that discards the opponent's Energy), Sprout Tower takes 30 off Colorless Pokémon's attacks after Weakness and Resistance. Special Energy (`NEO1_ENERGY`, so `cardImplemented` counts them): Darkness and Metal Energy play by the EX rules (agreed; see EX Ruby & Sapphire); Recycle Energy goes back to the hand when it's discarded from play (`GameState.recycleWatch`, `returnRecycleEnergy`). Card-Flip Game uses the `pick` Trainer question (labelled buttons) for a face-down Prize (`TrainerSituation.oppFaceDownPrizes`) and the guess, and turns it face up for both players (`PlayerState.faceUpPrizes`; the view's `faceUpPrizes` is in Prize order, null while face down). Mary (`shuffleFromHand`), Time Capsule (`timeCapsule`: the opponent first, 5 cards or none, all or none with fewer; the CPU takes Pokémon first: agreed) and Double Gust (`doubleGust`: the opponent picks which of the player's benched Pokémon comes in first) are pending choices; Pokémon March asks the opponent, then the player (`pickFromDeck` with `then`). PokéGear, Time Capsule and Professor Elm stop further Trainers that turn (`noTrainersUntil`).

Neo Discovery (`effects/neo2.ts` attacks, `neo2-powers.ts`, `neo2-trainers.ts`; tests in `neo2.test.ts`, `neo2-powers.test.ts`, `neo2-trainers.test.ts`): every attack, Pokémon Power and Trainer is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Neo Discovery packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Its non-holo rares 21–36 are the holo rares 2–17 again (`withNonHolo`, `neo2-prints.ts`); 20 Espeon and 32 Umbreon are different cards. Igglybuff and Tyrogue are Babies (`BABIES`). Printed-card fixes: Dust Devil hits non-Fighting Pokémon, Collect Fire and Flamethrower take Fire Energy, Recover Water, Spike Barrage counts Water Energy, Feint Attack has no printed damage, and Unown M, N and I's powers, which TCGdex leaves blank, are written out (with a power's `name` as well as its `text` overriding TCGdex's: the Unowns' names have their brackets). Agreed rulings: Pineco's Burst flips twice, heads on the first doing 40 to Pineco and 10 to every benched Pokémon, tails on the second only stopping the 40 to the Defending Pokémon; Wobbuffet's Counter does back exactly the damage it took, without Weakness or Resistance; Hatch and Shockwave do their "Then…" part only on heads; Energy Evolution works on any Energy card its owner attaches to Eevee, by any effect, even the turn Eevee was played, and Rainbow Energy lets it become any Eeveelution.

Neo Discovery mechanics: attack effects on a Pokémon (`PokemonEffects`) for Counter (`counter`), Secrete Poison (`secretePoison`: the attacker is Poisoned and its player's benched Pokémon take 10), Metapod's Harden (`preventUpTo`: 20 or less), Slime (`slime`: the attacking player flips, tails prevents the damage), Pursuit (`pursuit`: 10 damage when it tries to retreat) and Lock-on (`lockOn` on Magnemite, `lockedOnBy` on the Defending Pokémon: Electric Bolt's tails count as heads next turn). Triple Poison sets `poison` to 30. Sketch copies the attack the Defending Pokémon used last turn (its `lastAttack`). Hatch evolves Metapod from the deck (`evolveFromDeck`); Scout shows the opponent's hand (`lookOppHand`). Powers: Spikes (`spikes`) hits a player's new Active Pokémon during their own turn, found by `newActives` (run with every Knock Out check: `PlayerState.lastActive` remembers the Active Pokémon), which also turns a Gazed Pokémon's power back on once it's left the Bench (`InPlayPokemon.gazed`, with `powerOffUntil` for the turn). Frog Song (`frogSong`) and Unown D, M and N (`unownGuard`) change the damage in `hit`; Anger (`anger`) is counted by Hidden Power, an attack effect on every Unown. Energy Evolution (`energyEvolution`) is checked after every action (`applyAction` compares each of the player's Eevees' Energy before and after). Unown E and I react to being put onto the Bench from the hand (`onPlayFromHand`, now for Basics too); Engage is the `engage` pending choice (the opponent first; the CPU takes a new hand of 4 only with fewer than 4 cards), Observe `lookOppDeck` (shown like a hand). Revive Friends, Fossil and Fossil Egg put Stage 1 cards onto the Bench as Basic Pokémon (`pickFromDeck` with the `evolvesFrom` filter for Mysterious Fossil's evolutions). Hyper Devolution Spray returns the top Evolution card to the hand, as Devolution Beam does, and the Pokémon can't evolve again that turn. Its Trainers and powers: the gym leader CPU plays them through its generic judgement (see The CPU opponent) (and a CPU's Eevee evolves when it attaches Energy).

Southern Islands (`effects/si1.ts`; tests in `si1.test.ts`): every attack is done (the set has no Pokémon Powers or Trainers), and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Southern Islands packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). TCGdex has no attacks for Marill and lists Tentacruel's twice: the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES` (`packages/db/prisma/seed.ts`) has the printed ones (re-seed for them), and the API and the test pool keep each attack name once (`printedAttacks`). Tentacruel's Poison Sting Paralyzes, as printed. Strange Scent's players remove their damage counters one at a time (`removeDamage` with a `count`; none is asked when there's nothing to choose), the attacking player first; Rainbow Wave asks a type only when Mew's Energy provides more than one.

Neo Revelation (`effects/neo3.ts` attacks, `neo3-powers.ts`, `neo3-trainers.ts`; tests in `neo3.test.ts`, `neo3-powers.test.ts`, `neo3-trainers.test.ts`): every attack, Pokémon Power and Trainer is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Neo Revelation packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Smoochum is a Baby (`BABIES`); Parasect's power (Allergic Pollen), which TCGdex leaves blank, and the Energy symbols TCGdex drops (Dark Flame, Searing Flames, Stoke, Plasma, Lightning Strike, Core Stream, Lava Flow, Devastate) are written out. Agreed rulings: Legendary Body (Entei, Raikou and Suicune) keeps every Trainer card, either player's, but Stadiums from affecting the Active Pokémon and its Energy (`trainerProof`: Trainers' Pokémon and Energy questions leave it out, and the Trainers that reach the Active Pokémon unasked, such as Switch, Gust of Wind, Full Heal, PlusPower, Pokémon Center, Double Gust, Warp Point, Sleep! and Digger, don't), and Trainer cards attached to it are discarded; Pokémon Breeder Fields' heads find any later-Stage card of that Pokémon's line.

Neo Revelation mechanics: Time Travel (`timeTravel`) flips when an opponent's attack would Knock Celebi Out and marks it (`InPlayPokemon.timeTravel`); `resolveKnockouts` then shuffles it into its owner's deck, with no Prize card. Hard Shell (`hardShell`) and Crystal Body (`crystalBody`: the attack's `untouchable` checks, which stop everything but damage) are in `attacks.ts`. Prehistoric Memory (`prehistoricMemory`) works like Recall for every Evolved Pokémon in play (`recallsAttacks`); Mimic (`mimic`) adds the Defending Pokémon's attacks to Sudowoodo's (`usableAttacks`' `defender`); Genetic Memory asks which lower-stage attack with the `amount` question's `labels`. Magma Pool (`magmaPool`) is found by `newActives`, which now knows the first turn's Active Pokémon too (set when the battle starts). Allergic Pollen (`allergicPollen`, `discardsLocked`) drops attacks' discard-pile questions (`AttackSituation.discardsLocked`) and stops Fire Recharge, Reel In and Rebirth; Unown K's Keep (`keep`, `energyKept`) stops the opponent's Energy discards (`discardDefenderEnergy`, Energy Removal, Super Energy Removal, Super Removal, Energy Drain, Twister, Devastate). Energy Converter treats a basic Energy card as another type until the end of the turn (`CardInstance.converted`, read by `energyProvided` and `isEnergyCardOf`; `refreshConverted` drops it once Porygon2 is Asleep, Confused or Paralyzed, and Submerge's `InPlayPokemon.submerged` likewise). Lightning Burst asks the opponent which benched Pokémon comes in (`switchIn`); Howl (a `mayFlip` choice, which the CPUs decline) discards the top 5 cards and gives their Fire Energy to Fire Pokémon (`distributeEnergy` with `toType`), then ends the turn (`endTurnNow` after a pending choice); Softboiled is a `mayFlip` too. Triggered Poison (`triggeredPoison`) is checked after every action, as Energy Evolution is. Attract Current and Unown Y's [Yield] attach cards found in the deck to some Pokémon only (`pickFromDeck`'s `attachOnly`, `attachCard`'s `only`; each card is attached before the search goes on, `attachCard`'s `next`). Evolutionary Spore is `evolveMany`; Gold Scale is a `drawCount` with `allOrNone`; Old Rod's Trainer is `discardToHand`'s `trainer` filter. Rocket's Hideout gives Pokémon with Dark in their names 20 HP (`InPlayPokemon.hideout`, `refreshHideout`); Balloon Berry makes the retreat cost 0 and is discarded instead (`retreatCost`, `RETREAT`); Healing Field is a usable Stadium. Unown B and Y's powers work while Asleep, Confused or Paralyzed (`ignoresConditions`, now for used powers too). Its Trainers and powers: the gym leader CPU plays them through its generic judgement (see The CPU opponent).

Neo Destiny (`effects/neo4.ts` attacks, `neo4-powers.ts`, `neo4-trainers.ts` with Miracle Energy; tests in `neo4.test.ts`, `neo4-powers.test.ts`, `neo4-trainers.test.ts`): every attack, Pokémon Power, Trainer and Miracle Energy is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Neo Destiny packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Its Light Stage 2 Pokémon evolve from the Light Stage 1, as the Dark ones do (`evolvesFrom`, in the API and the test pool), and Dark Omanyte from Mysterious Fossil. Printed-card fixes: Light Piloswine's Fluffy Wool and Unown P's [Perform], which TCGdex leaves blank, Light Machamp's Tag Team and Beatdown, the Energy symbols TCGdex drops, and Shining Tyranitar's two attacks, which TCGdex leaves unnamed (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them). Shining Pokémon and Miracle Energy are 1 to a deck. Agreed ruling (as Hatch): Explosive Evolution and Sunbathe do their "Then…" part only on heads.

Neo Destiny mechanics: Scare (`scare`, `scaredBy`) stops the opponent's Baby Pokémon's powers (`powerActive`) and attacks (`attackError`); Deep Sleep (`deepSleep`) makes waking need 2 heads; Conductivity (`conductivity`) hits a Pokémon given an Energy card from the hand (`energyFromHand`, Rain Dance too); Hot Plate (`hotPlate`) hits Baby and Basic Pokémon put onto a Bench from the hand; Miraculous Wind (`miraculousWind`, `refreshWind`) marks every Special Energy card in play `windless`: Colorless Energy only (`energyProvided`, `isEnergyCardOf`), and no Darkness, Metal, Rainbow or Recycle Energy effects. [Chase] is in `RETREAT`, [Zoom] and Tentacle Wrap (`heavier`) in `retreatCost`, [XXXXX] and [Perform] in Hidden Power (`hiddenPowerOf`, also for Unown S and T's 20), [Quicken] in the attack's protections (`PlayerState.quickenUntil`, once a turn per player: `quickenTurn`). Attack effects on a Pokémon: Light Wave (`effectProof`: nothing but damage, like Crystal Body), Pulse Guard (`pulseGuard`), Dodge (`dodge`), Reflect Shield (`reflectShield`), Armor Up (`armorUp`), Charm (`charm`, before Weakness and Resistance, on all the Pokémon's attack damage), Dark Tentacle (`noEvolve`, checked by `evolveError`) and Flash Touch (`flashTouch`, while it's the Active Pokémon); Ice Pillar is `PlayerState.icePillar`. New questions: `ownEnergyAny` (Rushing Flames: Energy from any of the player's Pokémon) and pending choices `stadiumFromDiscard` (Spatial Distortion), `oppHandToActive` (Eerie Howl), `discardToDeck` (Fish Out), `returnUnown` ([Vanish], a `mayFlip`), `invention` (Impostor Professor Oak's Invention) and `cunning`; "your opponent may…, then you may" searches chain to the other player (`then` on `discardToHand`, `pickFromTop` with `basicEnergy` for Flipper Stroke, `evolveMany` with `once` for Evolution Song; `thenSame` on `pickFromDeck` for Gift, which searches for a Pokémon Tool: `TrainerEffect.tool`). EXP.ALL and Counterattack Claws work in `hit`; Magnifier turns off Resistance; Thought Wave Machine returns Energy to the hand (`removeEach` with `toHand`) and ends the turn; Pokémon Personality Test is a `guess`; Team Rocket's Evil Deeds takes any hand card (`oppHandTrainer` with `any`); Radio Tower looks at the player's own deck (`lookOppDeck` with `own`); Miracle Energy goes only on Shining and Light Pokémon (`EnergyEffect.attachError`) and is discarded at the end of its owner's turn. Its Trainers and powers: the gym leader CPU plays them through its generic judgement (see The CPU opponent) (the CPUs decline Howl).

Expedition (`effects/ecard1.ts` attacks, `ecard1-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies, `ecard1-trainers.ts`; tests in `ecard1.test.ts`, `ecard1-powers.test.ts`, `ecard1-trainers.test.ts`; `packages/campaign/src/ecard.test.ts` checks every card against the snapshot): every card is done (Bill's Maintenance can't be played with no other card in the hand, agreed), and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Expedition packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). The non-holo prints of the holo rares take the holos' effects (`withNonHolo`, `ecard1-prints.ts`). Printed-card fixes, checked against every card image: the Weakness and Resistance TCGdex gives cards printed without them (the Babies have neither), the non-holo Arbok's and Blastoise's "Evolves from" (also filled in by the API, `MISSING_EVOLVES_FROM_BY_ID`, until a re-seed), Ninetales' Ethereal Flame (30+), Caterpie's Entangling Thread, Hoppip's single attack and Multi Technical Machine 01's attack (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them).

Expedition mechanics: Poké-Powers stop with any Special Condition, Poisoned and Burned included (as every Pokémon Power now does); Poké-Bodies always work (`ignoresConditions`); the engine's Pokémon Power rules (Toxic Gas, Goop Gas Attack) cover both. Supporters (`TrainerEffect.supporter`) are one a turn (`PlayerState.supporterTurn`, checked by `trainerError`), discarded as soon as they're played. A Technical Machine (`TrainerEffect.technicalMachine`, Multi Technical Machine 01) is attached until the end of the turn and adds its attack to `usableAttacks`, as Psymimic (`psymimic`) adds every attack of the opponent's Pokémon in play (`usableAttacks`' `oppBench`, passed by the attack list too). Strength Charm (a Pokémon Tool) adds 10 after Weakness and Resistance and is discarded at the end of that turn; Expedition's Darkness and Metal Energy play by the EX rules, as every set's do (agreed; see EX Ruby & Sapphire). Rock Body and Exoskeleton (`damageReduction`), Dark Aura (`energyAs`, in `energyPool`), Tailwind (`tailwind`, in `retreatCost`), Burning Energy (`converted` basic Energy for the turn) and Harvest Bounty (`PlayerState.activeEnergyTurn`: the turn's Energy card from the hand went to the Active Pokémon). New questions: `ownPokemonTo` for attacks (Energy Patch: another of the player's Pokémon to move something to) and `side` for powers (Chaos Move: whose Pokémon, `fromSide` and `toSide`, shown as buttons in `PowerPanel`); Dual Ball's deck search is `basicNotBaby`.

Aquapolis (`effects/ecard2.ts` attacks, with the Cubes' attacks; `ecard2-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies; `ecard2-trainers.ts` Trainers and special Energy, `ECARD2_ENERGY`; rulings in their headers; tests in `ecard2.test.ts`): every card is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Aquapolis packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Printed-card fixes: Smeargle's Doubleslap and the Cubes' attacks, which TCGdex leaves out (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them).

Aquapolis mechanics: the Cubes are Technical Machines for a Pokémon of their type. Dark Impact stops the Defending Pokémon's Poké-Powers (`effects.noPokePowers`, in `powerActive`/`powerError`). Retreat-cost bodies (Extreme Speed, Lightweight, Gluey Slime, ...) are `retreatChange`/`glueySlime`, summed by `retreatBodies` into `retreatCost`; Suction Cups (`suctionCups`) discards a retreating opponent's Energy. Fluff (`fluff`, in `transparent`), Dense Body (`basicGuard`, in `barriers`), Poison Resistance (`noPoison`, in `inflict`), Enervating Pollen (`enervatingPollen`: Resistance takes off at most 10) and the Anti- bodies (`antiAttach`, `attachBlocked`) are passive. Crystal Type gives Kingdra the type of the basic Energy card attached from the hand for the turn (`InPlayPokemon.crystalType`, read by `inPlayDef`).
