Crystal Energy is one Energy that counts as any one of the types of basic Energy attached (`crystal:<types>` in the energy pool; `counts` and `payCost` handle it); Boost Energy (3 Colorless, Evolved Pokémon only, no retreating) and Miracle Energy are discarded at the end of the turn. Healing Berry is in `useBerry`, Memory Berry in `usableAttacks`, Time Shard in `knockOut` (with more than 2 basic Energy cards attached, its owner picks up to 2 once the attack is over: `discardAttached` with `toHand` and `optional`, agreed). Pokémon Park is in `energyFromHand`; Apricorn Forest, Undersea Ruins and Power Plant are used like Lucky Stadium; Forest Guardian, Juggler, Pokémon Fan Club, Seer, Town Volunteers and Traveling Salesman are Supporters, and Vileplume's Addictive Pollen stops the opponent's Supporters (`PlayerState.noSupportersUntil`). New pending choices: `revealEnergy`, `devolveOwn` and `powerPlant`; Dark Moon reuses `oppHandTrainer` with `count`.

Skyridge (`effects/ecard3.ts` attacks, with the Miracle Spheres' and Mystery Plates' attacks; `ecard3-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies; `ecard3-trainers.ts` Trainers and special Energy, `ECARD3_ENERGY`; rulings in their headers; tests in `ecard3.test.ts`): every card is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Skyridge packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Printed-card fixes: Omanyte has no Resistance, Miracle Sphere α's text and Miracle Sphere γ's Thunder Force cost (Lightning) (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them); Bounce Energy provides 2 Colorless Energy (`apps/api/src/cards.ts`).

Skyridge mechanics: Buried Fossil takes the Pokémon that evolve from Mysterious Fossil (`evolvesOnto`) and, like Clefairy Doll, can't be affected by Special Conditions (`conditionProof` in cards.ts). Primal Aura and Primal Stare stop Evolution cards from the hand (`primalBlock`, in `evolveError`); Ancient Wind turns every Poké-Body off for the turn (`PlayerState.bodiesOffTurn`, in `powerActive`); Dark Gaze stops Benched Pokémon's Poké-Powers (`darkGazed`, in `powerError` and `playedFromHand`). Immunity is Crystal Body (`crystalBody`), Thick Shell `evolvedGuard`, Mirror Coat `mirrorCoat` (in `inflict`), Rare Metal and Prismatic Body `basicEnergyAs` (in `energyPool`), Psychoflow and Slippery Skin `retreatChange` (which now sees the opponent), Synchronicity `synchronicity` (any Technical Machine, the Cubes too). The "when you play X from your hand to evolve your Active Pokémon" powers use `onPlayFromHand`. Streaming Mantle's basic Energy cards go back by themselves, or through `discardToDeck` when a player has more than 3 (`mantleEnergy`, agreed). Retro Cave and Healing Oasis ask for their Pokémon only when the opponent has exactly 2 Prize cards left (`AttackSituation.opponentPrizes`). Freeze Lock is `PlayerState.freezeLockUntil` (ATTACH_ENERGY), Mirage Stadium's flip is in RETREAT, Star Piece evolves between turns (`useStarPiece`), Crystal Shard makes its Pokémon Colorless (`inPlayDef`) and is discarded after it attacks, and Skyridge's Pollen Shield covers Burned too (`effects.conditionProof`). New pending choices (`skyridgeView`, `resolveSkyridge` in trainers.ts): `mayUse` (Energy Recharge, Flame Vapor, Streaming Mantle, Retro Energy), `attachFromDiscard`, `discardToBench` (Manipulate, Underground Lake), `moveAttached` (Attract Energy), `oracle`, `bottomPick` (Underground Expedition), `evolveFromHand` (Water of Evolution), `mysteryZone` and `placeCounters` (Electric Blast); `oppHandTrainer` takes a `filter` (Carry Off, Sudden Swipe), `lookOppDeck` `prizes` (Investigate: the player picks which 1 or 2 Prize cards, `picked`, agreed), `removeEach` `toDeck` (Stone Crush), `discardDraw` `max`, `attachFromHand` `basic`, `discardToDeck` a `filter`, and `pickFromDeck` the `supporter`, `supporterOrStadium`, `ballTrainer` and `pokemonType` filters. Ancient Ruins needs the turn in `stadiumBlocked`, Bounce Energy the card library in `attachError`.

Expedition (`effects/ecard1.ts` attacks, `ecard1-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies, `ecard1-trainers.ts`; tests in `ecard1.test.ts`, `ecard1-powers.test.ts`, `ecard1-trainers.test.ts`; `packages/campaign/src/ecard.test.ts` checks every card against the snapshot): every card is done (Bill's Maintenance can't be played with no other card in the hand, agreed), and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Expedition packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). The non-holo prints of the holo rares take the holos' effects (`withNonHolo`, `ecard1-prints.ts`). Printed-card fixes, checked against every card image: the Weakness and Resistance TCGdex gives cards printed without them (the Babies have neither), the non-holo Arbok's and Blastoise's "Evolves from" (also filled in by the API, `MISSING_EVOLVES_FROM_BY_ID`, until a re-seed), Ninetales' Ethereal Flame (30+), Caterpie's Entangling Thread, Hoppip's single attack and Multi Technical Machine 01's attack (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them).

Expedition mechanics: Poké-Powers stop with any Special Condition, Poisoned and Burned included (as every Pokémon Power now does); Poké-Bodies always work (`ignoresConditions`); the engine's Pokémon Power rules (Toxic Gas, Goop Gas Attack) cover both. Supporters (`TrainerEffect.supporter`) are one a turn (`PlayerState.supporterTurn`, checked by `trainerError`), discarded as soon as they're played. A Technical Machine (`TrainerEffect.technicalMachine`, Multi Technical Machine 01) is attached until the end of the turn and adds its attack to `usableAttacks`, as Psymimic (`psymimic`) adds every attack of the opponent's Pokémon in play (`usableAttacks`' `oppBench`, passed by the attack list too). Strength Charm (a Pokémon Tool) adds 10 after Weakness and Resistance and is discarded at the end of that turn; Expedition's Darkness and Metal Energy play like Neo Genesis's (agreed). Rock Body and Exoskeleton (`damageReduction`), Dark Aura (`energyAs`, in `energyPool`), Tailwind (`tailwind`, in `retreatCost`), Burning Energy (`converted` basic Energy for the turn) and Harvest Bounty (`PlayerState.activeEnergyTurn`: the turn's Energy card from the hand went to the Active Pokémon). New questions: `ownPokemonTo` for attacks (Energy Patch: another of the player's Pokémon to move something to) and `side` for powers (Chaos Move: whose Pokémon, `fromSide` and `toSide`, shown as buttons in `PowerPanel`); Dual Ball's deck search is `basicNotBaby`.

Aquapolis (`effects/ecard2.ts` attacks, with the Cubes' attacks; `ecard2-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies; `ecard2-trainers.ts` Trainers and special Energy, `ECARD2_ENERGY`; rulings in their headers; tests in `ecard2.test.ts`): every card is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Aquapolis packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Printed-card fixes: Smeargle's Doubleslap and the Cubes' attacks, which TCGdex leaves out (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them).

Healing Berry is in `useBerry`, Memory Berry in `usableAttacks`, Time Shard in `knockOut` (with more than 2 basic Energy cards attached, its owner picks up to 2 once the attack is over: `discardAttached` with `toHand` and `optional`, agreed). Pokémon Park is in `energyFromHand`; Apricorn Forest, Undersea Ruins and Power Plant are used like Lucky Stadium; Forest Guardian, Juggler, Pokémon Fan Club, Seer, Town Volunteers and Traveling Salesman are Supporters, and Vileplume's Addictive Pollen stops the opponent's Supporters (`PlayerState.noSupportersUntil`). New pending choices: `revealEnergy`, `devolveOwn` and `powerPlant`; Dark Moon reuses `oppHandTrainer` with `count`.

Skyridge (`effects/ecard3.ts` attacks, with the Miracle Spheres' and Mystery Plates' attacks; `ecard3-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies; `ecard3-trainers.ts` Trainers and special Energy, `ECARD3_ENERGY`; rulings in their headers; tests in `ecard3.test.ts`): every card is done, and its cards are in Gen 2 runs and Skyridge packs (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`). Printed-card fixes: Omanyte has no Resistance, Miracle Sphere α's text and Miracle Sphere γ's Thunder Force cost (Lightning) (the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES`: re-seed for them); Bounce Energy provides 2 Colorless Energy (`apps/api/src/cards.ts`).

Skyridge mechanics: Buried Fossil takes the Pokémon that evolve from Mysterious Fossil (`evolvesOnto`) and, like Clefairy Doll, can't be affected by Special Conditions (`conditionProof` in cards.ts). Primal Aura and Primal Stare stop Evolution cards from the hand (`primalBlock`, in `evolveError`); Ancient Wind turns every Poké-Body off for the turn (`PlayerState.bodiesOffTurn`, in `powerActive`); Dark Gaze stops Benched Pokémon's Poké-Powers (`darkGazed`, in `powerError` and `playedFromHand`). New pending choices (`skyridgeView`, `resolveSkyridge` in trainers.ts): `mayUse` (Energy Recharge, Flame Vapor, Streaming Mantle, Retro Energy), `attachFromDiscard`, `discardToBench` (Manipulate, Underground Lake), `moveAttached` (Attract Energy), `oracle`, `bottomPick` (Underground Expedition), `evolveFromHand` (Water of Evolution), `mysteryZone` and `placeCounters` (Electric Blast); `oppHandTrainer` takes a `filter` (Carry Off, Sudden Swipe), `lookOppDeck` `prizes` (Investigate: the player picks which 1 or 2 Prize cards, `picked`, agreed), `removeEach` `toDeck` (Stone Crush), `discardDraw` `max`, `attachFromHand` `basic`, `discardToDeck` a `filter`, and `pickFromDeck` the `supporter`, `supporterOrStadium`, `ballTrainer` and `pokemonType` filters. Ancient Ruins needs the turn in `stadiumBlocked`, Bounce Energy the card library in `attachError`.

Generation 3 (agreed: the EX series, EX Ruby & Sapphire to EX Power Keepers, 2003–2007): the snapshot has every card of `ex1`–`ex16` (`EX_SETS` in `packages/db/scripts/fetch-tcgdex.ts`; EX Poké Card Creator Pack and the Unseen Forces Unown Collection left out, like the WOTC era's `miscp`). They're in the catalog (the deck builder's Gen 3 · Hoenn sets, Training), are Gen 3's runs' cards (`GEN3_SETS`, see Generations) and have booster packs (`EX_SETS` in `PACK_SETS`, under Gen 3 on the Packs tab), unlocked by Gen 3's (Hoenn's) runs as each generation's are. Reverse holos (agreed): the seed makes a reverse-holo print of each EX card that has one (`reverseHoloCard` in `packages/db/prisma/holo-energy.ts`, id "<card id>-reverse", `reverseHolo` in its data): TCGdex lists them up to EX Unseen Forces, and from EX Delta Species on (where TCGdex lists none) every card but the Pokémon-ex, the Gold Stars (☆) and the secret rares gets one, as in the earlier EX sets. A reverse holo is a special addition like a holo with a non-holo twin (`holoTwins` maps it to its card): its copies count, a deck holds no more than were gained, and it unlocks the card's own print. It plays as its card (the engine's effect lookups drop "-reverse", `printOf`), is shown in the library's reverse-holo style (foil everywhere but the art window: `reverseHolo` in `holo.ts`), is a holo for rewards, Stardust and crafting, and an EX pack has one in place of a common (`PackSet.reverse`, the `reverse` slot). Like holo Energy, the Collection tab shows one only once it's been gained, and the numbers of cards collected (the Collection's, the Packs tab's) leave them out (`isVersion` in `cardList.ts`, agreed). TCGdex has no stage for many Pokémon-ex: the seed works it out (`stageOf`: no "Evolves from" is Basic, otherwise a stage above the Pokémon it evolves from), and the API (`toCardDef`) and the campaign's test pool read a missing one as Basic or Stage 1 until a re-seed. Pokémon-ex are `ex: true` on the card (from TCGdex's `suffix`): Knocked Out, they give up 2 Prize cards (`knockOut`). Basic Energy is never holo in its base form (`notHolo` in the seed: TCGdex marks EX Emerald's, Holon Phantoms' and Power Keepers' holo; the holo versions are the holo Energy, agreed).

EX Ruby & Sapphire (`effects/ex1.ts` attacks, `ex1-powers.ts` Poké-Powers and Poké-Bodies, `ex1-trainers.ts` Trainers and special Energy, `EX1_ENERGY`; rulings in their headers; tests in `ex1.test.ts`; `packages/campaign/src/ex.test.ts` checks every card against the snapshot): every card is done. Rulings agreed with the user: the wording made for 2 Active Pokémon a side reads "each Defending Pokémon" as the Active Pokémon and every Benched one (the Benched ones' damage without Weakness or Resistance, Special Conditions only on the Active one), "the other Defending Pokémon" as one of the Benched ones picked by the player whose Energy card moves (Fast Stream, the `fastStream` choice), and every other "Active Pokémon" as the one Active Pokémon; "Energy attached" counts the Energy provided (Double Colorless is 2), and "not used to pay for this attack's cost" is that less the cost; Lazy stops the older sets' Pokémon Powers too (`lazied`, in `powerActive` and `powerError`); Psy Shadow may Knock Out the Pokémon it attaches to (`attachCard`'s `counters`); Water Call's Energy is attached from the hand, so it sets off Natural Remedy; EX Pokémon Reversal's opponent picks the Benched Pokémon. Darkness and Metal Energy play by the EX rules for every set's cards (agreed): Darkness Energy adds 10 to the Active Pokémon before Weakness and Resistance, only from a Darkness Pokémon or one with Dark in its name, with no damage counters; Metal Energy takes 10 off after Weakness and Resistance, only on a Metal Pokémon, with nothing off its own attacks (`attacks.ts`). New mechanics: Withering Dust (`witheringDust`), Intimidating Fang (`intimidatingFang`), Hard Cocoon (`hardCocoon`, in `barriers`), Rough Skin (`onAttackDamage`), Natural Cure and Natural Remedy (`onEnergyFromHand`), Stockpile (`PokemonEffects.stockpile`), Lady Outing and Signs of Evolution (`pickFromDeck`'s `distinct`), PokéNav (`pickFromTop`'s `pokeNav`, then `arrangeTop`), Lum Berry and Oran Berry (`useBerry`).

Shared EX wordings (`effects/ex-templates.ts`): the EX sets after Ruby & Sapphire (Sandstorm to Power Keepers) word many attacks alike ("Flip a coin. If heads, the Defending Pokémon is now Paralyzed.", "Does 50 damage plus 10 more damage for each Water Energy attached to … but not used to pay for this attack's Energy cost…"). Each such wording is a template (`ATTACK_TEMPLATES`: a pattern over the normalised text, `normText`, with the Pokémon's own name as "@", and the building block that plays it, from the numbers and words in it and the printed damage). `packages/game-engine/scripts/generate-ex-templates.ts` (run with `npx tsx scripts/generate-ex-templates.ts` in `packages/game-engine`, after changing a template or the snapshot) matches every attack of ex2–ex16 against them and writes the matches to `ex-template-data.ts` (1,041 of the 1,772 attacks with text); a wording whose base damage isn't the card's printed damage is left out (`fits`) and reported. `effects/index.ts` puts them under each set's own effects, which have the last word on any attack. Only wordings whose rules are settled are templates: the EX Ruby & Sapphire rulings and the older cards' that word them alike. A set's Poké-Powers, Poké-Bodies, Trainers, special Energy and other attacks are still its own file's.

EX Sandstorm (`effects/ex2.ts`, `ex2-powers.ts`, `ex2-trainers.ts` with Multi Energy, `EX2_ENERGY`; tests in `ex2.test.ts`) and EX Dragon (`effects/ex3.ts`, `ex3-powers.ts`, `ex3-trainers.ts`; tests in `ex3.test.ts`): every card is done (with the shared wordings), rulings in their headers. Agreed with the user:
- "All Defending Pokémon" is the opponent's Active and Benched Pokémon; "each Defending Pokémon" is their Active Pokémon only (changed 2026-10-22: Super Slap Push, Trembler, Sand Swirl, Double Wing Attack, Double Damage and Dragon Crush, which discards an Energy card from it only). Random Curse's 5 damage counters go on any of the opponent's Pokémon (`placeCounters`' `active`), Split Blast may do 50 to each of them whenever the opponent has a Benched Pokémon, and Dragon Dance's 40 more (`dragonDanceTurn`) is for whichever Pokémon attacks next turn, on Benched damage too.
- A partner a power needs "in play" (Lunar and Solar Eclipse, Glowing Screen, Uplifting Glow) is its owner's own.
- A named Pokémon matches every variant of it (owners' Pokémon such as Team Aqua's Seviper and Brock's Onix, Dark, Light and Shining Pokémon, δ and ☆: `namesMatch`), in every set's "named" deck searches too; only a Pokémon-ex never matches its non-ex name.
- Energy Variation makes Kecleon every type of basic Energy card attached (`variedTypes`, `PokemonCardDef.types`, `hasType`): Weakness or Resistance applies once if any matches, Low Pressure System counts it, and only Darkness Energy's own rule gives Darkness Energy's bonus.
- Baby Evolution can be used on the first turn and the turn the baby was played; Rare Candy and Wally's Training keep the usual limits, and Rare Candy can take a Fossil straight to its Stage 2.
- Supernatural uses a Supporter's effect from the opponent's hand as if its player had played it (`supernatural` choice, `trainerContext`), its questions asked during the attack one at a time (Mr. Briney's Compassion's Pokémon…); the card stays there.
- Multi Energy is Colorless only while another Special Energy card is attached to its Pokémon, checked all the time (`colorlessOnly`, refreshMultiEnergy).
- Mr. Briney's Compassion can return the Active Pokémon.
- The older sets' Pokémon Powers count as Poké-Powers (Special Circuit, the shared "has any Poké-Powers or Poké-Bodies" wording, Muk ex's Toxic Gas).
- Chain of Events does nothing (it needs a second Active Pokémon).
- Luster Purge's 3 Energy count as provided; Rainbow and Multi Energy count as Fire or Water for Mist Ball; Big Explosion does its 80 to Graveler too (Weakness and Resistance only on the opponent's side).

New mechanics: Primal Veil (`supportersVeiled`), Primal Lock (`primalLocked`, dropLockedTools), Safeguard and Wonder Guard (`guardFrom`), Submerge (`benchImmune`), Energy Guard (`energyGuard`), Glowing Screen (`partnerGuard`), Power Pinchers (`powerPinchers`), Rain Dish (`rainDish`, betweenTurns), Muk ex's Toxic Gas (a Poké-Body: only as its owner's Active Pokémon), Ampharos ex's Conductivity (`conductivityEx`), Buffer Piece, High and Low Pressure System (`lowPressure`), TV Reporter (`discardFromHand`), Energy Shower and Speed Growth (`attachFromHand`'s `anyTarget`), Pick On (`oppHandTrainer`'s `exact`), Scam (its `supporter` filter), Strike and Run (an optional `switchIn`), the EX Fossils (played as Basic Pokémon: Claw and Root Fossil 40 HP). TCGdex's EX data: the seed gives an evolving Pokémon-ex TCGdex calls Basic (EX Dragon's Ampharos ex, Dragonite ex, Golem ex, Kingdra ex, Magcargo ex, Muk ex) its real stage (`stageOf`), fixes four cards' text (`TCGDEX_FIXES`), and applies every fix to a card's reverse-holo print too (re-seed).

EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua (`effects/ex4.ts`, `ex4-powers.ts`, `ex4-trainers.ts` with Aqua Energy, Magma Energy and Double Rainbow Energy, `EX4_ENERGY`; tests in `ex4.test.ts`) and EX Hidden Legends (`effects/ex5.ts`, `ex5-powers.ts`, `ex5-trainers.ts`; tests in `ex5.test.ts`): every card is done, rulings in their headers. Agreed with the user:
- A Pokémon printed as two types (Team Aqua's and Team Magma's Pokémon: "This Pokémon is both … type") is both (`PokemonCardDef.types`, `hasType`): Weakness and Resistance apply if either matches, once; it's a Darkness Pokémon for Darkness Energy and Dark Splash; Steel Wave counts a Benched Pokémon sharing either type.
- A second printed Weakness or Resistance (`moreWeakness`, `moreResistance`, from the API; Team Magma's Aggron: Grass and Water) counts too, but it's applied once however many match. This also fixes the earlier EX sets' cards that print two.
- Evolution follows the printed "Evolves from" exactly (Team Aqua's Cacturne from Team Aqua's Cacnea only), for Rare Candy, Wally's Training, the Belts and every evolving effect too.
- Deck building (`limitName`, packages/shared/src/deck.ts): a δ Pokémon counts towards its base Pokémon's 4-copy limit; every other name has its own (Shining, Gold Star and Pokémon-ex print or have their own).
- "A Pokémon that has an owner in its name" (`hasOwner`): Team Aqua's, Team Magma's, the Gym Heroes owners' and Rocket's; Dark and Light Pokémon have none.
- Make a Wish and Ascension keep the usual limits for evolving (`evolveMany`'s `limits`); the Team Aqua and Team Magma Belts are their owner's choice between turns, with those limits (askBelts, a `betweenTurns` choice).
- Aqua Trance and Slow-Acting Poison (`delayed`) happen at the end of the opponent's next turn only if the Pokémon is still the Active one; benching or evolving it ends them.
- Overheat's and Auxiliary Light's damage counters may Knock their Pokémon Out; Power Diffusion stops every attack's damage to its owner's Bench, their own attacks too; Darkness Chant and Shadow Steal count discard piles while Allergic Pollen works; Team Play counts both players' Pokémon; the Schemers can't be played without a Pokémon in the hand; Aqua and Magma Energy provide any mix of their 2 types; Forecast never plays a Stadium with the name of the one in play; Ice Generator discards the opponent's Stadium too.

New mechanics: Power Saver (`powerSaver`), Mark of Antiquity (`markOfAntiquity`), Primal Pull (`primalPull`), all in attackError; Block Dust (`trainersDusted`); Ice Wall (`specialGuard`); Shell Retreat and Core Guard (`coreGuard`); Fast Feet (`fastFeet`); Power Diffusion (`powerDiffusion`); Overzealous (`overzealous`); Energy Grounding (`energyGrounding`, the `energyGrounding` choice); Relicanth's Deep Sleep (as the Active Pokémon only); Healing Shower and the Belts (`mayUse`); Power Shift (`shiftEnergy`); Temperamental Weather (`castform`, once a turn: `weatherTurn`); Heal Dance (once a turn: `healDanceTurn`); Silver Wind and Extra Comet Punch (`silverWind`, `cometPunch`); Aqua and Magma Energy (`TEAM_ENERGY` in energyPool), Double Rainbow Energy (10 less damage; discarded off a Pokémon that's no longer Evolved); Team Magma Hideout (PLAY_BENCH), Team Aqua Hideout, Ancient Tomb, Desert Ruins, Island Cave, Magnetic Storm; Archie (`stage2Counters`), Maxie, the Conspirators (`ownerBasicOrEnergy`), the Teams' Call for Family (`basicTypeOrOwner`); Chime (Supernatural from the discard pile, `fromDiscard`); Flutter Trick and Push Aside (`oppHandTrainer`'s `place`); Forecast (`stadiumFromDiscard`'s `fromDeck`); the Team and Ancient Technical Machines (their attacks read from the printed cards). TCGdex's data: the seed fills in every EX Team Magma vs Team Aqua evolution's "Evolves from" (TCGdex has none; read from the cards) and Swampert ex's Crushing Wave (re-seed).

EX FireRed & LeafGreen (`effects/ex6.ts`, `ex6-powers.ts`, `ex6-trainers.ts` with Multi Energy, `EX6_ENERGY`; tests in `ex6.test.ts`) and EX Team Rocket Returns (`effects/ex7.ts`, `ex7-powers.ts`, `ex7-trainers.ts` with Dark Metal Energy and R Energy, `EX7_ENERGY`; tests in `ex7.test.ts`): every card is done, rulings in their headers. Agreed with the user:
- "The Defending Pokémon" and "each Defending Pokémon" are the opponent's Active Pokémon only; only "all Defending Pokémon" reaches the Bench (Double Wing Attack, Double Tackle, Black Suction Cups, Sheer Cold, Acid Sampler).
- Fiery Aura makes Burn 4 damage counters (40); it, Eerie Aura and Methane Leak hit both players' Pokémon. Retreat Aid (2 less each) and Power Gene (10 more each) add up, one for each Pokémon with it.
- Form Variation's and Swoop! Teleporter's new Pokémon keeps everything on the old one, can evolve this turn if the old one could have, and can use its own Poké-Power this turn.
- Multi Energy is checked all the time (as EX Sandstorm's). Energy Flame makes Double Colorless 2 Fire Energy, for Burn Down too; Crush and Burn and Volcanic Ash count the Energy cards discarded (Double Colorless is 1).
- Magic Odds and Magic Evens look at the final damage. With more than one EXP. ALL in play, their owner picks which takes the Energy card, asked before the Knock Out discards it (as Energy Grounding).
- Darkest Impulse puts 2 damage counters on every Pokémon the opponent evolves from their hand, one Darkest Impulse however many. Dark Seed's damage counters, like a delayed Special Condition, happen only to the Pokémon still Active then.
- Explosive Evolution evolves only on heads (as Neo Destiny's); Detour uses the effect of the player's Supporter in play, its questions asked during the attack (as Supernatural); Surprise! Time Machine swaps evolutions freely; R Energy's 10 more damage adds up; Raging Thunder may damage Rocket's Zapdos ex itself, never with Weakness; Rocket's Tricky Gym's Feint Attack keeps the attacker's own boosts.

EX Crystal Guardians (`effects/ex14.ts`, `ex14-powers.ts`, `ex14-trainers.ts`; tests in `ex14.test.ts`), EX Dragon Frontiers (`effects/ex15.ts`, `ex15-powers.ts`, `ex15-trainers.ts`; tests in `ex15.test.ts`) and EX Power Keepers (`effects/ex16.ts`, `ex16-powers.ts`, `ex16-trainers.ts`; tests in `ex16.test.ts`): every card is done, rulings in their headers, so every EX set is. Agreed with the user:
- Crystal Beach makes any Special Energy card providing 2 or more Energy at the time (conditional ones too: Scramble Energy, a Holon's Pokémon, a boosted React Energy) provide 1 Colorless Energy.
- Imprison and Shock-wave markers can go on Benched Pokémon and stay until the Pokémon leaves play (Tropical Heal removes them).
- Swampert ex's Energy Recycle attaches as many Energy cards as there are, up to 3; the turn ends anyway.

- Igglybuff and Snorlax δ have a Poké-Body and a Poké-Power each: the body works as usual and the power is used (`usePoke`: stopped as a Poké-Power is); the seed's `TCGDEX_FIXES` gives them both (re-seed), and fixes Aggron's Terraforming (a Poké-Power), Vibrava δ's Psychic Wing and Xatu δ's Extra Feather (Poké-Bodies).

New mechanics: Crystal Beach and Chlorophyll (`beached`, `chlorophyll`, `cardEnergy`, energyPool), Holon Veil (`holonVeil`, `veiled`, `isDelta`, `isDeltaCard`, deckFilter), Holon Legacy, Battle Frontier and Cessation Crystal (`stadiumMuted`), Holon Circle (performAttack), Sidney's Stadium (`sidneyGuard`, conditionBarred), Drake's and Glacia's Stadiums, Delta Protection, Hard Rock, Armor and the Synergy Effects (damage after Weakness and Resistance in attacks.ts), Extra Smoke (`reduceBefore`), Battle Aura and Extra Feather (`allyMore`), Extra Liquid (attackError), Intimidating Armor (attackError, `auraStops`), Dark Eyes and the `usePoke` powers (usePower, powerError), Imprison (`imprisoned`), Shield Beam (`powerTurns['Shield Beam']`, powerError), Psychic Protector (the `protector` choice before the attack, `psychicProtector` on the game), Metal Gravity (RETREAT), Hover Lift, Stages of Evolution, Extra Wing, Flotation and Psychic Wing (retreatBodies, `retreatLessFor`), Star Light and Rage Aura (`costNow`; `ownPrizes` on the attack situation), Time Travel to the bottom of the deck (`timeTravel: 'bottom'`), Type Shift (`typesCopied`), Peal of Thunder (the `pealTarget` choice, `pickFromTop`'s `attachTo`), Cursed Eyes (`cursedEyes`), the ☆ Rays, Crush Chance and Excavate (`mayUse`, `onBenchMay`), Mining (`pickFromDeck`'s `toolMayAttach`, `attachCard`'s `orHand`), Skill Hack (the `skillHack` choice, `performCopiedAttack`), Skill Copy, Mimicry and Delta Copy (`copyAmong`'s `defender` and `needsEnergy`), Rock Blast (`opponentBenchMany`'s `repeat`: the same Pokémon any number of times, on the board too), Shock-wave (`shockwave`), Island Hermit (`faceUpPrizes`), Windstorm, Castaway (queued searches) and Steven's Advice (7 cards, itself included).

Base Set 2 (`effects/base4.ts`): every card reprints a Base Set or Jungle card (`BASE4_REPRINTS`; TCGdex words a few differently, but the printed cards play alike), so it takes the original's attacks, Pokémon Power or Trainer effect (`base4Reprints` in `effects/index.ts`), and every card is done. It's in the run card pool; a reprint is its own card there (unlocked separately from the original), and the 4-copy deck limit counts both, by name. `packages/campaign/src/base4.test.ts` checks the table against the TCGdex snapshot.

Legendary Collection (`effects/lc.ts`): every card reprints a Base Set, Jungle, Fossil, Team Rocket or Wizards promo card (`LC_REPRINTS`), so it takes the original's attacks, Trainer and special Energy effects (`lcAttacks`, `lcReprints`), and every card is done; it's in Gen 1's run pool and packs (agreed), after Gym Challenge. Its Pokémon Powers are the originals' (LC prints "affected by a Special Condition" where the originals say "Asleep, Confused, or Paralyzed", and every Pokémon Power now stops with any Special Condition anyway, agreed); only Haunter's Transparency differs, stopping with any Special Condition where Fossil's stops only while Asleep (`lcPowers`). TCGdex's LC data leaves out some damage (Dark Dragonite's Giant Tail, Dark Persian's Poison Claws, Dark Slowbro's Fickle Attack, Muk's Sludge, Beedrill's Poison Sting, Moltres's Dive Bomb, Kakuna's Poisonpowder) and has Omastar's Spike Cannon as Colorless: the printed values are in `lc.ts`. It misspells Pin Missile and Minimize, and spells Nidoran "Nidoran ♀" with a space (as the e-Card sets do), so they wouldn't evolve into Nidorina and Nidorino: the seed fixes these (`TCGDEX_FIXES`, `printedName`; a re-seed is needed, and until then `lc.ts` answers to the misspellings too). `packages/campaign/src/lc.test.ts` checks the table against the TCGdex snapshot, `lc.test.ts` in the engine the differences.

Jungle attacks: every Jungle Pokémon's attack effects are in `effects/jungle.ts`. Hurricane returns the Defending Pokémon and its cards to the hand unless it Knocks it Out. Chain Lightning does 10 to every benched Pokémon, on both sides, of the Defending Pokémon's type (none if it's Colorless). Teleport switches the attacker with a benched Pokémon the player clicks. Acid stops the Defending Pokémon retreating, Tail Wag and Leer stop it attacking that Pokémon, and Pounce and Snivel take 10 or 20 off its attacks on it, all during the next turn; benching or evolving either Pokémon ends them. Swords Dance makes Slash do 60 base damage on its owner's next turn. Big Eggsplosion flips a coin per Energy provided (Double Colorless = 2). Flareon's Flamethrower discards a Fire Energy card and Vaporeon's Water Gun counts Water Energy only (TCGdex drops those icons). Call for Family (Nidoran♀) finds either Nidoran; Call for Friend finds a Fighting Basic Pokémon.

Jungle Pokémon Powers (`effects/jungle-powers.ts`): Invisible Wall prevents attack damage of 30 or more to Mr. Mime, even from its owner's attacks, checked after weakness, resistance, PlusPower, Defender and other reductions. Thick Skinned stops Snorlax becoming Asleep, Confused, Paralyzed or Poisoned. Shift (once a turn) changes Venomoth's type to that of another Pokémon in play, not Colorless, until it leaves play or Shifts again. Heal (once a turn) flips a coin; on heads the player clicks one of their damaged Pokémon on the board to remove a damage counter (no choice if only one is damaged). Each working Retreat Aid on the Bench takes 1 off the Active Pokémon's retreat cost. Peek (once a turn) shows the player the top card of either deck, a random card from the opponent's hand, or a Prize card of either player they pick, until their next move; the log says only what they looked at.

Promos (`effects/promos.ts`, `promos-powers.ts`, `promos-trainers.ts`): every Wizards Black Star Promo (1–53) is implemented. Mewtwo (Wizards Promo 3 and 14)'s Energy Absorption attaches 1 or 2 Energy cards from the discard pile (it needs one there). Notes on the rest:
- **Baby Pokémon** (Cleffa, Pichu, Igglybuff; `baby` on the card, set by the API since TCGdex lists the promos as Basic): attacking an Active Baby takes a coin flip first, and tails ends the attacker's turn. A Baby evolves only into a card printed "Evolves from" it; none of the WOTC cards in the catalog are (TCGdex's "evolves from Pichu" on Basic Pikachu is Pokédex data, not printed). Pichu's Let's Play! puts a Baby from the deck onto the Bench.
- **Stadiums** (Lucky Stadium, Pokémon Tower): one is in play at a time for both players (`GameState.stadium`, shown face up left of your Active Pokémon). A new one sends the old one to its owner's discard pile, and one with the same name can't be played over it. Lucky Stadium: once during each player's turn, a flip; heads draws a card (`USE_STADIUM`). Pokémon Tower: Trainers that would put cards from a discard pile into the hand (Energy Retrieval, Item Finder, Super Energy Retrieval) can't be played, and attacks that would (Energy Conversion, Scavenge) leave the cards where they are.
- **Computer Error**: the player draws 0–5, then the opponent does (the CPU always takes 5; the player is asked during the CPU's turn), and the turn ends.
- **Rulings agreed**: it's never your birthday (\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_'s Pikachu logs "It's not your birthday, liar"). Lightning Bolt does 20 instead of 10 to a Defending Pokémon with a Lightning Rod marker and 20 to each marked benched one, with Weakness and Resistance. Pure Body: the player picks which Energy card leaves Suicune, the new Water Energy included. Chain Reaction is offered to each Eevee's owner after any Pokémon evolves, either player's, under the usual evolution rules. Unown [J]'s Join works as printed, though Unown O, I and N aren't in the run card pool. Marill's Water Gun counts any Energy (its text names no type). On tails, Cat Punch's 20 damage goes to the benched Pokémon the opponent picks (the CPU picks the one with the most HP left).
- **New mechanics**: Light Screen (half damage, rounded down, after everything else), Togepi's Snivel (20 less before Weakness), Mini-Metronome (copies on heads, as Colorless, its discard still paid), Devolution Beam (either player's Evolved Pokémon), Neutral Shield (attacks by Evolved Pokémon), Protective Flame and Aurora Veil (attacks can't affect the Bench), Guard (the Defending Pokémon can't retreat), Bolt (shuffled away after the attack), Paint (a Coloring counter changes the type until it leaves play), Lightning Rod markers (stay until it leaves play). Aurora Veil and Guard also stop while Poisoned or Burned.
- TCGdex leaves out the printed damage of Poison Claws (10) and Sacred Fire (60) and drops Energy icons from Recharge, Flames of Rage, Hyper Flame, Burning Fire and Mini-Metronome; the effects carry the printed values (`damage`, `text`).

Jigglypuff (Wizards Promo 7) has First Aid and Double-Edge. Super Energy Retrieval (`effects/neo1-trainers.ts`, the Neo Genesis printing, standing in for the deck's Japanese Fan Book promo) trades 2 other cards from the hand for 4 basic Energy cards from the discard pile, or all of them if there are fewer.

Jungle Trainers (`effects/jungle-trainers.ts`): Poké Ball needs a card in the deck; on heads the player takes a Basic Pokémon or Evolution card from the deck (they must when there is one), and the log names the card taken, as it's shown to the opponent.

Fossil attacks: every Fossil Pokémon's attack effects are in `effects/fossil.ts`. Damage to benched Pokémon (Dark Mind, Stretch Kick, Gigashock, Thunderstorm, Blizzard, Selfdestruct, Earthquake) ignores weakness and resistance, but Defender still takes 20 off it, as it does off damage an attack does to the attacker itself (Selfdestruct, Thunder, Double-edge…, and the 20 a Confused Pokémon does to itself on tails). Headache works even if the Defending Pokémon is protected by Agility or Barrier. Energy Conversion needs an Energy card in the discard pile and takes 1 or 2 of them. TCGdex drops the energy icons from card text ("each Energy" for "each Water Energy"), so effects carry the printed wording: Fossil's Water Guns count extra Water Energy only, and Scavenge discards a Psychic Energy card. Minimize takes 20 off attack damage after weakness, resistance, PlusPower and Defender. Smokescreen works like Sand-attack. Headache stops the opponent playing Trainer cards, Clefairy Doll included, during their next turn; it's on the player, so switching doesn't end it. Prophecy (either deck) and Call for Family leave a search to finish, and the turn ends once it's done. Terror Strike, like Whirlwind and Ram, has the opponent choose which benched Pokémon comes in.

Fossil Pokémon Powers (`effects/fossil-powers.ts`): Toxic Gas makes every other power stop working, on both sides, while Muk has no Special Condition. Prehistoric Power stops both players playing Evolution cards (Pokémon Breeder included). Ditto's Transform, while it's the active and has no Special Condition, makes it a copy of the Defending Pokémon: type, HP, Weakness, Resistance, retreat cost, attacks and power; its Energy counts as any type; damage stays, so it's Knocked Out if it stops being a copy with more damage than its own 50 HP. Step In and Curse can be used once per turn per Pokémon; Curse can Knock Out, and the opponent then promotes without the turn ending. Strange Behavior can't Knock Out Slowbro. Transparency flips once per attack against any attack that does anything to Haunter, even its owner's, and only Sleep stops it. Kabuto Armor halves attack damage, rounded down to the nearest 10, after weakness, resistance, PlusPower and Defender. Clairvoyance shows the opponent's hand in the player's view. Cowardice returns Tentacool to the hand (not the turn it was put into play, nor your only Pokémon), discarding its cards.

Fossil Trainers (`effects/fossil-trainers.ts`): Mr. Fuji shuffles a benched Pokémon and everything attached into the deck. Energy Search takes a basic Energy card from the deck. Gambler shuffles the hand into the deck, then draws 8 on heads or 1 on tails. Recycle needs a card in the discard pile; on heads the player picks one (not Recycle itself) to put on top of the deck. Mysterious Fossil follows Clefairy Doll's rules (a 10 HP Pokémon on the Bench, never the starting Active, no prize when Knocked Out, can be discarded during your turn). Omanyte, Kabuto and Aerodactyl evolve from it, and Pokémon Breeder can put Omastar or Kabutops straight onto it. TCGdex leaves out "evolves from Mysterious Fossil", so `apps/api/src/cards.ts` fills it in.

## Runs

An account plays runs, one at a time. A run is steps of encounters, choosing one of 1–3 at each step (you can see what later steps offer), with bosses along the way: every gym leader comes after one of their gym's trainers, who must be fought (except in the Gym Challenge and Champion runs), and the run is beaten with its last boss. Step rules (agreed): at least 4 steps are a required battle, which can't be skipped (`REQUIRED_BATTLES`; the first step always is one, the others are random); a run offers at most 2 Pokémon Centers (`MAX_CENTERS`); the other steps offer 1, 2 or 3 options (weights 4:2:1, `OPTION_COUNTS`), so a step offering a non-battle encounter has only that one about half the time (a lone shop or gift is simply where the road goes). A step offering a single trainer and nothing else (required or rolled) offers a second trainer to choose between 75% of the time (`PAIR_CHANCE`, agreed; steps with a non-battle option keep their options), so about two thirds of the steps offering a trainer battle offer two in a 10-encounter run; one of them must still be fought. The pure logic is in `packages/campaign` (`RunState` and its reducer `applyRunAction`, like the engine's), the API saves it in the `Run` table, and the web shows it on the home screen.

**Run types** (`run-types.ts`):

| Run | Steps | Bosses |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pewter, Cerulean, Vermilion, Celadon, Fuchsia, Saffron, Cinnabar and Viridian City | 10 encounters along the city's route (one per spot), then inside the gym the Gym Trainer and the gym leader (12) | Brock, Misty, Lt. Surge, Erika, Koga, Sabrina, Blaine or Giovanni |
| Kanto Gym Challenge | The 8 gym leaders back to back, no encounters or Gym Trainers, with a Pokémon Center after the fourth (9) | All eight gym leaders |
| Kanto Champion | The 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four and the Champion back to back, no encounters or Gym Trainers (13) | All eight, Lorelei, Bruno, Agatha, Lance and Champion Blue |
| Violet City, Azalea Town, Goldenrod, Ecruteak, Cianwood and Olivine City, Mahogany Town, Blackthorn City (Gen 2) | 10 encounters along the city's HeartGold/SoulSilver map (one per spot), then inside the gym the Gym Trainer and the gym leader (12) | Falkner, Bugsy, Whitney, Morty, Chuck, Jasmine, Pryce or Clair |
| Johto Gym Challenge (Gen 2) | As Kanto's: the 8 gym leaders back to back, a Pokémon Center after the fourth | All eight Johto gym leaders |
| Johto Champion (Gen 2) | As Kanto's: the 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four and Champion Lance back to back | All eight, Will, Koga, Bruno, Karen and Champion Lance |
| Rustboro City, Dewford Town, Mauville City, Lavaridge Town, Petalburg, Fortree, Mossdeep and Sootopolis City (Gen 3) | 10 encounters on the city's Emerald map, then inside the gym the Gym Trainer and the gym leader (12) | Roxanne, Brawly, Wattson, Flannery, Norman, Winona, Tate & Liza or Juan |
| Hoenn Gym Challenge (Gen 3) | As Kanto's: the 8 gym leaders back to back, a Pokémon Center after the fourth | All eight Hoenn gym leaders |
| Hoenn Champion (Gen 3) | As Kanto's: the 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four and Champion Wallace back to back | All eight, Sidney, Phoebe, Glacia, Drake and Champion Wallace |

**Routes** (agreed; every Kanto gym run, Johto's to follow): a run type can have routes (`routes` on `RunType`, shown one after another; `routes.ts`): a FireRed map drawn from the pret/pokefirered decompilation's layout and tilesets (`tools/routes/render_route.py`) and one spot per encounter step. The run screen then shows the map zoomed in on the step's spot (`RouteView`) in place of the icon map. Pewter City's is Route 2 (`ROUTE_2`, `public/routes/route2.png`): 10 spots across the main road past Viridian Forest's gates and the east strip by Diglett's Cave, picked on walkable tiles (`tools/routes/pick_spots.py`: evenly spaced from Viridian City at the bottom to Pewter City at the top, alternating sides where both can be walked, in the open). Each route keeps its walkability grid (from the map's collision data, ledges, water, doorways and its objects such as cut-trees and item balls left out), and a test checks every spot is walkable (`onPath`). Runs saved before a route was added keep their steps. Every Kanto gym run now has its maps: the route the player arrives on in FireRed (Route 4 from Mt. Moon for Cerulean, Route 5 to Saffron's gatehouse then Route 6 for Vermilion (agreed: 3 encounters on Route 5, `ROUTE_5`, leaving by the gatehouse to Saffron, 24,32, its `exit`; then 7 on Route 6, coming in at Saffron's south gate, its `entrance`, the first at 13,8), Route 8 for Celadon (the user's choice, from Lavender Town in the east), and, agreed with the user: the Pokémon Tower 1F to 5F for Saffron (2 encounters a floor, the player coming in by the stairs on the left, 1F by its door at the bottom, and leaving by the stairs on the right: `entrance` and `exit`, the stairs and door tiles walkable), Route 17 (the Cycling Road) for Fuchsia, ridden on a bike (`travel: 'bike'`: Red's bike sprite, every walk twice as fast) with most of its trainers Bikers, Route 20 for Cinnabar, surfed (`travel: 'surf'`: its water `~` in `walkable`, which only a route tagged for surfing can walk on (water stays unwalkable otherwise), from `render_route.py`'s `water`; Red surfing on water, walking on land) with Swimmers in the water (their swimming sprites; Swimmers are met only on surfed routes, never on any other: `LAND_TRAINERS` in run.ts, agreed), its spots with no land within 3 tiles offering only battles, those with land near (but the land battle's) only non-battle encounters (never required battles), and everyone else (non-battle encounters' NPCs, other trainers) always standing on land, never walking into water, but for one battle on land by the Seafoam Islands' west cave against any other trainer (`landBattle`, always a battle), the caves linking the waters either side of the islands (a fade), and the Power Plant for Viridian, most of its trainers Team Rocket Grunts (men and women, decks of many types: `Rocket Grunt` in `trainerFavorites`); a route's `trainers` (`names` and the `share` of its battles they take, the rest any other trainer) set who's met there. Saffron's run comes before Fuchsia's in Gen 1 (`KANTO_GYMS`, the Gym Challenge's order too). The new maps have every object's tile walkable) and the gym's interior (`kanto-routes.ts`), drawn by `tools/routes/` with item balls and cut-trees walkable (and in a gym, every FireRed trainer's tile but the Gym Guy's). The gym puzzles (Vermilion's electric barrier, Fuchsia's invisible walls, Saffron's teleport pads, Cinnabar's quiz doors) and routes' ledges, fences and gatehouses split some maps into areas Red can't walk between, so he fades between them until the user gives their links or walkable tiles. The gym's steps are a run's last (counted from its end, so runs made with 24 encounters still show the gym at the end).

**Johto route maps** (agreed): each Johto gym run crosses one map, 10 encounters, then its gym (see Johto gym interiors): Route 30 (Violet City, from Cherrygrove), 32 (Azalea Town), 34 (Goldenrod City), 35 (Ecruteak City), 40 (Cianwood City), 42 (Olivine City), 44 (Mahogany Town) and 45 (Blackthorn City), in `johto-routes.ts`. HeartGold/SoulSilver's maps are 3D, so `tools/routes/hgss/hgss_map.py` takes a map's terrain chunks from the pret/pokeheartgold decompilation (its map matrix, land data and the area's texture set), converts them and their buildings to GLB with apicula (github.com/scurest/apicula, the Nintendo DS model converter) and draws them with `render_hgss.py`, a small software renderer looking down with a slight tilt (agreed: vertical faces show, as in the games; the ground lies on the 16 px tile grid), applying each material's texture matrix, which apicula leaves out (`texture_matrices`: the shorelines' wave band is shifted by one), and writes which tiles can be walked on from each chunk's tile permissions (collision, ledges, water, doors; people and item balls aren't map data, so their tiles are walkable). Spots are picked along the walk from the start edge to the far end. Route 45 goes top to bottom with its ledges jumped going down only (agreed; `v` in `walkable`), 24 of them moved down one to three rows from where the map data has them to where they're drawn (the user's: `USER_LEDGE_MOVES` in johto_routes.py), its 8th spot at 17,105 below one, its 10th at 6,144 (its figures left and right of it, then above), its exit at 0,142 on the left edge (opened: the map data has it as wall), and 20-22,134-135 walkable (the user's: `USER_OPEN`). A ledge is never stood on: the player jumps over it, from the tile above to the one below, in one hop (the user's; `routePath`'s `jump` steps), as FireRed does: two steps' time, his sprite walking as it rises and falls along FireRed's own heights (`JUMP_Y`, its "high" jump), its shadow on the ground under him (`JumpShadow`) and a puff of dust where he lands (`JumpDust`), FireRed's shadow and dust on every map (the user's; `tools/routes/jump_effects.py`). Three are made up (agreed), by `generate_johto.py`: Route 40 is surfed from Olivine's beach (the real route's beach chunk, to its shore) down a sea lane between two columns of rocks as near the edges as they go, nudged in and out at random, with sandbars of random sizes and shapes at random places across it, some against the rocks (built as the real one is, from its textures: a flat sand top and a wave band sloping into the sea round it) for non-battle encounters, and a beach across the south end, its light sand along the water (4 or 5 tiles deep, the user's) giving way to dry sand behind at random, with the real beach's rim between (straight, and curving round corners both ways), and two of the real beach's rocks on the dry sand, near its ends, and a few of its pebble marks (road01_sub, as at the beach's 12,15) (agreed), and grass behind the dry sand to the map's bottom edge as on Cianwood City's beach (grass01, with the dry sand's rim against it, road01_r), Cianwood's little sprouts on the light sand (beach_sub) and its tufts on the grass (grass01_a) (the user's; their own random numbers, so the rest of the map didn't move), its trainers Swimmers; Route 42 is a cave from left to right in Mt. Mortar's textures, its rock built as HGSS's caves are (each tier a tile high, a sloped wall on every side with Mt. Mortar's wall profile and shading, `dwall_n/s/e/w`, mitred at corners; the floor's rim along the walls, `droad01_r`, its inside corner where two walls meet, swept round in a quarter circle where rock is only across a corner; the rock past the last tier fading to black), with a small waterfall (HGSS's `dsm_fal1`) falling 3 tiles between two pools on two levels: the upper one up on the north wall's third tier in a small floor of its own (its shore blending into it), the walls one field of tiers from both floors so they run on into the raised floor and stay joined (`raised` in `cave`), running down between bare rock to the fall's edge; the waterfall model squashed to the 3-tile drop, as Mt. Mortar's does; the waterfall drawn in full colour with Mt. Mortar's own waterfall textures, as the model alone comes out dark) into a pool whose stream (6 wide) the path crosses by a bridge (Route 48's, `plank_bridge`: its `bridge_a` planks and their `f_kage` shadow on the water, 60% opaque; the user's), the cave's water a full tile down (`cave`'s `depth`, its shore stretched down to it; the user's); its water is half a tile below the floor with Mt. Mortar's shore round it (`dpond_line` and the wash under it, `sea_line02`, mitred into corners and swept round the corners that jut out), and its floor has Mt. Moon's other ground patches (`droad01_sub`, `droad01_sub2`) and clumps of its crystal rocks (`drock01s`, blocking their tiles) only against the walls, never by water, a spot or in the way (`moon_floor`) (agreed); then up the third chamber's ladder (HGSS's own cave ladder models, `dun_stairup` leaning on the wall and `dun_stairdw` in a lit pit copied from Mt. Moon's, the floor round it cut away inside its rim as the real caves' is so its shadowed sides show (`pit_cut`, Route 44's too); the ladder the map's `exit`) into a fourth chamber, its own map (`ROUTE_42_UPPER`, "Route 42 (upper)", in at the hole; the user's), with a pool, leaving by its right edge, encounters 9 and 10 there (the user's; the cave rendered once and cut between the two, `write`'s `band`); Route 44 is an ice cave from the bottom up in the Ice Path's textures, its ice slid across (agreed with the user: the route's `ice` rows; `routePath` slides on the way the player was going until the next tile is a wall, a rock or someone standing there, or he's onto ground, its steps `slide`, Red held mid-step, legs out, as he glides; ice cyan on the debug overlay; a test checks every encounter can still be walked to), in two maps (the user's): the lower area (`ROUTE_44`) up to its ladder, its `exit`, faded from to the upper area (`ROUTE_44_UPPER`, "Route 44 (upper)", in at the ladder's hole), 5 encounters each (the same HGSS ladder models as Route 42's; each map rendered with the other area as rock and cropped to its own, `write`'s `trim`), its ice drawn as Ice Path B2F's (see-through over deep blue, the scalloped edge `ice_fe01r` where it meets the floor) and the ring round its hole B2F's own; a raised area two tiers up (encounters 4 and 5 on it; its cliffs two tiles deep) with B2F's stairs (`dslope`, one a tier) at each end, drawn on its own tiles (`lift`: the tilt would otherwise draw it 0.6 of a tile up its cliff), and Ice Path's big and small icy rocks (`drock01`, `drock01s`) and patches of other ground (`droad01_sub`, `droad01_sub2`, by `other_ground`, shared with Route 42's). The Johto maps use HeartGold/SoulSilver's overworld sprites (`public/overworld/hgss/`, by `hgss_sprites.py` from the decompilation's `data/mmodel`): 32x32 strips of 12 frames, standing and walking for down, up, left and right (agreed: their size as in HGSS, right not mirrored); Ethan walks, rides his bike and surfs (on HGSS's surf Pokémon, the field model `sea_ride`, drawn facing each way, with the surf effect's foam under it the whole time he moves (his walk cycle there uses only the frames with it); standing still he bobs on the spot without it: `bobs`, `bobFrame` and `BOB_MS` in overworld.ts, the strip's 4 extra frames); each trainer class and non-battle encounter has HGSS's nearest sprite (`trainerSprite`, `encounterSprite`). The tools need Python with numpy and Pillow, and apicula.

**The user's Johto route fixes** live in `johto_routes.py`, so a regenerated map keeps them: `USER_SPOTS` (a spot moved: Route 32's 5th to 5,74), `USER_BLOCKED` (tiles that can't be walked on), `USER_GRASS` and `USER_NOT_GRASS` (tall grass the map data misses or has wrong), and `USER_EXITS` (where the player leaves for the gym: Route 32's Union Cave mouth, 14,143); the gym tool's are `USER_GYM_TRAINER` and `USER_LINKS` (Azalea Gym's Spinarak carts) in `johto_gyms.py`.

**Johto gym interiors** (agreed): every Johto gym run ends inside its gym, as the Kanto runs do: the leader's room from HeartGold/SoulSilver (Azalea's and Mahogany's gyms are several maps; only the leader's room is shown, agreed), drawn as the Johto routes are and cropped to the room (`johto-gyms.ts`, `apps/web/public/routes/<city>_gym.png`, by `tools/routes/hgss/johto_gyms.py`). From each map's events: the player comes in at the door, walks to the Gym Trainer, who stands where the gym's trainer nearest halfway in stands (Olivine's, a gym with no trainers, where its girl stands), then to the leader in their place, with the Gym Guide by the door where the gym has one. Each Johto gym's Gym Trainer is picked from the trainers in that gym in HGSS, every room of it (`trainers` on the leader's `gym`, johto-bosses.ts; not Azalea's Twins, a double battle), Olivine's from the Olivine Lighthouse's (agreed), with HGSS's sprites (Mediums, Skiers, Boarders and the Gym Guide added to `public/overworld/hgss/`, by hgss_sprites.py from the decompilation's mmodels 83, 22, 177 and 57). Azalea's floors are crossed on Spinarak carts, which the map data doesn't show, so they're the user's links (`USER_LINKS` in johto_gyms.py): the player walks from the door to 9,33, fades into 9,23 and walks to the Gym Trainer (the user's: the player at 9,20, the trainer beside them at 7,20, `USER_GYM_TRAINER`), then walks to 9,18 and fades into 9,8, below Bugsy (a test checks every Johto gym can be walked). Mahogany Gym's ice is slid across as Route 44's is (agreed with the user): `hgss_map.py` reads it from the map's tile behaviours (32, `TILE_BEHAVIOR_ICE`, into the map's `ice` rows), and its three ice blocks (the map's `SPRITE_ICE` objects, drawn with HGSS's ice block sprite, `hgss/ice`, mmodel 195) block their tiles; with the Gym Trainer standing on the ice, they stop the player's slides so Pryce can be reached, as in HGSS (a test checks it). Blackthorn Gym's lava is crossed in HGSS by moving platforms (its gymmick, not drawn), so it's walked only on its four floors (`USER_FLOORS`; the lava is walkable in the map data) and crossed by warp panels, the user's links: its own four pair with two the user added (`USER_PADS`: copies of the one by the door, cut from the render against the floor beside it and pasted on 12,64 and 13,38): the door's floor's panel (7,83) to the middle floor's added one, the middle floor's own (2,62) to the Gym Trainer's floor (22,41), and that floor's added one to Clair's (4,9). The Johto Gym Challenge and Champion runs have a map per step too, as Kanto's (`JOHTO_BOSS_ROOMS`, `bossMaps` in run-types.ts): each leader in their gym (`leaderRoom`, now in routes.ts, the player coming in 5 tiles from them, south where it can), the Pokémon League's rooms for Will, Koga, Bruno, Karen and Lance (`hgss_pokemonleague_<boss>s_room.png`), each in their place with the player coming in 5 tiles south of them, and for the Gym Challenge's Pokémon Center after Morty, Ecruteak's (`JOHTO_POKEMON_CENTER`, `hgss_ecruteak_pokemoncenter_1f.png`: in at the door, up to the counter), all made by johto_gyms.py.

**Bridges overhead** (agreed): a map's `overhead` image is drawn over every sprite (below the bubbles and "!"), so the people walking under it are hidden, as in the games: Route 32's Magnet Train bridge (its girders and deck, the materials `overpass` and `lambert76`; not its pillars), rendered alone and see-through elsewhere by `hgss_map.py --overhead` (`OVERHEAD` in johto_routes.py, `route32_overhead.png`); and Olivine Gym's steel arches and their gems (`tekkin` and the gems' materials, from row 9 down; `USER_OVERHEAD` in johto_gyms.py, `olivine_gym_overhead.png`).

**Goldenrod Gym's track** (the user's): the gym is walked along one fixed line (`track` on the Route, built with `trackLine` from its corners; `USER_TRACKS` in johto_gyms.py), its tiles the only walkable ones: from the door up the left stairs to the Gym Trainer (who stands at 6,10, the player stopping beside them at 7,10), over the green walkways (bridges) round to the right, back under the bridges at 19,9–19,10 and 13,8 and up to Whitney. `routePath` follows the track between two of its tiles (never turning where it crosses itself), however long the walk; the tiles walked under a bridge are `under`, and only while the player is on one is the map's `overhead` (`goldenrod_gym_overhead.png`, the gym's bridge tiles 12–14,8–9 and 18–20,9–10 cut from its image) drawn over him.

**Tall grass** (agreed): people standing in tall grass on the route map (the player, the encounters' figures, a gym's guide; not an item ball) are drawn half hidden, as in the games: the bottom of their sprite (`GRASS_HIDES`, half a tile) is hidden so the grass on the map shows there, fading out over 4 map pixels across the line rather than a hard edge (`GRASS_FADE`, `GRASS_MASK`: a CSS mask; the user's) (`OverworldSprite`'s `grass`, from `inGrass`); walking or jumping into it, only once the step or jump has brought them onto the grass tile, and walking out of it, at once (the user's). The player also wades through it a little slower: he moves 30% slower onto grass tiles, his walking frames slowed to match (`GRASS_PACE`). Each map's grass is `grass` on its `Route` ('g' rows, as `walkable`), from the game's tile behaviours: FireRed's `MB_TALL_GRASS` (`tools/routes/kanto_grass.py`, which renders each Kanto route with render_route.py and writes only its `grass` rows into kanto-routes.ts and routes.ts: Routes 2, 4, 5, 6 and 8) and HGSS's tall and very tall grass, behaviours 2 and 3 (`hgss_map.py`, written by johto_routes.py: Routes 30, 32, 34, 35 and 45).

**Generations** (agreed): runs belong to a generation (`GENERATIONS` in `run-types.ts`): Gen 1 is Kanto, Gen 2 Johto, Gen 3 Hoenn. Each has its own sets (`poolSets`): Gen 1's are Base Set to Gym Challenge and Legendary Collection (its Gen 1 reprints, agreed), Gen 2's Neo Genesis, Neo Discovery, Southern Islands, Neo Revelation, Neo Destiny, Expedition, Aquapolis and Skyridge (`GEN2_SETS`), Gen 3's the EX sets, EX Ruby & Sapphire to EX Power Keepers (`GEN3_SETS`). A run's cards (gifts, rewards, shops, trades, events) and its trainers' random decks come only from its generation's sets (`cardPool(gen)`/`runPool` in `apps/api/src/pool.ts`), and the random decks use the generation's basic Energy and Trainers (`DeckRules`: `GEN1_DECK_RULES`, `GEN2_DECK_RULES`, `GEN3_DECK_RULES` in `decks.ts`; Gen 2 has no Double Colorless or Pokémon Breeder; Gen 3 has no Double Colorless, and Rare Candy in Pokémon Breeder's place). Every Gen 2 set's card effects are done (`GEN2_DONE_SETS`: Neo Genesis to Skyridge). A generation's pool takes only the cards the engine plays fully from its finished sets (`implementedSets`: all of Gen 1's and Gen 2's), and every card of the others, implemented or not: their Pokémon attack for base damage, and their Trainers can't be played yet. The gym leader CPU plays every Gen 2 card, the Trainer CPU its short list (see The CPU opponent). The trainer classes' favourite Pokémon (`trainerFavorites`) list Johto Pokémon after Kanto's, and Hoenn's after them, so Gen 2's and Gen 3's trainers get themed decks too. Gen 3's sets are all done (`GEN3_DONE_SETS`), so its pool is every card the engine plays fully, and the Trainer CPU's short list has Gen 3's simple Trainers and special Energy too.

**Unlocking runs and packs** (agreed): Kanto's gym runs are open from the start. Each later generation's runs need the previous generation's Gym Challenge beaten (agreed 2026-10-24: the Kanto Gym Challenge opens Johto's 8 gym runs, the Johto Gym Challenge Hoenn's; `generationLock`), and until then the run picker's Generation dropdown lists the generation, saying what opens it, but it can't be picked. The Kanto Gym Challenge needs all 8 Kanto badges (key items, kept by the account), and Kanto Champion a beaten Kanto Gym Challenge, as in every generation (`runTypeLock`; `run.overview` lists each run type with `locked` and how often it's been `beaten`, and `run.start` refuses a locked one). Every run type belongs to a generation (`GENERATIONS` in `run-types.ts`: its region, gyms, Gym Challenge and its sets' packs in release order; Kanto's are Base Set, Jungle, the Wizards promos, Fossil, Base Set 2, Team Rocket, Gym Heroes and Gym Challenge). An account starts with no booster packs: a run's first battle completed without conceding it unlocks the next pack of its generation (`nextPack`), and beating a run unlocks all of them (`apps/api/src/progress.ts`, saved as `PackUnlock` rows when the run is saved; the run log says "Unlocked … booster packs."). Gen 2's runs will unlock packs from Neo Genesis on. The migration `20261024090000_pack_unlocks` gave existing accounts the packs their runs so far would have: all of Kanto's after a beaten run, otherwise one for each run with a battle finished.

The overworld sprites in `apps/web/public/trainers/` are the bosses' front-facing frames from FireRed/LeafGreen (the remake of Red and Blue), taken from the [pret/pokefirered](https://github.com/pret/pokefirered) decompilation (`graphics/object_events/pics/people/`, with their in-game palettes), the background made transparent and trimmed. A later generation uses its remake's sprites where it has one.

**Johto's bosses** (`johto-bosses.ts`, merged into `BOSSES`): Falkner, Bugsy, Whitney, Morty, Chuck, Jasmine, Pryce and Clair, the Elite Four Will, Koga, Bruno and Karen, and Champion Lance (boss ids `johto-koga`, `johto-bruno` and `johto-lance`, as Kanto has its own Koga, Bruno and Lance). No Game Boy game plays the Neo and e-Card sets, so their decks are our own: each built around the character's team in Gold, Silver and Crystal (and HeartGold and SoulSilver), from Gen 2's sets only, with Neo Genesis's basic Energy, Darkness or Metal Energy where they need it, and Gen 2's Trainers (Professor Elm, Pokémon March, Gold Berry…). A gym whose type has no basic Energy (Falkner's and Whitney's Colorless, Jasmine's Metal) gives its Gym Trainer a deck built from its favourite Pokémon (`gym.favorites`), and such decks name their types for the run picker (`types`). Beating them gives the Johto badges (Zephyr to Rising) and the Johto Champion Ribbon. Their run picker sprites are their HeartGold/SoulSilver overworld sprites (`public/trainers/`, each standing facing down, cropped to the figure: the [pret/pokeheartgold](https://github.com/pret/pokeheartgold) decompilation's GSLEADER1–8, GSBIGFOUR1–4 and WATARU sheets, by `tools/routes/hgss/hgss_sprites.py`'s `boss` layout; they replaced Crystal's).

**Hoenn maps** (Gen 3, from Pokémon R/S/E): each Hoenn gym run crosses one Emerald map, 10 encounters, then its gym, in the order Emerald visits them: Petalburg Woods (Rustboro City, from Route 104 at the bottom), Route 106 (Dewford Town, surfed from the west), Route 110 (Mauville City, up the Seaside Cycling Road on a bike, most of its trainers its Triathletes, `TRIATHLETES`), Jagged Pass (Lavaridge Town, down from Mt. Chimney), Route 102 (Petalburg City, from Oldale Town in the east), Route 119 (Fortree City, up along the river), Route 124 (Mossdeep City, surfed from Lilycove) and Sootopolis City (from the top of the crater down through the city, then surfing its lake to the gym's island). The surfed maps' trainers are Swimmers, and their spots follow the surf rules (out at sea only battles, by the shore only non-battle encounters), but Sootopolis's spots on land are ordinary ones (`landSpots` on its `Route`: any encounter, battles against anyone but a Swimmer). Emerald is a GBA game like FireRed, so `tools/routes/emerald/emerald_map.py` draws a map from a checkout of the [pret/pokeemerald](https://github.com/pret/pokeemerald) decompilation as `render_route.py` does FireRed's, with Emerald's tileset sizes (512 tiles and metatiles in a primary tileset, 6 palettes) and its 16-bit metatile attributes, and writes which tiles can be walked on, the tall grass and the water. `hoenn_maps.py` picks each map's spots along the walk from the edge the run starts at (`hoenn-routes.ts`). Each gym is the leader's map (`hoenn-gyms.ts`), with, from its events, the door, the Gym Trainer where the gym's trainer nearest halfway in along the walk to the leader stands (Sootopolis's and Lavaridge's, whose trainers aren't on that walk, beside it halfway), the leader in their place, and the Gym Guide; Petalburg Gym's rooms are joined through their doors (`links`), ledges in a gym are walked either way (Lavaridge's sand slopes), and Mauville Gym's electric barriers across the middle are open (the gym puzzles the map data doesn't show solved). The Hoenn Gym Challenge and Champion runs have a map per step as Kanto's: each leader in their gym, the Elite Four's and Wallace's rooms at the Pokémon League, and Lavaridge Town's Pokémon Center (`HOENN_BOSS_ROOMS`, `HOENN_POKEMON_CENTER`). The maps use Emerald's overworld sprites (`sprites: 'rse'`, `public/overworld/rse/`, by `emerald_sprites.py`: each sprite's frames in its frame table's order, with its palette): Brendan walks, rides his bike and surfs on the surf blob; each trainer class and non-battle encounter has Emerald's nearest sprite, and the Hoenn gyms' trainers the sprites Emerald draws them with. The run picker's Hoenn bosses are their Emerald overworld sprites' front-facing frames (Tate and Liza side by side), and the Hoenn badges are PokeAPI's badge artwork, as the others are.

**Hoenn's bosses** (`hoenn-bosses.ts`, merged into `BOSSES`): Roxanne, Brawly, Wattson, Flannery, Norman, Winona, Tate & Liza (boss id `tate-liza`) and Juan, the Elite Four Sidney, Phoebe, Glacia and Drake, and Champion Wallace, as Emerald has them. Their decks are our own, as Johto's are: each built around the character's team in Emerald, from the EX sets only, with EX Ruby & Sapphire's basic Energy, special Energy where they need it (Sidney's Darkness, Dark Metal and Multi Energy), Gen 3's Trainers (Professor Birch, Steven's Advice, Rare Candy, Great Ball…) and their own Stadium for the Elite Four (Sidney's, Phoebe's, Glacia's and Drake's Stadium), each with one Pokémon-ex at most as its star. Their Gym Trainers are the trainers in their gym in Emerald (every room's). Norman's and Winona's gyms (Normal and Flying) have no basic Energy type, so their Gym Trainers' decks are built from the gym's favourite Pokémon. Their badges are the Stone, Knuckle, Dynamo, Heat, Balance, Feather, Mind and Rain Badges, and Wallace's prize the Hoenn Champion Ribbon.

**Bosses** (`bosses.ts`) play their counterpart's deck from the Game Boy Color *Pokémon Trading Card Game* (TCG 1), named for the Generation I character: Brock (Gene's Rock Crusher), Misty (Amy's Rain Dancer), Lt. Surge (Isaac's Zapping Selfdestruct), Erika (Nikki's Flower Power), Koga (Rick's Wonders of Science), Sabrina (Murray's Strange Psyshock), Blaine (Ken's Fire Charge), Giovanni (Mitch's First-Strike); the Grand Masters' Legendary decks for the Elite Four, Lorelei (Jack's Articuno, Ice), Lance (Rod's Dragonite, Dragon), and by elimination Bruno (Steve's Zapdos) and Agatha (Courtney's Moltres); and Ronald's Legendary Ronald Deck for Champion Blue. Cards that only exist in the Game Boy game (its promos, its "Mystery" and "Laboratory" prints and the Japanese Fan Book card) are replaced by the closest TCGdex card of the same type, noted on each deck (e.g. Fossil Moltres for the promo Moltres, Fossil Dragonite for the promo Dragonite, Jungle's Eeveelutions, Neo Genesis Super Energy Retrieval). The Japanese-only Pokémon Web Electrode (Lt. Surge, Bruno) is the Base Set Electrode instead. Ronald's promo Articuno and Zapdos are Moltres instead, as no Water or Lightning Pokémon can attack with his Fire and Double Colorless Energy. Bulbapedia's First-Strike and Rain Dancer lists add up to 61, so each has one basic Energy fewer. Bosses play at Gym leader difficulty; a loss costs 1 HP and they're fought again. Beating one gives more ₱ and EXP, a choice of rarer cards, and their badge (or, for Blue, the Champion Ribbon) as a key item at once.

| File (`packages/campaign/src/`) | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `run.ts` | `RunState`, `createRun` (the map from a seed and the run type's segments), `applyRunAction` (choosing, encounters, battle results, bosses and badges, items and their effects, the testing retry), `battleSetup` (the CPU's deck, difficulty and battle rules, with the player's items as rules) and `runView` (what the player sees) |
| `decks.ts` | `STARTER_DECK`, `generateDeck` (random medium-strength decks; one type only for a Gym Trainer; built from a trainer's favourite Pokémon, `trainerFavorites`, when they have them) and `deckErrors` |
| `packs.ts` | Booster packs: `PACK_SETS`, `PACK_PRICE` and `openPack` (a rare, holo 1 in 3, 3 uncommons, 6 commons and a basic Energy; for the promos, which are all Common or holo, 9 or 10 commons and a holo 1 in 3) |
| `bosses.ts` | The bosses (`BOSSES`: Kanto's 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four, Champion Blue, and Johto's and Hoenn's from `johto-bosses.ts` and `hoenn-bosses.ts`) with their decks, gyms and badges |
| `hoenn-bosses.ts` | Hoenn's bosses for Gen 3 runs: 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four and Champion Wallace, with decks of EX cards (see Hoenn's bosses) |
| `johto-bosses.ts` | Johto's bosses for Gen 2 runs: 8 gym leaders, the Elite Four and Champion Lance, with decks of Gen 2 cards |
| `cosmetics.ts` | The cosmetics (`COSMETICS`): card backs, playmats and coins, each the default, sold for ₱, or unlocked by beating a run type; `hasCosmetic` (all of them while debugging) |
| `music.ts` | The music player's auto play (see [Music](#music)): each region's songs in the original games' and the remakes' soundtracks (`REGION_MUSIC`, from the decompilations' map data), `runMusic` (the song for where a run stands), `battleMusic`, `victoryMusic`, `practiceMusic`, `healedJingle` and `pickSong`; `music.test.ts` checks every song it names is in the web app's `tracks.json`; `towerMusic`, the Battle Tower's lobby and rooms songs; `idleMusic` and `IDLE_MAP_MUSIC`, the Idle Adventure's (each FireRed map's own song by its `MUS_` constant, Oak's lab's too, Surfing and the bike's, the battle themes, the Hall of Fame; `packages/idle`'s `areas.test.ts` checks every map it walks has its own song in both soundtracks) |
| `berries.ts` | The berries (`BERRIES`): Ruby, Sapphire and Emerald's, Cheri to Starf (the Enigma Berry left out), then Platinum's other 22 (each `gen` 3 or 4; `berryInfo`, `isBerry`) and the 5 `FLAVORS` (see [Extras](#extras)) |
| `berry-data.ts` | The berries' data from pokeemerald's `gBerries` (generated by tools/extras/emerald_extras.py) |
| `berry-farm.ts` | The Berry Farm: `GROW_HOURS` (4, a placeholder), the trees' `STAGES`, `FARM_TREES` (a plot per berry), `farmOf` (an account's farm, new trees planted staggered), `stageAt`, `farmView`, `harvest`, `harvestAll` (every ripe tree at once: Gather all) and `berriesOf` |
| `berry-farm-map.ts` | The Berry Farm's map: its size, walkable tiles, entrance and its two gardens' soil plots, 48 (Emerald's berries') and 24 (Platinum's) (generated by tools/extras/emerald_extras.py) |
| `berry-blender.ts` | The Berry Blender, ported from pokeemerald's `berry_blender.c`: `blenderFrame` (one frame of the game: the arrow, the presses' Best, Good or Miss, the people's presses, speed, progress and top speed), `blenderRanking`, `opponentBerries` and `makePokeblock` (the game's Pokéblock formula); `berries.test.ts` tests it and the farm |
| `poffin-cooking.ts` | Poffin cooking, ported from pokeplatinum's `ov83_0223F7F4.c` and `poffin.c`: `cookFrame` (one frame of the game: the stylus's stroke round the pot, the batter's speed, turn and laps, the 3 phases, spills and burns, the arrow's way), `makePoffin` (the game's Poffin formula from the berries, time, spills and burns), `POFFIN_TYPES`, `poffinName`, `poffinLevel`, `batterScale` and `cookTime`; `poffin-cooking.test.ts` tests it |
| `game-corner.ts` | The Game Corner's rules: the Coin Case (`MAX_COINS`), the counter's offers (`COIN_OFFERS`, `buyCoinsError`), the tables' bets (`ROULETTE_MIN_BETS`), the slot machines' luck each day (`slotMachineId`, field_specials.c's GetSlotMachineId with the day's number for Dewford's trend), and the API's check of a round (`coinRoundError`, `afterRound`); `game-corner.test.ts` tests it |
| `game-corner-map.ts` | The Game Corner's room, Mauville's (`GAME_CORNER_MAP`: its size, walkable tiles, entrance, the 12 machines with their seats, the 2 tables with their sides, its people with their sprites, facing and words, and the counter's texts and menu; generated by tools/extras/game_corner_map.py) |
| `roulette.ts` | The Game Corner's roulette, ported from pokeemerald's `roulette.c` (and `palette_util.c`'s RouletteFlash): `newRouletteGame`, `rouletteFrame` (one frame: its tasks, the ball's and Shroomish's or Taillow's sprites, the flashes), `rouletteMultiplier`, the grid (`ROULETTE_GRID`, `rouletteSelectionAt`), `ROULETTE_SOUNDS`; `roulette.test.ts` tests its payouts by square, column and row, a ball always ending in a slot not yet hit this round, the same play for the same random numbers, and the coins kept between 0 and 9999 |
| `slot-machine.ts` | The slot machines, ported frame by frame from pokeemerald's `slot_machine.c` (its bugs kept): `newSlotMachine`, `slotFrame` (one frame of every task: betting, the bias draws, the reels' spin and steered stops, the lines' matches and payout, Replay, the Pika Power meter, Reel Time's lottery and slowed reels, the digital display's sprites, the info screen, the quit prompt, the fade), `slotMatch`, `SLOT_PAYOUTS`; `slot-machine.test.ts` tests it |
| `voltorb-flip.ts` | Voltorb Flip, ported from pokeheartgold's `voltorb_flip_game.c`: the boards (`BOARD_CONFIGS`, `newBoard`: the level's 10 boards, cards laid out until few enough multipliers are free), the level's rules (`nextLevel`, `levelsGained`), the totals beside the board (`boardTotals`), the coins (`afterFlip`, `maxPayout`), and a round checked against its board (`settleRound`); `voltorb-flip.test.ts` tests it |
| `berry-data-gen4.ts` | Every farm berry's flavours and smoothness as Platinum has them, for Poffins (`POFFIN_BERRY_DATA`), and the 22 berries Platinum has that Emerald's farm didn't (`GEN4_BERRY_DATA`) (generated by tools/extras/platinum_extras.py) |
| `items.ts` | The item catalog (consumables, misc items, key items, equipment) with each item's effects (`ItemEffect`) or use (`ItemUse`), equipment slots, the shop's stock, `itemText` (what an item does, for the screen), `activeEffects` and `battleRules` (battle effects as the player's battle rules) |
| `run-types.ts` | The runs to pick from, as segments of encounters and bosses (one per gym, each region's Gym Challenge and Champion run), each in a generation (`GENERATIONS`: its packs, badges and Gym Challenge); `generationLock`, `runTypeLock`, `nextPack`, `bossTypes` and `routeAt` (the route map a step is on) |
| `routes.ts` | Route maps for runs (`Route`: image, size, a spot per encounter, walkability grid ('.' walkable, '#' not, '~' water on a surfed route, 'v' a ledge, never stood on, jumped over going down (a `jump` step); `sprites: 'hgss'` on the Johto maps, `'rse'` on the Hoenn ones; `landSpots`, a surfed map whose spots on land are ordinary), `links` through gatehouses; for a building its `entrance`, the figures' fixed `stands` and other `npcs`): `ROUTE_2`, `PEWTER_GYM`; `SWIMMERS` (the Swimmers every generation's surfed routes share); `onPath`, `routePath` |
| `johto-routes.ts` | The Johto gym runs' maps (see Johto route maps): Routes 30, 32, 34, 35 and 45 from HeartGold/SoulSilver (hgss_map.py), and the made-up Routes 40 (surfed), 42 (a cave with a waterfall) and 44 (an ice cave in two areas, joined by a ladder) (generate_johto.py); every one drawn with HGSS's sprites (`sprites: 'hgss'`); written by johto_routes.py |
| `johto-gyms.ts` | The Johto gyms' interiors (see Johto gym interiors): each leader's room from HeartGold/SoulSilver, written by tools/routes/hgss/johto_gyms.py |
| `tower.ts` | A Battle Tower challenge (see Battle Tower): `TowerState` and its reducer `applyTowerAction` (as a run's), each floor's generation, opponent and reward, the checkpoints and the opponent's battle setup |
| `battle-tower-trainers.ts` | The Battle Tower's trainers (see Battle Tower): Emerald's Battle Frontier trainers, their lines and their sprite in each set, written by tools/battle-tower/frontier_trainers.py |
| `battle-tower-maps.ts` | The Battle Tower's rooms (see Battle Tower): each generation's lobby, elevator, corridor and battle room, with their places, written by tools/battle-tower/tower_maps.py |
| `hoenn-routes.ts` | The Hoenn gym runs' maps (see Hoenn maps): Petalburg Woods, Routes 106, 110, 102, 119 and 124, Jagged Pass and Sootopolis City from Emerald, drawn with its sprites (`sprites: 'rse'`), and the Cycling Road's `TRIATHLETES`; written by tools/routes/emerald/hoenn_maps.py |
| `hoenn-gyms.ts` | The Hoenn gyms' interiors, the Pokémon League's rooms and Lavaridge's Pokémon Center (see Hoenn maps), with `HOENN_BOSS_ROOMS`; written by tools/routes/emerald/hoenn_maps.py |
| `kanto-routes.ts` | The other Kanto gym runs' maps (generated from the decompilation): routes (Route 4 for Cerulean, Route 5 then Route 6 for Vermilion (3 spots and 7), Route 8 for Celadon, the Pokémon Tower 1F to 5F for Saffron (2 spots a floor, from the stairs on the left, or 1F's door, to the stairs on the right), Route 17 for Fuchsia (on a bike), Route 20 for Cinnabar (surfing), the Power Plant for Viridian; 10 spots each from the end the run starts at), the trainer lists of the routes with their own trainers (`BIKERS`, `ROCKET_GRUNTS`; the surfed routes' `SWIMMERS` are in routes.ts) and gym interiors (the door, the Gym Trainer where FireRed's trainer nearest halfway in stands, the leader in their place, the Gym Guy); for the Kanto Gym Challenge and Champion runs, each boss's map (`KANTO_BOSS_ROOMS`: the leader's part of their gym, `leaderRoom`, the player coming in 5 tiles from the leader; the Elite Four's and the Champion's rooms at the Indigo Plateau) and a Pokémon Center (`POKEMON_CENTER`, Viridian City's, in at the door, up to the counter) |
| `run-types.test.ts` | Tests for run type locks, generation packs and `bossTypes` |
