| `run-maps.test.ts` | The agreed map rules on every run type over 20 seeds (the first encounter a battle, the required battles, Pokémon Centers from step 3 and Ace Trainers from step 5, Gym Trainers before leaders), every surfed route's spots at sea, ashore and its land battle, Repel on bike and surfed routes (and on a required battle: it re-rolls freely, an open question), trades with a Johto deck or an all-Energy deck in a Kanto run, Escape Rope from a Gym Trainer, a skipped or conceded gym leader battle, the Gym Trainers' own lists, and the route of each step on runs with several maps |
| `effects.test.ts` | The engine's effect tables against the snapshot the other way round from `ex.test.ts`: every card id they name is a card, and every attack name one of its attacks (the seed's renames allowed; a Technical Machine's or Stadium's attack is the Trainer card's own) |
| `pool.ts` | Card pool types, rarities and prices, each generation's sets and basic Energy ids |
| `card-fixes.ts` | The catalog's conversion to the engine's card definitions (`catalogCardDef`) and TCGdex's fixes with it (Baby Pokémon listed as Basic, missing "Evolves from", holos listed as normal, owners' and Dark and Light lines, Trainers played as Pokémon, the special Energy cards), `isHolo`, `isGalaxyFoil`, `isBasicEnergyCard`, `isImplemented` and `cardRarity`; shared by the API's `cards.ts` (`toCardDef`) and `test-pool.ts`, so the two can't drift apart |
| `random.ts` | Seeded randomness kept in the state (`Rng`) |
| `test-pool.ts` | Tests only: the pool built from the TCGdex snapshot with `card-fixes.ts`, the rows shaped as the seed writes them (`toCatalog`; `run.test.ts`, `decks.test.ts`, and the API's `cards.test.ts`, which checks its own definitions are the same) |

- **Start**: the first visit sets up the account: a `Starter deck` (`STARTER_DECK`, medium strength, Water/Lightning: Poliwag and Pikachu lines, Lapras, Electabuzz, 17 Trainers, 24 Energy) saved in its deck list with every card in it unlocked, and a first run of **Celadon City** with it. A run starts with 3 HP and ₱500. Older accounts get their starter deck's cards unlocked on their next visit.
- **The map**: the panel beside the map (`RouteChoice`) shows every option of the current step at once, battles together with one deck choice for them, and other encounters together; clicking an option on the map goes there at once, with the current deck. When taking any option on the map, battle or not, you can switch the run's deck for another of your playable decks; it replaces the run's deck (and any trades in it) for the rest of the run (`SET_DECK`, with the deck's name in `RunState.deckName`).
- **Battles**: the trainer plays a random 60-card deck of medium strength (`generateDeck`: 1–2 types, 2–4 middling evolution lines, 14–16 Trainers, all ones a CPU plays (Gym Trainers too: Erika, Misty's Wrath, Master Ball, Brock and Good Manners in any deck; an owner's Trainers and Stadium when the deck has 4 or more of that owner's Pokémon cards, `OWNER_TRAINERS`; Misty's Tears and Fervor in Water and Fire decks), the rest Energy split by the attacks' needs; the same seed gives the same deck) at the CPU's **Trainer** difficulty. Its deck suits the trainer's name: each class (Swimmer, Bug Catcher, Hiker, Bird Keeper…) favours Generation I Pokémon (`trainerFavorites`), whose lines are chosen first and set the deck's types, so a Swimmer plays Tentacruel, Golduck or Starmie rather than any Water Pokémon; other lines join only when the favourites run out. A **Gym Trainer** is the only option on the step before their gym leader: a random deck of the gym's type alone, at Trainer difficulty. **Ace Trainers** (elite battles, from step 6) can be declined; they play at **Gym leader** difficulty and pay more. A win pays ₱ and EXP and offers 1 of 3 cards; a loss costs 1 HP and the run moves on, except against a boss, who is fought again. At 0 HP the run is lost: start over (a new run), or, for testing, **Retry with 3 HP**, which carries on from where it ended (on in production too, for now). Trainer battles on the map lean different ways (`MapEncounter.focus`, rolled with the map: 40% none, 20% each): **Big Payout** (twice the ₱), **Extra EXP** (twice the EXP) or **Rarer Cards** (reward cards one rarity up), tagged on the map and in the encounter panel (`battleReward`; placeholder amounts, to balance later).
- **Other encounters**: a card shop (cards for ₱ by rarity), a free card (1 of 3), a card trade (one of your deck's cards for one at least as rare; it replaces it in the deck), a Pokémon Center (1 HP for ₱100) and Mystery events: a Frenzy (Pokémon of one type do double damage in the next 3 battles, both sides: the engine's `GameState.rules`), a coin-toss bet, a shady dealer (a rare card for 1 HP), a Fan Club donation (₱ for EXP) and a lucky find.
- **Cards**: a Gen 1 run's cards come from every card the engine plays fully (`cardImplemented`) in the Base Set, Jungle, Fossil, Wizards promos, Base Set 2, Team Rocket, Gym Heroes and Gym Challenge, a Gen 2 run's from every card of Gen 2's sets, a Gen 3 run's from the EX sets' (see Generations above; `apps/api/src/pool.ts`), rarer ones less often. A card gained in a run is unlocked at once (`CardUnlock`, `apps/api/src/unlocks.ts`) but doesn't join the run's deck (only trades change it): it can go in your next decks on the Collection tab. Unlocks are per card, not per copy (up to the usual 4); basic Energy is always unlocked. A holo with a non-holo version of the same card in its set (same name, HP, attacks, powers and text: Jungle's, Fossil's and Team Rocket's holos, and promo Mew) is a special addition: it counts every copy gained, from packs and runs (`CardUnlock.quantity`), and gaining it unlocks the non-holo version too (`holoTwins` in `unlocks.ts`; the migration `20261021100000_holo_twins` did this for holos already unlocked). Holo-only cards (Base Set's and Base Set 2's holos, most promo holos) are ordinary unlocks with the usual 4 copies. Holo Energy counts copies too. A deck holds no more copies of a special holo or a holo Energy than were gained (`lockedProblem`; the deck builder's `cantAdd` too, from the catalog's `limitedCopies`), so the rest of a playset is the non-holo version. Special holos unlocked before counting began have 1 copy, or the number gained in runs if more (migration `20261021090000_holo_copies`; packs never recorded duplicates), and saved decks holding more were changed to the non-holo version for the extras (`20261022090000_holo_deck_limit`). The starter deck uses Fossil's non-holo Lapras, so the holo one is only gained by pulling it. Base Set 2 reprints earlier cards with the same art, so a Base Set 2 card and the earlier print it matches unlock together, either way round, whenever neither is holo: its commons, uncommons and non-holo rares (104 pairs; Base Set 2's non-holo Kangaskhan, Mr. Mime, Pinsir, Snorlax, Venomoth and Victreebel pair with Jungle's non-holo prints). Holo rares look different (Base Set 2's have the galaxy foil), so they're unlocked separately. Only Base Set 2 (`BASE2_PAIRS` and `unlockCards`; the migration `20261019090000_base2_reprint_unlocks`, after the commons-only `20261018090000_base2_common_unlocks` and `20261018100000_base2_common_originals`, did this for unlocks made before). The Collection tab shows locked cards darker (and still, without the holo effect, when viewed large). The deck builder adds them with a warning and the API saves them (so an imported deck keeps them, see [Deck sharing](#deck-sharing)), but a deck holding any (or more copies of a special holo or holo Energy than were gained) can't start a run or a single battle: it's greyed out and tagged Locked wherever decks are picked.
- **The run deck**: while a run is on, the deck list has an automatic deck, **Current run** (`Deck.runId`, `syncRunDeck` in `runs.ts`), kept a copy of the run's deck: made when the run starts, and updated by every trade or switch of decks (runs from before it get one on the next `run.overview`). It's read-only on the Collection tab (tagged Run deck: no renaming, editing or deleting; Copy to edit makes an ordinary copy), can't start a run, and can be used for single battles. When the run is over, `SaveRunDeck` offers to save it under a name filled in with the run's number and type: it becomes one of your decks (`run.saveDeck`). If it isn't saved, the next run takes it over: it's renamed Current run again and copies the new run's deck, which is one of your own decks.
- **After a run**: beating it adds its EXP to the account and gives the gym leader's badge (a key item). Then comes the shop, where items cost EXP (a different selection after each run: 3–4 consumables, 1–2 misc items and 1 piece of gear, rolled from the run's seed so it stays put until the next run ends), and then the next run, with a run type and one of your playable decks to pick. When a run ends, beaten or lost, its leftover ₱ go into the account's bank (`User.bank`) at once; the next run starts with ₱500. (Retry with 3 HP takes them back out of the bank, as far as it still has them.)
- **Conceding**: while choosing the next step, Concede the run (`ConcedeRun`, the `CONCEDE` run action) ends it as lost (`RunState.conceded`) after asking. The cards gained stay unlocked (they were unlocked as they were gained). Its ₱ are banked only if at least one battle was completed without conceding it, won or lost (`battlesCompleted`, `bankablePokedollars`: the API checks the game's action rows for the player's `CONCEDE` and passes `conceded` with `BATTLE_OVER`; older runs count every battle finished, from the log if need be), so a fresh run's ₱500 can't be banked by conceding its first battle and then the run. The same rule holds however the run ends: a run lost by conceding every battle banks nothing either. There's no EXP (that's for beating a run) and no retry.
- **Items** (`items.ts`; prices and strengths are first guesses, to balance later). Owned items are `InventoryItem` rows.
  - **Equipment** goes in head, chest, legs, Poké Ball and 2 accessory slots (`Equipment`). It's read when each run action is applied, so a change works from the next encounter. Head: ₱ per win, more EXP, more reward cards. Chest: less damage to your Pokémon (of one type, or all). Legs: cheaper Pokémon Centers or Card Shops, more Free Card choices. Poké Balls: more cards in the opening hand, and more cards to pick from. Accessories: +10 damage for one type's attacks (Charcoal, Mystic Water…), more ₱ or EXP, a lucky first coin flip (Coin Case).
  - **Consumables** are used from the Bag (or by clicking one on the Inventory tab, which asks "Use a …?" first) before a battle (at most 3 per run, and one at a time: another can't be used until the one in effect has run out) and last a number of battles: X Attack and Dire Hit (more damage), X Defend (less damage taken), X Accuracy (first flips heads), Guard Spec., Antidote, Awakening, Paralyze Heal, Burn Heal and Full Heal (no Special Conditions from attacks), Lemonade (bigger opening hand), Lucky Egg (double EXP), Big Pearl (more ₱).
  - **Misc items** do one thing when used, with their own limits: Escape Rope (leave a battle without losing HP; its game is conceded, and a boss or Gym Trainer stays to be fought again), Itemfinder (reveal the step's Mystery encounters), Repel (re-roll the step's encounters, not at a gym), Potion (heal 1 HP), Nugget (₱300).
  - **In battles**, equipment and consumable effects become the player's battle rules (`damageBonus`, `damageGuard`, `conditionGuard`, `openingHand`, `luckyFlips`; see `BattleRule`), each named for its item. Damage bonuses add to the base damage (after a Frenzy's multiplier, before Weakness); guards come off after Weakness and Resistance, like Defender. In the run, they change battle prizes and EXP, prices, and how many cards are offered.
  - **Key items** are records that can't be removed.
- **API** (`routers/run.ts`, `runs.ts`, `games.ts`: what saving games, runs and Battle Tower challenges share (the JSON cast, seeds, action rows, debug's `debugWinGame`), `pool.ts`, `unlocks.ts`, `progress.ts`: pack unlocks and run type locks): `run.overview` (with each deck's problems), `act` (with `expectedVersion`, like games), `choose` (an option on the map; for a battle, with `deckId` it switches decks first: `SET_DECK`), `useItem`, `retry`, `start` (a playable deck of your own, not the run deck), `saveDeck` (the run deck, once the run is over), `shop`, `buyItem`, `inventory`, `equip`; `decks.unlocked`. Starting a battle creates a `Game` linked to the run (`Game.runId`); when the game ends, `game.act` (or the next `run.overview`) records the result in the run.

## Battle Tower

**Playable (asked for 2026-10-24, on the `battle-tower` branch): its assets, rules (`tower.ts`), database, API and screen (`pages/Tower.tsx`, `components/tower/`) are done; the screen is checked only by typecheck and build so far.** A Play mode beside the Badge Challenge (the runs) and Training: the Play button opens a menu of the three (Training moved into it from the site menu), and each has a Return to Home button back to it (the user's: a run's once it's beaten or conceded, beside Start over and Start a new run; the Battle Tower's by the receptionist's Leave Battle Tower (its button removed, the user's); Training's under its opponent settings). While a challenge is under way and not saved, Play stays on it. As many battles as the player can win with one deck, a new opponent as soon as one is beaten, the challenge saved between battles and taken up again later with the same deck (kept, but shown only to take it up). One loss ends it, and so does giving up with the attendant; beating floor 100 wins it. Opponents 1–10 play Gen 1 decks, 11–20 Gen 2, 21+ Gen 3 (staying Gen 3 until there's a Gen 4), all at Gym leader difficulty; every 5th opponent beaten is a checkpoint, and later challenges can start on the floor after any reached. Each win gives Battle Points only (BP, the user's: an account currency, `User.bp`, migration `20261113090000_battle_points`; placeholder amounts), exchanged in the lobby's BP Shop for booster packs (a placeholder stock, `BP_PACK_PRICE` 1 BP a pack: `BpShop`, opening them in the Packs tab's window with `packs.open`'s `currency` bp), the account's BP shown in the top bar beside the other currencies only in the Battle Tower mode (its page and its battles: `useTowerBp`). The player comes into the generation's lobby, walks to the desk and picks the floor and the deck; a clerk takes him to the elevator and up to the battles (in Gen 1, FireRed's Trainer Tower, he climbs the stairs to 1F by the receptionist, as the game has it: its elevator only brings players down from the roof, so it's used only between generations, and the Battle Room's attendant speaks from behind the player). A beaten opponent says their line and leaves, and the attendant asks whether to go on, save and stop, or give up; between generations the clerk leads him back into the elevator, up to the next generation's rooms (all agreed with the user).

The rooms (`battle-tower-maps.ts`, `public/routes/battle-tower/`; FireRed's and Emerald's with an `overhead`, `<room>-overhead.png`: the game's top layer, the second layer of each metatile whose layer type is normal or split, drawn over the people in `TowerScene` so the receptionist stands behind her counter, as in the games; written by `render_route.py --overhead` and `emerald_map.py`'s `<prefix>_overhead.png`): Gen 1 FireRed's Trainer Tower (its lobby and elevator; FireRed has no Battle Tower) and, up its stairs, HGSS's Battle Room with FireRed's sprites (the user's: the player fading in at its top door and walking to the court's left edge, 4,6, the attendant behind him at 2,6, the opponent in by the same door to the right edge, 10,6; the picture moved 8px left so its door, centred between two tiles, is on tile 7: `SHIFT` in tower_maps.py; HGSS's own room the same), Gen 2 HeartGold/SoulSilver's Battle Tower (its lobby and elevator are field maps; its corridor and battle room are 2D frontier scenes, decoded from its NARC a/1/8/3 by `hgss_rooms.py`, their floors read off the images), Gen 3 Emerald's Battle Tower (lobby, elevator, corridor, battle room). Each room's places are the game's where its events or scripts give them (Emerald's battle room: the player up 3 from the door to 4,5 facing right, the opponent down 4 to 5,5 facing left, the attendant up to 4,6); the rest are noted as ours in `tower_maps.py`. The trainers (`battle-tower-trainers.ts`) are Emerald's 300 Battle Frontier trainers with their lines as Emerald writes them, each floor meeting only those with a sprite of their class in its set (the user's): 104 on the Gen 1 floors, 111 on Gen 2's, 295 on Gen 3's (its School Kid boys have no sprite). A challenge (`TowerState`, `tower.ts`) is plain JSON like a run's, changed only by `applyTowerAction`: `CONTINUE` lets the next opponent in, `SAVE` saves and stops (between battles; `saved`, until `RESUME` takes it up again), `QUIT` gives it up (`quit`); then `BATTLE_STARTED` and `BATTLE_OVER` from the server, as a run's battles. A floor's opponent is one of its trainers not yet met this challenge (`floorTrainers`), with a random deck of the floor's generation (`towerBattleSetup`: its deck rules, their class's favourites where it has them, Gym leader difficulty). Beating floor `TOP_FLOOR` (100) wins the challenge (`won`). A win pays `towerReward` (BP: 1 a win on Gen 1's floors, 2 on Gen 2's, 3 from floor 21: placeholders, given to the account at once) and moves up a floor; a loss (or a conceded battle) ends the challenge; a battle skipped by debug pays nothing. `startFloors` gives where a challenge can start from the highest floor beaten (`floorsBeaten`), and `nextIsNewGeneration` when the clerk leads the player back into the elevator. Music (`towerMusic`, campaign's `music.ts`): Gen 2's lobby Gold, Silver & Crystal's Battle Tower or HGSS's Battle Tower Reception Desk, its rooms GSC's Battle Tower or HGSS's Battle Tower (Johto); Gen 3's Emerald's Battle Tower in both soundtracks (ORAS has none); FireRed's Trainer Tower (MUS_TRAINER_TOWER, its rooms' music) isn't in `tracks.json` (KHInsider refused to be fetched for its link), so Gen 1's floors play Gen 2's for now, and autoplay always moves off a battle's music back in the tower (the user's); the battles a trainer's battle theme of the floor's generation. The screen (`pages/Tower.tsx`, `components/tower/`): the staff are FireRed's Union Room receptionist and the 1F man, HGSS's receptionist woman (BFSW1, at the single battles' desk) and its man in uniform (BFSM) as the clerk and attendant (ours: its frontier scenes don't say), and Emerald's Teala everywhere, as its maps have; the attendant's choices are Continue, Save and quit, Concede (asked again before conceding); going on to a new generation's floors, the attendant leads the player out (Gen 1's and Gen 2's, behind the player, send him on alone) and into the next generation's elevator; a challenge started on another generation's floor goes up from the Gen 1 lobby by that generation's elevator. The Play menu (`pages/Play.tsx`) has the Badge Challenge at `/badge` (the run screen, its finished run's screen with Return to Home beside Start new run) and Training at `/battle` (Return to Home under its opponent settings). The database (migrations `20261111090000_battle_tower`, `20261112090000_battle_tower_won`): a challenge is a `TowerChallenge` row (its `TowerState` in `state`, with `version` for optimistic concurrency, as a run's; ACTIVE until it's lost or given up (LOST) or won (WON)), its deck kept only in `state.deck` (never a Deck row, so it's in no deck list) with the account deck it came from (`deckId`, not a relation) and that deck's cosmetics when it began (`cosmetics`, for every battle); its battles are games with `Game.towerId`; the account's highest floor beaten is `User.towerBest`, which `startFloors` turns into the floors a new challenge can start on. The API (`routers/tower.ts`, `towers.ts`): `tower.overview` (the latest challenge, under way, saved or over, its battle settled if it just ended; the highest floor beaten and the start floors), `tower.start` (a playable deck of the account's own, not the run deck, and a start floor; refused while one is under way, and a saved one is given up: the receptionist asks first, saying "Welcome to the Battle Tower!", then "You already have a Battle Tower challenge in progress. Would you like to continue?" (Continue / Start new challenge; the user's), continue taking it up with `RESUME`, its deck shown), `tower.act` (`CONTINUE`, `SAVE`, `RESUME`, `QUIT`, with `expectedVersion`; going on starts the battle, a game with `Game.towerId`, the opponent's name in its state) and the debug-only `tower.debugSkipBattle`. `advanceTower` saves a challenge as `advanceRun` saves a run, giving each win's BP to the account at once (`User.bp`) and raising `User.towerBest`; `game.act` settles a finished tower battle (`settleTowerBattle`, conceded or not, as a run's). A run and a challenge can both be under way (the user's), but while a challenge is under way and not saved no run or Training battle can start (`towerUnderway`, in `run.start` and `game.create`).

## Idle Adventure

**Playable end to end in its rules and API (asked for 2026-10-24, issue #133, on the `worktree-idle-adventure` branch); the screen is checked only by typecheck and build so far; the migration `20261115090000_idle_adventure` is applied to the shared database (2026-10-25). Only in local debug for now (the user's, 2026-10-25): its Play tile is shown, and `/idle` opens, only in a debug build with the debug tools on (`useDebugStatus`, utils/debug.ts); anywhere else `/idle` goes back to the Play tab.** A fourth Play mode (`/idle`): the player picks a starter and the player character walks Kanto's main road by himself, from Route 1 to the Champion's room, fighting every wild Pokémon and trainer he meets in Gen 3 battles that play themselves (a turn every 10 s, the user's, 2026-10-25; 6 s and 3 s before; one pace watched or offline: no speed setting, agreed), buying in each town's Poké Mart, beating the eight Gym Leaders, the Elite Four and the Champion. FireRed only for now (Gen 1); the tables and screens are keyed on the generation so Emerald and HeartGold/SoulSilver can follow. All agreed with the user unless listed as a placeholder or an assumption below.

- **The run** (`packages/idle/src/idle.ts`): `IdleState` is plain JSON in `IdleRun.state`, changed only by `applyIdleAction` (the player's: `GYM`, `GRIND`, `STOP_GRIND`, `CONTINUE`, `VISIT_STORE`, `BUY`, `SET_ORDER`, `SET_SLOTS`, `USE_STONE`, `GIVE_UP`) and `simulateSegment` (the next stretch: steps along the area's track until a trainer sees the player or a wild Pokémon appears, then the whole battle; or to the area's end), with `expectedVersion` (CONFLICT when stale). One idle run per account at a time, beside any Badge Challenge run or Battle Tower challenge; it never holds the Play tab. It starts with ₱3000 (placeholder) and five Poké Balls; its ₱ go to `User.bank` when it ends only if the Champion was beaten (`bankedMoney`, the user's, 2026-10-25: a run given up banks nothing; `banked` marks it settled). The party is fully healed after every battle (no Pokémon Centers). In a town the run waits 5 s for the player's choice (no prompt waits longer: the user's): Gym (or the Elite Four at the Indigo Plateau), Grind to Lv. N (see Auto-grind), Store, Continue (once the gym is beaten); with no choice made in 5 s (the user's, `TOWN_WAIT_MS`) it picks Gym (the Elite Four at the Indigo Plateau), or Continue once the gym is beaten, store open or not, counting down in the scene's corner, started again whenever the run changes (a purchase, the party's order); offline it's the same (`townWait`, a segment of 5 s and a fade), so catch-up plays through towns, and a page opened in a town catches up the time past its wait too. A white-out sends it back to the area's start (a gym's or the League's: its town), the area's trainers to fight again, with no other penalty (the user's, 2026-10-25; it cost ₱1000, or half the money with less, before). Every move starts a battle ready (off cooldown).
- **Starters**: the first run Bulbasaur, Charmander or Squirtle at level 5 (their level-5 moves, random IVs, nature and gender); from the second run, any species the Pokédex has caught too, at level 5 with its record's best IVs and EVs, its nature re-rolled, and shiny if a shiny of it was caught (the player's choice). Wild Pokémon are shiny 1 time in 8192 (`SHINY_ODDS`): drawn in their shiny palette, kept shiny when caught, the Pokédex marking the species (`IdleDex.shiny`).
- **Battles** (`battle.ts`, `moves.ts`, `ai.ts`, `catch.ts`, `stats.ts`): FireRed's rules, from the decompilation: the damage formula in its order (CalculateBaseDamage, the critical hit, STAB, each type, the random 85–100%), FireRed's badge boosts (Boulder Attack, Soul Defense, Volcano Sp. Atk and Sp. Def, the player's only), stats from base stats, IVs, EVs, natures and level, stages and accuracy, the Special Conditions and the volatile ones, priority, two-turn, multi-hit, recoil, draining, OHKO and fixed-damage moves, weather, the abilities Kanto's Pokémon have, the held items trainers carry. Every move effect in the game has one implementation (`EFFECTS`, keyed on `battle_moves.h`'s effect, so Metronome works); a test checks it. Each tick both Pokémon act (items and balls first, then priority, Speed, ties at random), then the end-of-turn effects. No PP: each Pokémon has a **basic move** (no cooldown, always ready) and **3 slots**, picked by the **cooldown rule** (the user's, 2026-10-25, replacing the energy rule): each tick it uses the slot move with the highest power that's off cooldown and would do something (a status move already in place is skipped; `movePower`: a variable-damage move's cooldown × 20, status moves last, the slots' order breaking ties), which then cools down for its `cooldownTicks` (counted down at each tick's end, so a 1-tick move is ready every tick, a 6-tick move one tick in six), and its basic move while every slot cools down (`Battle.chooseMove`, `ready`, `startCooldown`; auto-catch's moves cool down too). The battle screen shows the player's Pokémon's moves under it, each with a bar filling as its cooldown runs down (`MoveBars`). Spite, with no PP to take, adds 2–5 ticks to the cooldown of the target's last move (it fails on a basic move or Struggle). The basic move is the species' earliest damaging high-PP (20+) level-up move it knows; a species that neither evolves nor evolves from anything takes the next stronger one; one with none its pre-evolution's (`basicMove`, `basicFor`; Abra's and Ditto's set by hand). Cooldowns (the energy costs before them, the same numbers) are placeholders: damaging moves `clamp(round(power / 20), 1, 6)` ticks, fixed damage by effect, status moves by effect (sleep 5; paralysis, poison, burn, confusion 4; one stage 2; healing 4; others 3). All level-up moves are kept; the slots default to the 3 strongest (newest first on ties) until the player sets them. Wild Pokémon and trainers' Pokémon follow the same rule with their moves (the game's last 4 level-up moves, or the trainer's). Experience by the Gen 3 formula at a quarter (`EXP_RATE`, the user's, 2026-10-25: slower, more idle; at least 1) (split among the Pokémon that faced the foe, the rest of the party half each as Gen 6's Exp. Share (agreed 2026-10-25), 1.5× from trainers), EV yields, level-ups mid-battle, evolution after the battle (Kanto's 151 only; trade evolutions at levels 36–38, placeholders, `TRADE_LEVELS`), stones from the bag or bought and used at once at a store that sells them (auto-evolve). Trainers send their party in order and use their bag's healing items at a quarter of their HP. Catching is the Gen 3 formula and its four shakes.
- **Settings** (`User.idleSettings`, `idle.settings`): auto-heal (Potions at half HP or less, the one wasting least; a status's own cure before Full Heal; Revives by `revives`, any of: when the last Pokémon is standing (the default), as soon as any faints, in boss battles with the last one standing, on the lead when it faints; none ticked, never), auto-catch (off, new species only, every wild Pokémon; the user's, 2026-10-25: the basic move once, again only while the wild Pokémon is above 60% of its HP, `CATCH_SECOND_HIT`, then the best ball owned every turn, a basic move that could knock it out on the highest roll with a critical hit skipped for the ball; out of balls, the battle goes on as any other), with a full party stop catching or replace the lowest-level Pokémon (otherwise the catch is registered and released), auto-evolve with stones, auto-grind (on by default, the user's, 2026-10-25), auto-buy balls (the user's: `autoBalls`, 0 to 99, 0 off; reaching any town, white-outs too and before auto-grind leaves it, with fewer balls than that in the bag, every kind counted, it buys the cheapest ball the store sells up to it, as many as the money allows: Poké Balls up to Vermilion, Great Balls from Lavender on, FireRed's later marts selling no Poké Balls; a `bought` segment event and a log line).
- **Grinding on a route** (asked for 2026-10-25, the user's): on a route or in a dungeon, out of battle, the page's header has Start grinding (Continue route while grinding; `ROUTE_GRIND`, `CONTINUE_ROUTE`, `IdleState.loop`, `routeGrindPlan`). With trainers beaten on the route, the run goes back (a fade) 4 steps before the first of them (`LOOP_RUN_UP`, where the track first comes into their sight: `meetStep`) and walks the route as usual, wild encounters on the way, fighting each of them again, round again (a fade) once the last is beaten, for as long as it goes. With none beaten, it paces back and forth over 3 (or 2) wild encounter tiles of the track near where it is, behind it first and out of unbeaten trainers' sight (ours: there's no planned loop at every spot); with no wild Pokémon on its stretch (Route 23 past Victory Road), on the most recent stretch that has them, an earlier leg of its area or the nearest earlier route or dungeon, near its end and out of every trainer's sight (the user's: the button names it, `Start grinding in …`). A white-out sends it to the route's start and the loop goes on. Continue route puts it back where it started grinding (or on from where it is, if further), every trainer it had beaten counted beaten again, and walks on as usual.
- **Auto-grind** (asked for 2026-10-25, the user's): before each gym the run can grind, running in a circle near the exit of the area walked just before the gym's town (its last leg with wild Pokémon: Route 2 for Pewter, Route 4 by Mt. Moon for Cerulean, Route 6, Route 7, Route 18, Route 12, Route 20 surfed, Route 21 surfed, and Victory Road's 2F by its exit for the Indigo Plateau, Route 23's stretch after it having no wild Pokémon), wild Pokémon appearing as usual (`wild_encounter.c`'s rules), until the party's highest level is the upcoming gym's highest (`grindTarget`: its Gym Leader's highest-level Pokémon, Brock's Onix at 14 (FireRed's), Misty's Starmie at 21…; at the Indigo Plateau the Elite Four's and the Champion's highest), then into the gym. The loop (`TownArea.grind`, a ring of 4–12 tiles of one kind of wild encounter, the roundest nearest the exit, walked clockwise) is planned by tools/idle/kanto_areas.py (`grind_loop`); the run's phase is `grinding` meanwhile (`IdleState.grind`: its step round the loop; `area` stays the town), the segments `grind` (onto the loop, a fade) and `circle` (steps round it). The town's Grind to Lv. N starts it by hand (greyed out once the party is there); the Auto-grind setting (`autoGrind`) starts it on reaching a gym's town, or with the party already there goes straight into the gym; back in the town after a white-out (in the gym, the League or grinding) it trains to 2 levels above the party's highest then (`REGRIND_LEVELS`, the user's; the gym's highest at least) and goes in again, so every lost try gains 2 levels (past level 100 it waits for the player); Stop grinding (`STOP_GRIND`, beside Give up) goes back to the town's choice. A white-out while grinding goes back to the town as any other.
- **Walking** (`areas.ts`, generated `data/gen1/areas.ts` and `maps.ts` by tools/idle/kanto_areas.py): each area is legs over FireRed's maps, each with a track planned once from the decompilation (a run starts coming out of Professor Oak's lab in Pallet Town, on its door, 16,13, facing down, then walks Route 1: the user's; the player's house's door before): from its start past every trainer's sight line (nearest first; `trainer_sight_or_berry_tree_id` tiles ahead, stopping at anything in the way; looking around every way; one with no sight, or facing a wall, met from beside them) to its end; each trainer faces the tile where the track first comes into their sight, where the run meets them (the user's: those who look around in the game turned that way, a test checks); maps are joined by their warps (stairs, ladders, Saffron Gym's pads), gatehouses and connections, ledges are jumped one way, elevation is respected, water is surfed where the area surfs, and HM obstacles (Cut trees, boulders, Snorlax) and gym puzzles (the gates its scripts open with `setmetatile`, Vermilion Gym's barrier as the run maps have it) are walked through. Each step carries its tile's wild encounters and battle terrain. Wild encounters follow `wild_encounter.c` step by step: the cooldown after an encounter, the 60% roll when the tile changes, the map's rate × 16 out of 1600 plus the build-up, Stench and Illuminate, the slots' odds (FireRed's tables). The areas, in the game's order: Route 1, Viridian City, Route 2 and Viridian Forest, Pewter City and its gym, Route 3, Mt. Moon, Route 4, Cerulean City and its gym, Routes 24 and 25, Routes 5 and 6 (by the Underground Path), Vermilion City and its gym, Routes 9 and 10 (Route 11 left out, the user's: a dead end until Snorlax), Rock Tunnel, Lavender Town, Routes 8 and 7, Celadon City and its gym, Routes 16–18, Fuchsia City and its gym, Routes 15–12, Saffron City and its gym, Routes 19 and 20, Seafoam Islands, Cinnabar Island and its gym, Route 21, Viridian City and its gym, Routes 22 and 23, Victory Road, the Indigo Plateau and the five League rooms. No S.S. Anne, Pokémon Tower, Silph Co., rival battles or Team Rocket. Trainers out of reach on the way the run walks (Route 4's Lass Crissy and Route 10's PokéManiac Mark, on strips reached only from the other way) are left out; `areas.test.ts` checks every other trainer's sight line is crossed and every step is walkable.
- **Segments and catch-up**: the server resolves everything and the browser plays it back. `idle.next` returns the next segment as an event log (`SegmentEvent`: the area, the walk, a trainer spotting the player, the battle with its own `BattleEvent`s and lines, money, evolutions, catches, badges, white-outs, the town, the title) and moves `IdleRun.simulatedAt` on by its length (refused while it's more than 15 s ahead of now; the browser waits and asks again). Each segment has a length (steps × 267 ms, ticks × 10 s, 4 s a battle's start and end, fades); on opening the mode `idle.catchUp` plays the time since `simulatedAt` (at most the last 12 hours away, agreed: `CATCH_UP_CAP_MS`) in whole segments that fit in it (a run just started, or a moment away, catches up nothing: the user's), through towns (each waiting 5 s) up to the title, 40 segments a call (the browser calls again), returning a summary (battles, Pokémon caught, levels, evolutions, white-outs, money, badges) shown, once a minute or more was caught up, page by page in the scene's text box (`DialogueBox`).
- **Music** (auto play, `idleMusic` and `IDLE_MAP_MUSIC` in campaign's `music.ts`; the user's: every song matching where the player is, as FireRed plays it, from the pokefirered decompilation): each map's own song, its `map.json`'s `MUS_` constant, in the Original (Red's album) or Remastered (FireRed's) soundtrack, the game's sharing kept (Seafoam Islands Viridian Forest's theme, Rock Tunnel and Victory Road Mt. Moon's, Lorelei's room the Gym's, Bruno's Rocket Hideout's, Agatha's Pokémon Tower's, Lance's and the Champion's Victory Road's); Routes 24 and 25 Welcome to the World of Pokémon!, as both games play there; Oak's lab's song with no run under way (the starters); Surfing on any water the player surfs, cave water too, but on Route 23, Victory Road and the Indigo Plateau, where the game keeps their song (`Overworld_MusicCanOverrideMapMusic`); the bike's on the Cycling Road (Route 17), where the game puts the player on the bike (the player is still drawn walking); in a battle the wild, trainer (rivals' and Giovanni's too), Gym Leader (the Elite Four's too) or Champion theme, from its start, starting as the trainer's "!" shows (a wild battle's with its flash; the user's: `cue` in the playback) and carrying on into the battle; the Hall of Fame once the Champion is beaten. Songs under a minute it needs are in `tracks.json`, `hidden`.
- **The Pokédex** (`IdleDex`): per account and species, seen, caught, and each stat's best IV and EV ever had by any of the account's Pokémon of the species (each stat on its own), updated after every battle from the party, on catching and releasing, and on the town's choices.
- **Database and API**: `IdleRun` (state, version, seed, `banked`, `simulatedAt`), `IdleDex`, `User.idleSettings` (migration `20261115090000_idle_adventure`); `routers/idle.ts`: `overview` (the latest run, settings, the starters it can pick, the Pokédex), `start`, `next`, `catchUp`, `act`, `settings`, and the debug-only `debugNext` and `debugWarp`; `apps/api/src/idle.ts` loads and saves runs (CONFLICT when stale), banks a run's money once and merges the Pokédex. Shared inputs in `packages/shared/src/idle.ts`.
- **The issue's open questions, answered by the user (2026-10-25)**: Blue's team is the one strong against the starter (by its type for a Pokédex starter, at random for a type without one); Revives by a setting (above); shinies shown, recorded and startable; auto-catch always throws the best ball; a full party never offers a manual switch (the settings decide); the mode is called Idle Adventure; catch-up plays at most the last 12 hours and its summary lists the totals and each catch; Routes 24/25 and 12–15 kept, Route 11 left out; no speed setting.
- **Balance** (2026-10-25, measured over 6 whole runs each, every starter): the energy rules tried were spending on the highest slot it could pay (slot 1 fired 1–3% of turns), waiting on slot 1 only (slots 2 and 3 never fired), a meter per slot, and slots in turn (chosen by the user); experience to those that faced the foe only (FireRed's: hundreds of white-outs at the Elite Four, the lead at 100 and the rest near 30), half to the rest (chosen) or full (almost no white-outs). With slots in turn and half shares a run takes about 4.5 hours of play with about 30 white-outs, most at Lorelei, and 8–9 of 12 bosses beaten first time. SonicBoom and Dragon Rage cost 2 and 3 energy (a fixed 20 damage every turn was too strong early). With the cooldown rule, 10 s ticks and a quarter of the experience (measured 2026-10-25 over 3 whole runs each, always going on from towns): about 32–42 hours of play and 13–20 white-outs with auto-grind on; 64–99 hours and 340–735 white-outs without it (most retrying gyms under-levelled).

| File (`packages/idle/src/`) | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `types.ts` | The generated tables' shapes (species, moves, items, encounters, trainers) and a run's Pokémon (`Mon`) |
| `data.ts` | Lookups over the tables (`species`, `moveData`, `itemData`), physical types, the type chart (`matchup`, `effectiveness`) |
| `stats.ts` | Natures, `calcStats`, the growth rates' `expForLevel` and `levelForExp`, EV caps (`addEvs`) |
| `mon.ts` | Making Pokémon (`createMon`), the moves known by a level, the last 4 (wild), `basicMove`/`basicFor`, `cooldownTicks`, `movePower`, `autoSlots` |
| `battle.ts` | The battle (`Battle`, `simulateBattle`): battlers, turn order, the damage formula, accuracy, statuses, contact abilities, items and balls, faints, experience, the end of turn; `statusUseless`, `MEDICINE` |
| `moves.ts` | Every move effect (`EFFECTS`) |
| `ai.ts` | The player's action each tick (auto-heal, auto-catch's basic hits and balls, then the cooldown rule) and a trainer's items |
| `catch.ts` | The catch formula and shakes, the balls |
| `evolution.ts` | Level, trade-level and stone evolutions |
| `areas.ts` | The areas (`AREAS`, `areaById`, `mapView`, `BADGES`) and their types |
| `idle.ts` | The run: `IdleState`, `createIdleRun`, `applyIdleAction`, `visitStore`, `simulateSegment`, `catchUp`, `storeStock`, `idleView` |
| `data/gen1/` | Generated (don't edit): species.ts, moves.ts, abilities.ts, items.ts, types.ts, encounters.ts, trainers.ts, marts.ts (tools/idle/firered_data.py); areas.ts, maps.ts (tools/idle/kanto_areas.py) |
| `battle.test.ts`, `areas.test.ts`, `idle.test.ts` | Known values (Charmander's stats, Scratch, Route 1's table, Brock's party, Viridian's mart), a hand-worked damage calculation, catch odds, experience, every move effect implemented, battles over many seeds; the areas' rules; whole runs to the Champion and catch-up |

## Booster packs

The ₱ in the bank (each run's leftover ₱, moved there when the run ends) buys booster packs on the **Packs** tab, ₱300 each (`PACK_PRICE`). There's one per set of a generation (`PACK_SETS`: Base Set, Jungle, Fossil, Wizards Black Star Promos, Base Set 2, Team Rocket, Gym Heroes, Gym Challenge, and Gen 2's sets, whose cards' effects are still to come), all shown at first, with a generation filter (`GenerationFilter`) to show only some generations' sets; each bought only once unlocked by runs (see [Runs](#runs): `packs.list` says which are `locked`, and which is `next`; `open` refuses a locked one, the debug `debugOpen` doesn't). Locked packs are dimmed on the page and can't be picked, and their cards can't be crafted with Stardust (`packs.craft` refuses them; the deck builder's Craft button says to unlock the set's packs, from `packs.unlockedSets`). A pack is 11 cards with the usual WOTC-era make-up, averaged rather than each set's own odds: 1 rare, a holo about 1 pack in 3 (`HOLO_CHANCE`), 3 uncommons and 6 commons, all different, and each of those 9 has a chance of being a rare (or holo) instead: 4% while the pack has no extra rare, then 2%, 0.5% and 0.1% (`EXTRA_RARE_CHANCES`; about 31% of packs get an extra rare, 2.5% two or more; a promo pack's extras are holo promos; placeholder odds). Southern Islands, whose 18 cards are all galaxy foil (counted as holos), gives 6 different ones a pack (`HOLO_SET_PACK`, a placeholder). And 0.6% of packs are all rares and holos but their Energy (`ALL_RARES_CHANCE`): the pack window sets off fireworks over the screen for a few seconds (`Fireworks.tsx`, with `canvas-confetti`) and says "All rares!". Each pack has 1 basic Energy of any type: the set's own, or the closest earlier set's by release date (Jungle, Fossil and the promos get Base Set's; Team Rocket gets Base Set 2's; never a reverse-holo print of it, `packBasicEnergy`), and 4% of the time a holo Energy instead (see [Holo Energy](#holo-energy)). TCGdex lists every promo as Common (10 of them are holo: the 6 TCGdex marks, Venusaur and Cool Porygon, and the galaxy-foil Entei and Pichu, which `cardRarity` counts as holo), so a promo pack's uncommon and rare slots hold more non-holo promos, and its last card is a holo promo about 1 pack in 3; the pack window labels them "Promo" and "Holo Promo". `openPack` in `packages/campaign/src/packs.ts` rolls it; the API's `packs` router (`apps/api/src/routers/packs.ts`: `list`, `open`) takes the ₱ from the bank, rolls the packs on the server and unlocks every card in them, in one transaction, and returns the cards in reveal order (each pack's Energy first and rare last, pack after pack, each with its `pack` number) with which are new unlocks (only the first time a card comes up). Up to `MAX_PACKS_AT_ONCE` (50) packs can be bought at once (`count`). The page shows the sets as packs, each with how many of the cards its packs can hold you've unlocked (`unlockedCount` of `packCards` from `packs.list`, basic Energy aside) and a progress bar; clicking one opens a window (closed with ×, or before opening by a click anywhere but the pack and its controls) with that pack and a quantity picker (×1, ×2, ×5, ×10, or Custom with a Max button for what the bank pays for), and two options remembered in the browser: separate commons and rares (the commons, uncommons and Energy of all the packs are laid out face up together, and the rares stay face down to be opened one by one) and auto open (each card moves on by itself, pausing longer on rares and holos; while it runs, ×2 speed doubles it, and Stop auto goes back to clicking for the rest of the packs, with Resume auto to start it again). Clicking the pack opens them with an animation, then the cards one at a time or all at once (Skip), and finally the list of cards opened. With the commons separated, or several packs on auto open, only the rares are revealed one by one, on the same screen as every card would be, then the list shows every card with the rares (and holos) framed and tagged (a holo Energy too). Any holo or galaxy foil counts as a rare there, whatever slot it came from.

## Favorites

Any card can be marked as a favorite: open it large (right-click it, or on a phone hold it and let go) and press its ★ (`CardInspector`'s `favorite`; `CardInspectProvider` everywhere, the deck builder's own inspector on the Collection tab). Favorites belong to the account (`FavoriteCard` rows; `decks.favorites` lists them, the latest first, and `decks.setFavorite` adds or removes one). On the Collection tab, the search's Favorites toggle (before Pokémon) shows only favorites (`DeckFilter.favorites`). Migration `20261026090000_favorite_cards` adds the table.

## Stardust

Stardust is the account's crafting currency (`User.stardust`). Every card pulled from a pack that you already had gives some (a new unlock gives none), by rarity (`STARDUST_PER_CARD` in `packages/campaign/src/packs.ts`): 50 a common, 100 an uncommon, 300 a rare or holo (a holo Energy too), none for basic Energy. The Packs tab shows how much you have, and the list of cards opened how much the packs gave. It's spent crafting cards on the Collection tab: right-click a card and Craft it (`CardInspector`'s `craft`; `packs.craft`), for 100 Stardust a common or uncommon and 500 a rare or holo (`CRAFT_COST`). Crafting unlocks the card the same way pulling it would (`unlockCards`): once, or for a special holo another copy and its non-holo version. Only cards packs hold can be crafted (the catalog's `craftCost`; not basic or holo Energy), and not one already unlocked unless it counts copies.

## Holo Energy

Holo versions of basic Energy, found only in booster packs: a pack's Energy card is a holo Energy 4% of the time (`HOLO_ENERGY_CHANCE` in `packages/campaign/src/packs.ts`), of the pack's Energy (its set's own, or the closest earlier set's). Each comes in one of every style of the holo card library (`apps/web/src/styles/holo/cards`, all but the single-card `swsh-pikachu.css`: regular and reverse holo, galaxy, amazing and radiant rares, V, VMAX, VSTAR, full arts, rainbow, secret, shiny and the Trainer Gallery styles), picked at random, and each type and style is a card of its own in the catalog: `<energy id>-holo-<style>`, e.g. `base1-98-holo-rainbow`. The seed makes them (`packages/db/prisma/holo-energy.ts`: `HOLO_ENERGY_STYLES` and `HOLO_ENERGY_SETS`, the sets packs take their Energy from), with the style in the card's data (`holoEnergy`), shown by `holoAttributes` and `HoloCard` (its `data-rarity`, `data-trainer-gallery` and any `supertype`/`subtypes` the style selects on).

They're never counted in the number of cards collected: the Packs tab's unlocked count for each set (`packs.list`'s `unlockedCount` of `packCards`, which leave out Energy, basic too) and the Collection tab's count of cards shown leave them out. A deck's card count includes them, as they're cards in it.

They play as basic Energy of their type (no limit by name), but unlike basic Energy they're locked until pulled, and every pull adds one copy (`CardUnlock.quantity`, counted by `unlockCards` like every holo card): a deck holds no more of each than were pulled (`lockedProblem`; the deck builder's `cantAdd` and `decks.save` check it too). The deck builder only lists the ones you've pulled, each as its own card tagged with its style and how many you have ("Holo · Rainbow ×2"). In the pack opener a holo Energy is treated like a rare: revealed in the rares-only stack and glowing like a holo.

## Inventory

The **Inventory** tab (`pages/Inventory.tsx`) uses the page width of the other tabs. Its Equipped section (`EquipmentPanel.tsx`) has two columns (stacked on a narrow screen), their tops level. On the left, one box: your trainer sprite (Hilbert, the Black/White hero, until you pick another) on its left side, and on its right side the slots in a 2×3 grid, close together (agreed): head, chest and legs down the left, the two accessories and the Poké Ball down the right. They never overlap: on a narrow screen the trainer's side narrows, cropping the sprite's empty sides. An empty slot shows its icon; a filled one shows its piece, with an × on hover to take it off and the piece's description below it, as in the grid. In the right column, under its hint ("Drag a piece onto its slot, or click it and then the slot.", in small text, on the Equipped heading's baseline: both in the panel grid's first row, `self-baseline`; no heading), in a grid of their own (its first row level with the first row of slots) sorted by slot (head, chest, legs, accessories, Poké Balls; the only order, agreed), every piece of equipment you have that isn't equipped is a box the size of a slot (a piece in a slot leaves the grid; a spare copy stays, with how many are left), describing itself on hover. On a screen without a hover (a phone), tapping a piece describes it at the bottom of the screen instead (`ItemDetails`), and tapping a filled slot does the same with Take off. Drag a piece onto a slot to equip it (dnd-kit; a finger holds it a moment first, so a swipe over the grid scrolls the page), or click it and then the slot; while a piece is held (clicked or being dragged), the slots it fits glow green and the others red, and Escape or a click elsewhere puts it down. A piece already in another slot moves there when you have no spare (`run.equip` refuses a second accessory of a kind without two). Under the trainer, **Change trainer** opens a window (`TrainerSpritePicker.tsx`) with every trainer sprite Pokémon Showdown has (about 1,500, `TRAINER_SPRITES` in `packages/shared/src/trainer-sprites.ts`, listed from its sprite folder and shown straight from play.pokemonshowdown.com, not copied), with a search box; clicking one makes it your trainer (`User.trainerSprite`, saved with `run.setTrainerSprite`; `run.inventory` returns it). Below, the Consumables, Miscellaneous items and Key items are listed with their pictures, as in the post-run shop. Badges aren't listed one by one: the key items start with a badge case for each generation of runs (`BadgeCases.tsx`, from `GENERATIONS`, so a new generation gets its own), saying how many of its badges you have; clicking one opens it, with every badge of the generation in gym order and its gym leader, the ones you have in colour and marked Obtained, the rest greyed out. Beside Change trainer, a drop-down picks your sprite on the run maps (agreed): the boy, Red, Ethan and Brendan, or the girl, Leaf, Lyra and May, each game's player (`User.playerGender`, null the boy; migration `20261104090000_player_gender`; `run.setPlayerGender`; `run.overview`'s `playerGender` for the map, `playerSprite`'s `gender`). The girls' sprites are made as the boys' are: Leaf's by `tools/routes/frlg_player_sprites.py` from pokefirered (`leaf`, `leaf_bike`, `leaf_surf`), Lyra's by `hgss_sprites.py` (`hgss/heroine`, `hgss/cycleheroine`, `hgss/swimheroine`: models 70, 72 and 74), May's by `emerald_sprites.py` (`rse/may`, `rse/may_bike`, `rse/may_surf`).

Clicking a consumable's tile asks "Use a …?" (`UseConsumableDialog`) and then uses it (`run.useConsumable`): in the run under way, as the Bag's Use does, or, with no run under way, it's taken from the inventory and waits for the next run (`User.pendingConsumables`, migrations `20261101090000_pending_consumable` and `20261102090000_pending_consumables`; a run lost (out of HP or conceded) with consumables still in effect puts them there too, with the battles they had left (taken back if the Retry testing aid carries the run on), `<id>:<battles>`: `carriedConsumables`, `pendingConsumable`, the user's; a conceded battle uses up one of its consumables' battles as any other, a debug-skipped one doesn't, and Battle Tower battles use none: they have no items; listed under the Consumables heading). Up to a run's 3 can wait; they all take effect at once when that run starts, counted among its 3 (`withConsumablesBefore`, in `startRun`), and one more can be used in the run (one at a time) once they've run out. When one can't be used now (`consumableProblem` in `BagPanel.tsx`: one already in effect, 3 used this run, or a battle under way; between runs, 3 already waiting), the dialog says why instead.

Item pictures (`public/items/<item id>.png`, `ItemIcon`) are the game items' icons from [PokeAPI's sprites](https://github.com/PokeAPI/sprites) (`sprites/items/`), and the Kanto badges its badge artwork (`sprites/badges/1–8`). Items with no game counterpart use the closest one: X Defend is X Defense, the Itemfinder the Dowsing Machine, the Champion Ribbon a Ribbon Sweet, the Badge Case (a Trainer Card screen in the games, with no bag icon) the Medal Box; Trainer's Cap a Rocky Helmet, Ace Visor Wise Glasses, Safari Hat Safety Goggles; Leaf Jacket, Wetsuit, Flame Vest and Rubber Coat the Meadow, Splash, Flame and Zap Plates, Padded Vest an Assault Vest; Hiking Pants Protective Pads, Running Shoes Heavy-Duty Boots, Cycling Shorts the Bicycle.

## Site menu

Every page has a top bar across the window (`main.tsx`): the ☰ button and the Poké TCG logo on the left, Sign out on the right (on a small screen just its icon), on a phone with the run's HP and ₱ (during a run), the bank and Stardust before it (`TopBarStats`). There's no account menu (agreed 2026-10-23): the account name is changed on the Settings page. The bar stays at the top as the page scrolls, on a phone too, but for a battle there (its screen covers the bar).

Below it on a big screen, down the left edge, is the menu (`NavRail`), dashboard style: minimised, a narrow column of the pages' icons (Play, Inventory, Cosmetics, Packs, Collection, Community Decks, Extras; each page's name on hover; Training is one of Play's modes, in its menu, the user's), the current page lit in yellow (Play on its menu, its modes and their battles: `usePlayLit`), and Settings under Extras. The ☰ opens it: wider, each icon with its page's name beside it, taking its room on the left of the window, the page beside it narrower but still in full view and usable; the ☰ again minimises it. It's remembered in the browser (`poke-tcg.nav-open`). The account's bank, EXP and Stardust are in the top bar on a big screen, at its right end before the music player's bar, run or not (`TopBarAccount`, the user's; each with its pokesprite item from `public/nav/`: the Big Nugget, the Exp. Share, Stardust). Under the pages, set apart by a line, the open menu has two sections: during a run, the run's numbers (`RunStats` with `nav`: only HP and the run's EXP, and ₱; not the account's figures, the items in effect, the badges won, the equipment or the Bag, the user's: the Bag is on the run screen); then the logs, with a tab each at the top for the Run log (during a run) and the Battle log (while a game is on screen, passed up by `useGameLogInNav`). The run's section is as tall as its numbers, so the logs start right under the run's ₱ (the user's; it was dragged to a height before) and fill the rest of the menu, down to its bottom (the run's section never scrolls: on a short window the log gives way). The log tab is remembered (`poke-tcg.nav-sections`). Minimised, the menu shows just the run's HP and ₱. On a big screen the run screen and the battle's ☰ menu no longer have Run or log tabs (the battle has no ☰ menu on a big screen, Debug being a tab beside the logs and the options on the Settings page; the run screen's tabs panel is gone, the step's options laid out as on a run without it); on a phone they're as before. On a phone (`useIsPhone`) there's no column: the ☰ opens the same menu as a panel from the left (`SidePanel`), with the music player's bar under it. Settings (`/settings`) holds the account name and the battle options.

The pages' icons (agreed 2026-10-23) are 32×32 pixel-art sprites in `public/nav/` (`navIcon` in `PageNav.tsx`, drawn at their own size so they stay sharp): from [pokesprite](https://github.com/msikma/pokesprite)'s items without the white outline, Play the Poké Ball, Cosmetics the Prop Case, Collection the Photo Album, Community Decks the Large Leek, Training the Focus Band (its tile on the Play menu), Extras the Xtransceiver (and the top bar's figures, on every screen: `bank.png`, `exp.png`, `stardust.png`, and `bp.png` for Battle Points, pokesprite's Honor of Kalos (the user's; its newer sprites have no white outline), shrunk from 24px tall to 20 (nearest neighbour) to match the others' size; the run money's `pokedollar.png`, the Pokédollar sign, is our own, by `tools/icons/pokedollar.py`: `PokedollarIcon` in the run's numbers, the minimised menu and the phone's top bar); Packs the English Laboratory booster pack of the Game Boy Color game (rebuilt from the [pret/poketcg](https://github.com/pret/poketcg) decompilation's tiles, tile map, palettes and logo sprite, shrunk to 16×24 with a black outline); Inventory a bag (the user's sprite, its shadow cleared, shrunk to 19×22 in its own colours with a black outline); Settings a PC.

## Cosmetics

Card backs, playmats and coins, picked on the **Cosmetics** tab (`pages/Cosmetics.tsx`, after Inventory in the site menu; `CosmeticsPanel`; agreed 2026-10-22). Each is the default (Classic, Plain), sold for banked ₱ (placeholder prices), or unlocked by beating a Gym Challenge or Champion run (`COSMETICS` and `hasCosmetic` in `packages/campaign/src/cosmetics.ts`); every one is unlocked while debugging. The API (`routers/cosmetics.ts`: `list`, `buy`, `assign`) keeps the ones bought (`CosmeticUnlock`, migration `20261103090000_cosmetics`) the decks each is assigned to and the default of each kind (below); the run-type ones are worked out from the runs beaten, so they're had however long ago the run was beaten.

- **Card backs** show on the player's own face-down cards: their deck and Prize cards (both layouts; the deck's, see below) and the cards of the packs they open (the default) (`CardBack`'s `mine`, `useCosmetics`); the CPU's stay Classic. Classic is the English back (tcg.pokemon.com) and Japanese the Japanese one (pokemon-card.com's `poke_ura.jpg`), both kept in `public/cardbacks/`; Shadow, Cosmos, Ancient, Glitter and Illusion are them recoloured or with a holo texture from `public/img` laid over, made beforehand as images (Kanto Gym Challenge: Cosmos; Kanto Champion: Ancient; Johto Gym Challenge: Glitter; Johto Champion: Illusion; Hoenn Gym Challenge: Maze, the classic back with `geometric.png`'s labyrinth lines; Hoenn Champion: Tidal, with `trainerbg.png`'s waves, both by `tools/cardbacks/hoenn_backs.py`; for ₱: Japanese, Shadow). Then the 68 Pokémon TCG Pocket card sleeves (`SLEEVES`, ids `sleeve-<slug>`; the full images from [Bulbagarden Archives](https://archives.bulbagarden.net/wiki/Category:Pok%C3%A9mon_TCG_Pocket_card_sleeves), resized to 660×921 WebP in `public/cardbacks/sleeves/`): two behind each run (agreed: Kanto Gym Challenge Erika and Red Pikachu, Kanto Champion Blastoise and Shiny Mewtwo, Johto Gym Challenge Espeon Umbreon and Suicune, Johto Champion Ho-Oh Lugia and Tyranitar, Hoenn Gym Challenge May and Mega Altaria, Hoenn Champion Mega Blaziken and Mega Sceptile), the rest for ₱1500, the shiny and special ones ₱2500 (placeholders). A test checks every card back's image is there.
- **Playmats** fill both sides of the table on the big screen, the CPU's in the player's playmat too (`Playmat`, behind the cards, clipped to its own box so the sides don't clip their content; not yet on the phone layout) with a faint geometric pattern that answers a click on its empty space subtly: things shift a few pixels or light up, but nothing turns or flips (agreed). **Dot matrix** (₱) sends a wave out that pushes the dots apart; **Grid** (₱) and **Triangles** (Johto Champion) light their lines and corners as the wave passes, nudging the corners aside; **Crosses** (₱) light up and open out; **Pinstripes** (₱), fine diagonal lines, and **Contours** (Kanto Gym Challenge), wavy contour lines, take a straight wave front instead of a circle (`bandsAt`): it travels across the lines away from the click, square to them, shifting each line aside a few pixels and lighting it where it crosses, fading along the lines far from the click; **Hexes** (Kanto Champion) lights the hex clicked and runs the light out along the hexes' edges, corner to corner; **Constellation** (Johto Gym Challenge), scattered stars linked to their neighbours (always the same layout, seeded), lights the star nearest the click and runs the light from star to star. A click on either side of the table carries on into the other side, as if the wave crossed the gap (Hexes and Constellation carry on from the hex or star nearest it, as late and as dim as the light would be by the time it got there, never starting over at full brightness, and without the clicked hex's fill). Nothing moves with reduced motion.
- **Coins** are tossed for every coin flip, the player's and the CPU's, and the turn-order flip (`SpinningCoin`'s `design`, `COIN_DESIGNS` in `coinDesigns.tsx`): Classic (gold H, silver T; the default); Poké Ball, Great Ball and Ultra Ball (heads the ball, tails its colour with the button embossed) and the Fire, Water, Grass, Lightning and Psychic Energy coins (the board's Energy symbols) for ₱; the Master Ball (Kanto Champion), GS Ball (Johto Champion) and Luxury Ball (Hoenn Champion) unlocked by beating the run. A coin's tile flips it when clicked.
- **Assigned to decks** (agreed 2026-10-23): a cosmetic the account has can be used by any number of its decks. Its tile's Assign (in place of Buy) opens `AssignDialog`: a search by name over a scrolling list of the account's decks (not the run deck) as compact tiles (`CompactDeckTile`), any number ticked (Tick all / Untick all, of those shown while searching; a deck ticked loses its other cosmetic of that kind, named beside it), and "Default for decks without a …", the account's default for that kind (`User.cardBack`, `playmat`, `coin`; the built-in one, Classic, Plain or the classic coin, can't be unticked, only replaced by ticking another). Save applies it (`cosmetics.assign`); the tile keeps its Assign button, which opens the dialog again to change them (agreed: no deck tiles or Default tag on the tile; `DeckAssign` in `CosmeticsPanel`; `cosmetics.list` gives each one's `deckIds` and `isDefault`, and the `defaults`). Assigning happens only on the Cosmetics tab. A deck with none of a kind, and everything outside a battle (the packs opened), uses the default. The decks keep them in `Deck.cardBack`, `playmat`, `coin` (migration `20261106090000_deck_cosmetics`). A battle keeps its deck's when it begins (`Game.cosmetics`: a Training battle's chosen deck's; a run's battle the run deck's, which takes the cosmetics of the deck the run starts with or switches to and keeps them through the run), and the game page passes them to `useCosmetics` (`DeckCosmeticsContext`). A copied deck has none.

## Extras

The **Extras** tab (`/extras`, `pages/Extras.tsx`, after Community Decks in the site menu, its icon the Xtransceiver) lists minigames and other modes, each a tile opening its page. Their pictures are Pokémon Emerald's, made by `tools/extras/emerald_extras.py` from the [pret/pokeemerald](https://github.com/pret/pokeemerald) decompilation. The API is `routers/extras.ts` (`extras.farm`, `harvest`, `blender`, `blend`, `poffins`, `cook`, `gameCorner`, `buyCoins`, `coinRound`, `voltorbRound`, `voltorbSettle`, and the debug-only `ripenAll` and `fillCoins`); the account keeps its farm (`User.berryFarm`), its berries (`User.berries`), its blender record (`User.blenderRecord`), its coins (`User.coins`), its Voltorb Flip session (`User.voltorbFlip`), its Pokéblocks (`Pokeblock`) and its Poffins (`Poffin`), migrations `20261108090000_berries`, `20261109090000_poffins`, `20261110090000_coins` and `20261114090000_voltorb_flip`. The minigames' sounds share one volume, set by the slider under each screen (`MinigameVolume`, remembered in the browser).

**Berry Farm** (`/extras/berry-farm`): Route 123's Berry Master's garden made bigger from its own metatiles (its soil-and-grass columns repeated to 8 pairs of plots a row, its 2 rows of soil made 3, the house and its path as they are: 48 plots), with a tree of each of Emerald's 42 berries (all of Gen 3's but the Enigma Berry; the last 6 plots bare soil); and below it, a second fenced garden of the same pieces (4 pairs of plots a row, 3 rows: 24 plots), the path running on down beside it, with a tree of each of the 22 berries Platinum has that Emerald's farm didn't (Occa to Chilan, the Enigma Berry, Micle to Rowap; the last 2 plots bare). Platinum's trees are Platinum's own sprites (its growing, blooming and fruit trees' 2 frames each, its sprout's, and the soil alone for planted), their icons Platinum's bag icons and their stages Platinum's hours; their yields are a choice (Platinum's data has none to use): 2 or 3 berries for the 17 that weaken a type's attack, 1 or 2 for the rarer 5. Each tree grows in real time through the game's stages (planted, sprouted, taller, flowering, berries), drawn with its own tree sprite in each stage's palette, and is ripe `GROW_HOURS` (4 hours, a placeholder: "TBD") after it was planted; a new farm has a fifth of its trees ripe and the rest ripening over the hours after. Clicking a ripe tree walks the player (Brendan or May, the Inventory's choice) up to it and picks it: between the berry's least and most yield (its `minYield`–`maxYield`, at random: there's no watering) go to the account's Berry Pouch, and the tree is planted again. A ripe tree stays ripe until it's picked. **Gather all ripe** (above the map, with how many are ripe) picks every ripe tree at once, the player staying put, each tree's berries rising from it (`harvestAll`, `extras.harvestAll`). The server's clock decides ripeness; picking runs with the account's row locked. Platinum's berries go in the Berry Blender too (their own flavours) and Poffin cooking.

**Berry Blender** (`/extras/berry-blender`): Emerald's minigame, as played at the Lilycove Contest Hall with 3 people (MISS, LADDIE and LASSIE), ported frame by frame from `src/berry_blender.c` (`berry-blender.ts`): the player picks a berry; the 4 berries are thrown in (the people's are the game's, from Cheri, Chesto, Pecha, Rawst and Aspear, never the player's); the centre drops in, the 3-2-1 countdown, START; then the arrow spins and a click, a tap, Space, Enter or Z/A presses as it passes the player's arrow (top left): a Best (8 of the window's 48 steps, right on it) or a Good speeds it up and fills the progress bar, a Miss slows it. The people press at the game's odds by the blender's speed (MISS, as in the game, only ever makes a Good below 500, a bug it shipped with). When the bar is full it winds down; then the ranking (Bests, then Goods, then fewest Misses), the results (berries, top speed in RPM, time) and the Pokéblock made: the API uses the berry up and makes it with the game's formula from the 4 berries and the top speed (`makePokeblock`: colour, flavours, level and feel), and keeps the record. Auto play plays Cycling (Ruby & Sapphire's, or Omega Ruby & Alpha Sapphire's) from the game's start through the results, as the game does; it starts the music if it wasn't playing (the cue's `start`) and stops it again for the Pokéblock's fanfare, unless it was playing already. With auto play off, the music player is left alone. Its sounds are the game's (`blenderSound.ts`): every sound effect `berry_blender.c` plays (the berries bouncing in, the centre falling and landing, the countdown, START, Best, Good and Miss, the menu's select) and the Pokéblock's fanfare, rendered from the decompilation's MIDI files and instruments by `tools/extras/m4a_render.py` (a small rendition of the game's sound engine; close, not bit-exact) into `public/extras/blender/sounds/`, each on its music player with its priority as the game's; the blender's whirring loops while it spins, its pitch rising with the speed (`UpdateHitPitch`) and falling as it winds down. A volume slider under the screen (its speaker silencing them and back) sets their volume, remembered in the browser (`poke-tcg.blender-volume`); the music's volume is the music player's. Above 1500 speed the music speeds up with the blender, its pitch kept, as the game's does (`blenderTempo`: ((speed − 750) / 20 + 256) / 256 times its own tempo, set only on a Miss or a score of LADDIE's, the game's bug kept; the music player's `useMusicTempo`, whatever song plays), and is back to its own when the blender stops or the screen closes. Pokéblocks aren't used anywhere else yet.

**Poffin Cooking** (`/extras/poffin-cooking`): Pokémon Platinum's, as cooked alone at the Poffin House, ported frame by frame from the [pret/pokeplatinum](https://github.com/pret/pokeplatinum) decompilation (`src/overlay083/`, `poffin.c`; `poffin-cooking.ts`): the player picks a berry from the Berry Pouch; it drops into the pot ("The Cheri Berry went in!"), the stylus hint blinks, 1, 2, 3, START; then they drag round the pot on the bottom screen (the pointer is the stylus) the way the arrow points, the batter speeding up with each stroke (as the game measures it round the pot's centre, half as much near the middle, nothing outside the pot) and slowing a little each frame, turning and swelling up to 1.25× with its speed. 3 phases, each over after 16 laps the arrow's way or 20 seconds, the batter cooking from its first look to its last (each fading into the next over the phase's last 2 seconds or 5 laps) over a fiercer flame; the arrow's way is drawn again every 5, 4 then 3 seconds or so (a change likelier the longer it stays), its countdown waiting while the batter goes the wrong way, and a reminder blinks while it does. Stirring flat out for a second spills it ("Too fast! Overflow!", not in the last phase); too slowly for 3 seconds burns it (the first time only a warning, "It'll burn! Stir it!"), each with the game's puffs, and a burn leaves a scorch mark riding on the batter until it's stirred fast again. Then DONE!, the results (the time, spills and burns) and the Poffin, which the API makes and keeps (`makePoffin`, the game's formula): the berry's Platinum flavours, each less the next, times 1800 / the frames it took (quicker is stronger), less a point for each spill and burn; its smoothness the berry's less 1 (at least 15); its type and level as the game's (Spicy, Spicy-Dry … Rich, Overripe, Foul, Mild). Everything drawn is Platinum's graphics (the pot scene, the screens, the sprites and their animations from the cell and animation data, the berries' pictures, and the Poffins' sprites from the prebuilt `poruact.narc`) and its sounds are the game's, rendered from its sequences, banks and wave archives by `tools/extras/nds_render.py`; the music is Platinum's Poffins (auto play, started if it wasn't playing). Platinum's berries differ from Emerald's for half the farm's (its own flavours and smoothness are used: `berry-data-gen4.ts`); Platinum's own 22 berries grow in the farm's lower garden. The text uses the site's Emerald font, not the DS's. The game's multiplayer cooking (sparkles when stirring together) isn't there: it's single-player.

**Game Corner** (`/extras/game-corner`, `GameCorner.tsx`): Mauville's Game Corner as Emerald has it (`game-corner.ts`). The account has a Coin Case of up to 9999 coins (`User.coins`; no Coin Case item is needed). The counter sells them for banked ₱ at the game's prices, 50 coins for ₱1,000 or 500 for ₱10,000, unless the Coin Case would overflow or the bank is short (`extras.buyCoins`, with the account's row locked). Coins buy nothing yet: the prize corner (its dolls and TMs) isn't there, as Pokéblocks aren't used. The page is the game's room, Mauville's Game Corner as Emerald draws it (`game_corner_map.py`), across the page's width, its door back to the Extras menu, its people where the game has them: clicking a slot machine walks the player (Brendan or May) to its seat and sits them at it, clicking a roulette table walks them to its nearest side; the coin clerk (talked to across the counter) says the game's lines and offers its menu (50 COINS ₱1,000, 500 COINS ₱10,000, EXIT), the money and coins boxes shown beside her, and answers as the game's does; the prize corner's clerks only welcome the player; everyone else says their first lines from the game's scripts (as for a player with a Coin Case, who's had the doll and the 20 coins: none are given here), page by page in the Battle Tower's text box (`DialogueBox`). With no coins a machine says the Game Corner's "COINS are needed to play here"; the roulette's own question says the same. Each machine has a luck (Unluckiest to Luckiest, never shown, as in the game) that changes every day: the game's GetSlotMachineId, with the day's number standing in for Dewford's trend, so every account's machines are the same on the same day. The games are played in the browser, which keeps its own count as the game does; each ball or spin over is settled by the API in the order played (`extras.coinRound`: its bet taken and its payout added, the bet one a table or machine takes and the payout one it can pay, checked by `coinRoundError`, the Coin Case kept to 9999). Auto play plays Ruby & Sapphire's Game Corner (MUS_GAME_CORNER) on the page and at the games, in either soundtrack (Omega Ruby & Alpha Sapphire has none), only switching music already playing; the fanfares pause it and play it on where it was (the music player's `useMusicHold`, as the GBA's PlayFanfare does). PokéNews's service day (the tables' doubled rates, every machine at least Lucky) isn't there.

**Roulette** (the Game Corner's 2 tables): Emerald's Mauville Game Corner roulette, ported frame by frame from `src/roulette.c` (`roulette.ts`). At the table the game asks "The minimum wager at this table is 1 (or 3). Do you want to play?". Yes fades into the board: the wheel on the left, red felt at the left table and green at the right, and the grid of bets on the right. The player moves the selection over the grid with the arrows, or clicks a square, and presses A. They can bet on a Pokémon of a colour (a square, ×12), on a Pokémon (a column, ×4, ×6, then ×12 as more of its squares are hit) or on a colour (a row, ×3, ×4, ×6, then ×12). The selection's slots flash on the wheel. The grid slides off and the ball is thrown in at one of 4 angles, chosen by the local hour. It rolls fast, then medium, then slow, using the game's single-precision float maths, travelling the table's base distance plus a random amount (`GetRandomForBallTravelDistance`, also by the hour and the ball count). It then drops into the slot under it as it passes a slot's middle. A slot already hit in this round of 6 balls makes the ball bounce to its neighbour, left or right at random. If that one is taken too, Shroomish (it falls in and shakes the screen, sending the ball flying) or Taillow (it flies in, carries the ball round and drops it) clears it into a free slot a few along; which one comes depends on where the ball stopped. The grid slides back, the winning square blinks under its ball, and the game says "It's a hit!" with the win fanfare, "Jackpot!" with the jackpot fanfare for ×12, or "Nothing doing!". Winnings are counted up a coin at a time, stopping at 9999. After 6 balls the board is cleared, and "Keep playing?" asks after every ball. The bugs are kept: the ball's resting frame depends on the frame its rolling animation had reached; a landed ball gets the screen shake added twice; the multiplier stops short of the edge as the grid slides off; and if Shroomish leaves before the ball lands, the ball counts as a miss. The board is drawn as the GBA draws it (`rouletteDraw.ts`): an affine wheel, a sliding grid, sprites by priority, Shroomish's see-through shadow and the palette flashes; the windows and text are drawn as the blender's (the site's Emerald font), and the Game Corner itself isn't drawn behind the question. The sounds are the game's, rendered by `tools/extras/m4a_render.py` (`rouletteSound.ts`): each on its music player with its priority, the two fanfares pausing the music for their length, Taillow's wing flaps looping, and the Shroomish and Taillow cries from their samples. The player's party (a Shroomish or Taillow in it makes the throws more alike) isn't modelled.

**Slot machines** (the Game Corner's 12): Emerald's Mauville machines, ported frame by frame from `src/slot_machine.c` (`slot-machine.ts`), each playing with that day's luck (its machine id). Down (or Bet 1) puts in a coin, up to 3, each lighting more lines: the middle row, then the top and bottom rows, then the diagonals. R (Max bet) puts in all 3, or says "You don't have three COINS." A (Z, Enter, Space, a click or a tap) spins and stops each reel in turn. SELECT (Shift) shows the Reel Time help, B (X, Backspace) asks "Quit the game?" (giving back the bet), and START (S) pays a win out at once; holding A pays it out faster. Each spin the machine draws a bias at the game's odds for its luck and bet: Replay, Cherry, Lotad, Azurill, Power, Reel Time, and Reg Bonus or straight 7s, which stay until 7s are matched. It then steers each reel up to 4 symbols past where the player stopped it to line the bias's symbol up, or to make a loss, taunting a missed 7s bias with the other colour's 7. A line pays the game's amounts: a cherry on the left reel 2 (4 on the top or bottom row, nothing on a diagonal), Power 3, Lotad 6, Azurill 12, Reg Bonus 90, 7s 300. A Replay is a free spin (settled with a bet of 0). A blue 7 makes the game lucky and a red one normal. Three Power bolts add to the Pika Power meter. A Reel Time draw opens the Pikachu machine's lottery: its spins (more likely with more bolts, which it spends) stop the reels exactly where the player presses, at half or quarter speed more often the more coins were lost; with none, the machine may explode. The game's bugs are kept: the Reel Time draw makes 0 spins far likelier than its tables intend; the lottery lands on the first check, comparing the spins left (still 0); with 3 coins, the third reel's losing stop can still leave a row matching; the 3-coin taunt misses one case; a Replay's spin counts its bet as coins lost. The screen is drawn pixel by pixel as the GBA does, from Emerald's own tiles, tilemaps, sprites and palettes (`tools/extras/emerald_slots.py`), the text in the site's Emerald font.
